## Overall Status
**(2026-08-19, DEC-0116) The Mainnet frontend is now LIVE at strategic-super-reserve.fun** (deployment `dpl_DqRggFv8SwF5NrmCFtfh5dyiVZ5R`), pointed at the Mainnet program via `VITE_SOLANA_CLUSTER=mainnet-beta`. Buy/Sell now uses a new swap-free direct-USDC path (no DevNet-only swap authority involved); a real bug was caught and fixed where the program ID was hardcoded to DevNet in 15 places across 7 files, meaning the app would NOT have actually used Mainnet even with the right env vars set. Non-visual verification only (deployed bundle contains the correct program ID, no leaked RPC key) -- full interactive browser verification is Creator's next step, same as every prior DevNet UI pass in this project. Reserve *creation* on Mainnet remains explicitly out of scope/deferred. See DEC-0116.

**(2026-08-19, DEC-0115) SSR Protocol is now LIVE ON SOLANA MAINNET.** Program `8hTW7fHwn8t8hcgTVeyAhHMiCTHGUP3783NWUTBBFwH9`, deployed and initialized with the DEC-0112 authority structure (Creator + Boss as independent Protocol Admins, Squads Treasury vault as protocol-fee destination), canonical IDL published on-chain, and Phase 6 minimal smoke testing complete (create/seed/mint/redeem, pause/unpause, unauthorized-rejection, fee routing, custody -- all verified with real signatures against a real USDC-seeded test Reserve). Two real bugs were found and fixed live during smoke testing, most notably an off-curve protocol-fee-destination bug in the shared SDK that would have broken every future mint/seed call. **Production frontend deployment (Phase 7) has not happened yet** -- `strategic-super-reserve.fun` still points at DevNet; that is the next step before Creator can manually verify Mainnet in a browser. See DEC-0112 through DEC-0115 for the full record.

On Track (pre-existing DevNet/public-launch summary below, unchanged by the above). Two parallel tracks are both live: **(1) the DevNet/beta engineering track** -- SSR Protocol has been live on Solana DevNet since 2026-07-28, the full 8-phase A-H delivery plan is complete, and ten rounds of corrective/hardening passes (most recently 2026-08-05's legacy-Reserve quarantine + canonical-Reserve-set pass) have progressively closed reported bugs -- is deployed to the closed testing site `strategic-super-reserve.fun`. **(2) The public-launch track** -- the real product domain, `ssr.fun`, went fully live 2026-08-04 (DEC-0073) on its own separate Vercel project with a Coming Soon page, ahead of the public launch itself. The two tracks are deliberately isolated: different Vercel projects, different domains, no shared deploy history.

## Current Phase
**The full 8-phase A-H DevNet delivery plan is complete** (see `docs/project/DEVNET_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_2026-07-29.md`): Phases A-D and F-G are implemented, deployed, and live-DevNet-verified (DEC-0041 through DEC-0044, DEC-0046, DEC-0048, DEC-0049, DEC-0050); Phase E (Jupiter DevNet swap feasibility) was proven infeasible and the resulting product decision was made (DEC-0045, resolved by DEC-0051). **Terminology reversed 2026-08-04 (DEC-0074):** "Reserve" now means the whole basket/product, "reserve assets" its holdings -- see CLAUDE.md's Mandatory Terminology section, which supersedes the 2026-07-29/DEC-0072 wording. **On-chain delegate management is now genuinely wired end to end (2026-08-04, DEC-0075)**: add/update/remove-delegate all submit real signed transactions, gated by a real on-chain permission check, live-verified with real signatures against a genuinely non-root delegate for the first time. **The `ssr_devnet_amm` swap mechanism has been reworked (WSOL hub -> devUSDC hub) and merged into this repo's working tree (2026-08-04, DEC-0076), and a new on-chain rebalance-execution instruction (`execute_rebalance_leg`) has been designed and added to `ssr_protocol` (DEC-0077) -- both are real source changes but NEITHER IS BUILT, DEPLOYED, OR VERIFIED.** **Toolchain claim corrected (2026-08-05, DEC-0081):** cargo/rustc/anchor/solana ARE installed on this machine (`~/.cargo/bin`, `/c/devtools/solana/solana-release/bin`) -- prior sessions' "no toolchain on PATH" reports were accurate for their own environment/PATH state at the time, not a permanent gap; this session confirmed all four work directly (including a clean `cargo check -p ssr_devnet_amm`) once added to PATH. Building/deploying/seeding `ssr_devnet_amm` and wiring Buy/Sell through it remains explicitly deferred -- not attempted this pass, by deliberate scope decision (see DEC-0081) -- so it is now correctly described as "technically unblocked, still not done," not "blocked." Buy/Sell for all Reserves, including the 3 new canonical ones (DEC-0081), still use the existing genuine fixed-price devUSDC-settled path -- Buy still mints non-devUSDC legs and Sell still mints devUSDC, both from a server-held authority rather than genuine AMM liquidity (DEC-0054, still open). Direct proportional redemption remains the canonical, only fully-genuine Sell path in production today. **The Rebalance tab's `add_reserve_asset_active` custom-program-error root cause is now fixed and its whole real-Reserve editing flow redesigned to a wallet-free local-preview-then-single-submit model (2026-08-12, DEC-0084)** -- see Last 5 Working Days below; committed, pushed, and deployed live to `strategic-super-reserve.fun` (commit `e90efd1`, `dpl_9KEt7maJoTBnKfYCM8vncCUKmNiR`).

## Current Objective
(1) build, deploy, and initialize the reworked `ssr_devnet_amm` (DEC-0076), then rewrite `zapInstructions.ts`'s Buy/Sell to route through it instead of server-minting (closing DEC-0054 for real) -- now genuinely unblocked (toolchain confirmed working, DEC-0081) but still not started; (2) build, deploy, and wire the frontend/SDK orchestration for `execute_rebalance_leg` (DEC-0077); (3) perform a real browser + Phantom click-through against the DevNet/beta site (no browser-automation tool available in this environment -- everything below the UI layer that CAN be verified without a browser has been, but the literal click path has never been human/browser-tested); (4) push the `ssr.fun` Coming Soon page's source to a real Git remote (currently a local-only repo -- no GitHub CLI/token available in this environment, see DEC-0073) and build/deploy the real (non-placeholder) `ssr.fun` product experience when the team is ready for public launch.

## Executive Summary
**SSR Protocol went from "not started" to "deployed live on Solana DevNet, fully tested against that deployment, and wired into the real frontend with working Buy/Sell and Create Reserve flows" across the initial build session.** Architecture was locked, the 17-instruction Anchor program and a TypeScript SDK were hand-written, a complete native toolchain was installed piece by piece, and the program was deployed and verified live at `2dURvmSdHeyaFES5rxaE1zgPSHCBLW5BLNguJ2Tu1mkW`. Its full test suite passes against that live deployment (14/14), and persistent Gate-9 fixture Reserves exist on-chain (`docs/protocol/DEVNET_FIXTURES.md`). Gate 10-11 then wired the existing SSR.fun frontend to all of this while preserving its design exactly (DEC-0027), and the subsequent Phase A-H DevNet delivery plan (2026-07-29 through 2026-08-03) took it from "wired up" to a fully on-chain testing environment: canonical on-chain Reserve discovery, a real devUSDC settlement token with faucet/onboarding, Phase F/G composition-management and wind-down instructions, and the program itself now sits at 23 instructions, all live-verified with real signed transactions.

**Current state (as of 2026-08-04): the protocol and program are stable and the last eight consecutive corrective passes (2026-07-29 through 2026-08-04) have each traced a specific, real, live-reported bug to its root cause and fixed it -- there is no known open bug in the DevNet/beta flow right now.** The most recent (DEC-0072, 2026-08-04) made Reserve deployment resumable after a partial failure: a reported "Custom 6400" error during seed-funding turned out not to correspond to any real program error at all (confirmed against both the Rust source and the deployed IDL), and the actual root cause -- no step-level resume path existed, so a failure after Reserve creation funneled the user into creating a duplicate Reserve -- is now fixed with real on-chain-state-driven resumption, idempotent seed-funding, and 24 new regression tests (188/188 offline tests passing). Immediately prior passes fixed: tradable-Reserve catalogue gating + genuine multi-asset Buy/Sell + a Price-History chart bug (2026-08-03, DEC-0071); a Buy-crashing signer bug affecting every 100%-devUSDC Reserve, plus AUM/holder/volume accounting fixes (2026-08-03); nine separate live-testing issues in one pass including a stale-closure bug that made duplicate-Reserve creation possible (2026-07-31); a full devUSDC Buy/Sell architecture correction removing all hidden/fabricated asset funding (2026-07-31); and Helius adoption as the primary DevNet RPC provider, fixing 429/"insufficient SOL" failures (2026-07-30). Full detail on every pass is in Last 5 Working Days below, and the dashboard's own Fixes panel (`docs/project/ENGINEERING_TIMELINE.md`).

**Separately, and on a different track from the DevNet engineering work above: the real public product domain, `ssr.fun`, went fully live today** (2026-08-04, DEC-0073) with a Coming Soon page, on its own dedicated Vercel project (`ssr-fun-final`) -- completely isolated from `strategic-super-reserve.fun`'s project, deployment, domain configuration, and repository, which were verified untouched throughout. `ssr.fun` is canonical; `www.ssr.fun` permanently redirects to it; both have valid, real (Let's Encrypt, Vercel-issued) SSL certificates; DNS is live at the registrar (GoDaddy). Remaining for this track: push the Coming Soon page's source to a real Git remote (currently a local-only repo, no GitHub credentials available in this environment) and build the real product experience for public launch.

Remaining across both tracks, none of them blockers: an actual browser/Phantom click-through of the DevNet/beta site (no browser-automation tool available in this environment), enabling Windows Developer Mode so localnet testing (DEC-0034) is fully usable, and building/deploying `ssr_devnet_amm` per DEC-0051 (now unblocked, not yet started).

- **2026-08-27 (Thu), ONE-APPROVAL RESERVE PURCHASES made real (DEC-0171): root cause of the 4-approval ECHO buy found, fixed client-side, and proven by Mainnet simulation -- ECHO already one-approval on live production via its newly-registered trading table; the rest ships with the pending deploy.** Full on-chain reconstruction of the Creator's $10 ECHO purchase (3 sequential Jupiter swaps + separate deposit/mint; 4 signatures, 3,205B, 328,262 CU, ~0.000425 SOL fees) established the root cause: NO Reserve had a DEC-0161 trading lookup table registered (registry alt:null for ECHO and CHARLI), so the DEC-0156 composed one-transaction path overran Solana's 1232-byte limit and fell back sequentially on EVERY trade. Fixed: ECHO's table created on Mainnet + registered (tx 4QnPbCS4..., table 2nNx3zSk..., 24 addresses, PDAs cross-checked against the real mint tx first); Reserve creation now auto-creates+registers the table (optional, decline-safe final step; Review copy updated); the buy path auto-heals table-less Reserves (pure fit-prediction first so table rent is never wasted on many-leg Reserves that can't fit regardless, then create+register+retry); table extension chunked so the 12-asset table (51 addresses) is buildable; and REAL on-chain minimum-output protection wired (min_reserve_tokens_out was hardcoded 1 -- disabled -- despite the deployed handler enforcing it). PROVEN read-only against live Mainnet with the real table: the exact ECHO purchase composes to 990 bytes, ONE signature, err:null, 312,153 CU, simulated net -10.200018 USDC / +9.891228 ECHO -- the mandated truthful Phantom preview. Measured architecture limits: one-transaction fits to ~3 swap legs; 5- and 10-asset compositions physically cannot fit (measured overruns) -- the >4-asset one-approval design is a Tier-2 intent-escrow + permissionless-keeper program upgrade, deliberately deferred with its analysis recorded. 948/948 offline tests (8 new); tsc -b, oxlint, npm run build clean. - **2026-08-27 (Thu), Reserve profile pictures: Create-flow Step 1 picker + signature-free picture edits (DEC-0168), built and verified -- not yet deployed.** Per Creator instruction: (1) the Create flow's Step 1 (Reserve Identity) now has the same optional profile-picture picker Manage already had -- normalized locally, stored content-addressed, embedded as `imageUrl` in the creation metadata, shown immediately on the new Reserve; (2) changing a picture no longer asks the wallet for anything: the old republish-metadata + one-update_metadata-approval flow is replaced by a mutable server-side pointer (`reserve_image_pointer`, reserve address -> content-addressed image id) upserted by the same image-upload POST and merged over metadata `imageUrl` by RealReserveSync each discovery pass (pointer wins; stored images stay immutable/cacheable; on-chain metadata untouched by edits). The audit had shown NO client-side cap existed (the on-chain rejection of a 13th asset would have stranded a creator mid-registration); CreateDTR now refuses a 13th asset before anything is submitted. - **2026-08-27 (Thu), DELTA seed compute-exhaustion root-caused via first-principles audit and fixed: explicit compute-unit limits on seed transactions (DEC-0166), deployed to production.** After DEC-0165 made the 10-asset DELTA seed LAND for the first time, it failed on-chain with InstructionError [1, ProgramFailedToComplete] -- decoded to its real cause: "exceeded CUs meter", consumed 202,850 of 202,850 (the runtime's ~200k default; no setComputeUnitLimit was ever requested). PROVEN, not inferred: the byte-identical transaction re-simulated read-only with only a raised limit SUCCEEDS (err:null, 219,754 CU, full ReserveSeeded event). Measured model CU(n) ~= 53k + 16.7k*n (CHARLI 4-asset seed: 119,408; DELTA 10-asset: 219,754): under the default budget <=8 assets fit on thin margin, 9 is knife-edge, 10+ always fails -- the binding constraint was never the program, state layout (12-asset validation max), or transaction size (ALT path works; 63 accounts landed). Fix: seedComputeUnitLimit(n) = 100k + 40k*n (~2x margin, <=580k at the program's own 12-asset cap, far under Solana's 1.4M) threaded through both seed paths; ProgramFailedToComplete now decodes to its real compute-budget explanation; resume gained an authoritative Reserve-status re-read BEFORE funding (closing the stale-read window behind the observed SeedAmountTooLow-after-successful-seed false-failure class). DELTA's authoritative state audited per-asset: reserve AssetsInitializing, RT supply 0, all 10 vaults 0, wallet holds >= required raw amount of ALL 10 assets, all classic SPL Token -- the failed seed rolled back atomically, funding verification was genuine, and $2/asset economics are proven viable. Corrected construction simulation-verified at the exact production limit: consumed 219,454 of 499,700, success. 935/935 offline tests (8 new); tsc/oxlint/build clean. Production deployment `dpl_AQMrhqK74SzLkzk9Ji3iCzqiRDv3` (Ready, site 200). OUTCOME (same day): Creator resumed and DELTA SEEDED SUCCESSFULLY -- signature 4ymuvjwarF27NxmT6LtB1BVJw4QJe4Cr9LcaG4ZHA93jL4KbsAcRYcb51WFavYGJqQVzAvFb48mPKPgHTebGiHJT (14:23 UTC), consumed 219,454 of 499,700 CU (matching the pre-fix simulation to the exact unit), RT supply 19.9 minted, Reserve #16 Active and live at strategic-super-reserve.fun/#/dtr/mainnet-beta-16. This is the protocol's FIRST 10-asset Mainnet inception -- the full USDC-only pipeline (10 Jupiter conversions, balance verification, ALT-compressed atomic seed) is now Mainnet-proven end-to-end. See DEC-0166.
- **2026-08-27 (Thu), DELTA-launch silent-drop incident root-caused and fixed: priority fees + same-signature rebroadcast on every app-built transaction (DEC-0165), deployed to production.** Creator's 10-asset DELTA launch failed at Step 2/2 (Seeding) with an ambiguous-confirmation error, and Resume failed again. On-chain reconstruction proved: create+register, all 8 Jupiter funding swaps, and the lookup-table create+extends ALL finalized OK -- only the final versioned seed_reserve was silently dropped (no ledger record), and the Resume attempt's transactions all dropped too (balance comparison proved the wallet paid ZERO for it; the "0.004 SOL" seen was the popup's rent estimate for transactions that never landed). Root cause, evidenced not guessed: app-built transactions carried NO priority fee (the landed create tx paid exactly 5000 lamports base) and were submitted exactly once (maxRetries:0, skipPreflight, no rebroadcast). Fix: every app-built transaction (createReserveClient + managementClient, legacy and versioned/ALT paths) now bids a clamped p75 of the RPC's recent prioritization fees and re-sends the SAME signed bytes every 3s until a definitive outcome (signature-idempotent, never double-executes); poll window widened to outlive the blockhash so drops resolve as honest "expired". getRecentPrioritizationFees added to both rpc-proxy allowlists (the self-auditing allowlist test caught its absence). Production deployment `dpl_61Za2yjjkJDDjf7pBQw2XP4pJ4En` (Ready, site 200). 927/927 offline tests; tsc/oxlint/build clean. DELTA is created/registered/funded and safely resumable -- nothing was lost. See DEC-0165.
- **2026-08-27 (Thu), Jupiter per-key rate-limit hardening (DEC-0162), deployed to production from a clean hotfix worktree.** Creator's 10-asset DELTA launch hit "Jupiter's API answered with too many requests for this key" AGAIN during Resume's fund-seed-assets step (second live occurrence after 2026-08-25's server-side retry fix) -- declared a major launch blocker. Root cause was structural, not congestion: every Resume click re-fetched a target quote for EVERY swap-eligible asset back-to-back (~10 unspaced quotes), and neither the client nor the server ever waited long enough to EXIT the per-key rate window. Shipped: (1) Resume no longer re-quotes assets already verified ready_to_seed -- the persisted target is reused and the freshly-read wallet balance still gates readiness (full-quote cache TTL 20s->60s); (2) every jupiter-swap proxy call (launch, Buy, Sell) is paced >=1.5s via a shared reservation gate; (3) the client retries a 429 up to twice honoring Retry-After or waiting 10s/20s; (4) server: 4 quote/build attempts with escalating 429 backoff (2s/4s/6s), Retry-After on every 429 (10s Jupiter-key / 15s per-IP), per-IP window 12->20 per 60s, and maxDuration: 60 on api/mainnet functions (vercel.json; Vercel rejected a specific-file pattern overlapping the existing glob, so the glob carries it). If limits are still hit at scale, the recorded money lever is upgrading the JUPITER_API_KEY plan tier on portal.jup.ag -- deliberately not taken while the code fix suffices. 897/897 offline tests (3 updated to the 4-attempt bound), npm run build clean, both verified in the clean worktree actually deployed (branch jupiter-429-hotfix; the main working tree carried the unrelated in-progress reserve-profile-image feature that must not ship early -- the same fix also remains in the main working tree so the next regular deploy retains it). LIVE: production deployment `dpl_ADoXTUJz4vSUaTjrXWkpnb2Cn1KQ` (READY, target production, 2026-08-27). See DEC-0162.

- **2026-08-25 (Tue), DEC-0151 follow-up: Jupiter per-key 429 rate-limit handling in api/mainnet/jupiter-swap.ts (DEC-0152).** Creator topped up USDC and hit "swap-quote service had a network error" on Resume -- correctly framed as retryable now, but a direct manual reproduction of the identical USD1 quote against production succeeded in 0.4s moments later, pointing at the burst shape of a many-asset Resume tripping Jupiter's PER-KEY rate limit: its 429 body is `{"message":...}` (no `.error` field, so the specific-answer branch never matches), and the retry loop's 400/800ms backoff lands straight back inside the same rate window. Both the quote and swap-build retry loops now honor a 429 specially: wait `Retry-After` (capped 5s) or a full 2s, never treat a 429 body's text as a stable answer, and -- if still exhausted -- return a real HTTP 429 with "too many ... requests" wording the client already classifies retryable, instead of the misleading "network error" text. 831/831 offline tests passing (3 new, incl. a real Retry-After-honoring timing assertion), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` clean, deployed to production. See DEC-0152.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), first-principles correction of Mainnet Reserve inception and purchasing (DEC-0151) -- root cause of the whole DEC-0144..0150 failure chain finally evidenced: the wallet ran out of USDC and nothing anywhere checked.** Creator escalated ("stop patching individual error messages"). Evidence-first reconstruction: Reserve 11 (`EQ5HNT9h...`, 10 assets, created 12:33:57) failed its 4th swap (USDCâ†’USD1) FIVE consecutive times over 34 minutes with Jupiter error **6024 = InsufficientFunds per Jupiter's own docs** (not slippage -- the aggregator failed at entry after 1,884 CUs), against a wallet holding **0.490579 USDC** (visible in the failed tx's own preTokenBalances) -- earlier abandoned launches had converted the USDC into other constituent tokens (9 of this wallet's 11 Reserves are stuck AssetsInitializing). The reported "ETH" asset never existed: mint-level identity shows it was **ZEC** (A7bdiYdS5G..., Zcash) / **HYPE** (98sMhvDwXj...) -- all 10 of Reserve 11's mints verified classic-token-program with healthy Jupiter routes, ruling out Token-2022/liquidity causes. Built the coherent fix: binding funding invariant documented in FRONTEND_INTEGRATION.md (user supplies ONLY USDC; launch = USDCâ†’swapsâ†’seedâ†’mint, purchase = USDCâ†’swapsâ†’depositâ†’mint, resume counts held balances but never repeats work); explicit persistent per-asset state machine (`launchFunding.ts`, `not_startedâ†’...â†’ready_to_seed`, persisted in PendingReserveDeploy) with seeding hard-gated on every asset verified; upfront feasibility preflight BEFORE PDA creation (precise per-composition minimum + exact USDC shortfall -- would have blocked the entire incident at click one); fresh execution quote per swap (found+fixed: the 20s-cached quote was being EXECUTED for unheld assets); per-swap USDC preflight showing held-vs-required (Jupiter's documented 6024 handling); signature reconciliation before any re-swap; "N of M assets funded" progress; 6024/6001 decoded to their documented meanings (partially supersedes DEC-0150); and **ALPHA (100% SSR) now buys with USDC** -- every non-pure-USDC Mainnet Reserve routes through the proven USDC-funded path. No program change needed. **$20/10-asset verdict: viable but zero headroom** ($2/asset clears the $0.50/swap practical floor; wallet must hold ~$18.54 USDC for a fresh $20 launch + SOL for fees/rent; computed minimum seed for 10x10%: $5.00). 828/828 offline tests (27 new/updated incl. the literal 0.49-USDC case), tsc/oxlint/build clean, deployed to production. Reserve 11/DELTA untouched per instruction. See DEC-0151.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), added diagnostic logging to jupiter-swap.ts (DEC-0149), then fixed a THIRD distinct call site where a genuinely retryable error was told to the Creator as deterministic: a real on-chain Jupiter-route rejection (error 6024) whose own text says "Try again" (DEC-0150).** Creator moved to resuming CHARLIE directly (having locally un-tracked DELTA, which itself was never touched) and hit "The Jupiter swap was rejected on-chain by one of the programs in its route (error code 6024) ... Try again: a fresh quote is fetched automatically on retry." immediately followed by the UI's own contradictory "this specific failure will not resolve itself on a plain retry." `classifyCreateReserveError` had no rule recognizing describeJupiterSwapError's own message shape (its plain-English "error code N" phrasing doesn't match extractCustomErrorCode's patterns either), so it fell through to the conservative default. Added a check for describeJupiterSwapError's stable message marker, classified as retryable, checked before extractCustomErrorCode so a real Custom(N) code in the message can never cause a misread. 805/805 offline tests passing (4 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Also separately diagnosed (not yet fixed) two real ALPHA pricing bugs the Creator flagged: `change24h`/`change7d` are hardcoded to 0 at construction (never actually computed), and `avgPurchasePrice` is overwritten to the CURRENT price on every background sync (RealReserveSync/DTRDetail), so Unrealized P&L structurally reads ~$0 regardless of real cost basis -- deferred at Creator's direction to finish the Resume chain first. See DEC-0149, DEC-0150.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), fixed the same generic-fallback-hides-a-transient-failure bug on the OTHER Jupiter call this app makes (building the swap transaction, not just quoting it), closing this specific bug class; also fixed a real test-isolation gap (DEC-0148).** Immediately after DEC-0147 deployed, Creator's next Resume attempt on DELTA hit "Failed to build the Jupiter swap transaction." -- the identical shape DEC-0146 already fixed for the quote call, just on the swap-transaction-build call right after it. - **2026-08-25 (Tue), fixed two more issues on DELTA immediately after DEC-0146 shipped: a real held balance still triggering a swap prompt (market-price drift on the re-quoted target), and the recurring rate limit's own root cause (DEC-0147).** Creator: "still prompted me to buy tokens althought i have in wallet. and then hit the too many swap requests error." Fixed the prompt issue with `isWithinAcceptableShortfallTolerance`: skip a swap once the existing balance is within 5% (the same tolerance already used to decide whether a post-swap shortfall is worth warning about) of the freshly re-quoted target, not just an absolute few-cents dust gap -- a live-quoted target moves with real market price between Resume attempts, so a genuinely adequate balance can look "short" again purely from price drift. Fixed the rate limit's actual driver: `fundSeedAssetsIdempotent` was refetching a full Jupiter quote for EVERY swap-eligible asset on EVERY Resume click just to determine the comparison target -- for DELTA's 9 swap-eligible assets, that's 9 real requests per click against this app's own 12-requests/60s-per-IP proxy limit, before any actual swap logic runs. Cached that specific call for 20 seconds (safe: it's never the quote an actual swap transaction is built from). 798/798 offline tests passing (4 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0147.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), fixed a third Resume/funding bug on DELTA immediately after DEC-0145 shipped: a "no Jupiter swap route" message that was actually a transient upstream hiccup, not a real lack of liquidity (DEC-0146).** Independently tested every one of DELTA's 10 registered assets directly against Jupiter's own public quote API at several sizes ($1-$500) -- all 9 swap-eligible assets had real, healthy, low-impact routes the whole time, proving the failure was in `api/mainnet/jupiter-swap.ts`'s own upstream call, not a genuine no-route condition. Root cause: that endpoint's generic fallback message fires for ANY non-OK response with an unparseable body, not specifically a real "no route" answer (confirmed Jupiter's genuine shape for that case IS parseable JSON with a specific reason, e.g. `TOKEN_NOT_TRADABLE`) -- and a thrown `fetch()` hit the same generic-sounding path. Added bounded retry (3 attempts, short backoff) around exactly those two transient shapes, leaving a genuine parseable Jupiter answer reported immediately and unretried; the final give-up message now says "network error" so it's classified retryable client-side too, consistent with DEC-0144/0145. 794/794 offline tests passing (3 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Neither CHARLIE nor DELTA touched -- server-side fix only. See DEC-0146.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), fixed two more Resume/funding bugs surfaced while deploying a new Reserve ("DELTA"), and surfaced a materially bigger finding: 8 real Mainnet Reserves (not just CHARLIE) are currently stuck AssetsInitializing and unclosable (DEC-0145).** Creator reported Delta's Resume asking to fund tiny "~$0.02 of X token" top-ups and asked for a pragmatic fix: if the wallet already holds the asset, just push through. Root cause #1: a dust-sized remaining funding deficit could scale down to a Jupiter swap-quote amount so small Jupiter itself rejects it ("Cannot compute other amount threshold, with amount 1 and slippageBps 150") -- `scaleUsdcBudgetForDeficit`'s existing "never 0" floor of 1 raw unit was itself still too small. Fixed with a new `isDustDeficit` check: below a fixed $0.05 floor, and only when the wallet already holds SOME real balance for that asset, skip the swap and seed with what's already held, surfaced through the existing `onSwapShortfall` toast (no new UI needed). Root cause #2: a real HTTP 429 from `api/mainnet/jupiter-swap.ts`'s own rate limiter ("Too many swap requests...") doesn't contain the exact phrase `isRateLimitError` checked for ("too many requests"), so it fell through to the same false "deterministic" alarm DEC-0144 fixed for the ambiguous-confirmation case -- broadened the check to match both words present, not only the exact phrase. While diagnosing, a direct read-only on-chain enumeration of every Reserve this manager wallet owns (not just CHARLIE) found **10 real Reserves total, 8 stuck at AssetsInitializing** (reserve_ids 1-6 from 2026-08-20, CHARLIE/9, and DELTA/10 created today) -- flagged directly to the Creator, with an explicit request to stop starting new deploys until reviewed; reserve_ids 1-6 are a separate, not-yet-actioned follow-up. 791/791 offline tests passing (9 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Neither CHARLIE nor DELTA was touched -- client-side fix only. See DEC-0145.
- **2026-08-25 (Tue), fixed Resume's "ambiguous" RPC-confirmation timeout being misclassified as a deterministic failure, discouraging the exact plain retry that would have safely resolved it (DEC-0144).** Creator retried CHARLIE's Resume again after DEC-0143 shipped and hit (correctly labeled "Mainnet" this time) another RPC-confirmation timeout during Step 2/2 (Seeding Reserve), reported as being stuck in another Resume loop. Directly verified the reported signature (`5ZXVDEFL34cK9gQ69sn4c3n2LWi28wsfqCjw8NqNxY3iiwKXFBEbcFkiUMcu9WZaFv76FSeYoAcLpHqHr25iJ9eC`) against two independent Mainnet RPC sources (this app's own configured Helius endpoint and the public `api.mainnet-beta.solana.com`): both `getSignatureStatuses`/`getTransaction` confirm it never landed on-chain, and CHARLIE's on-chain state (Reserve `6FN24ZMVZw8fAiQhvtdRCAMdLPbieXHjBx4Trw9NnYiQ`, status `assetsInitializing`, assetCount 6) is unchanged from DEC-0142/0143 -- ordinary transient network behavior, nothing lost. Root cause: `createReserveOnChain`/`resumeReserveDeploymentOnChain`'s 6 catch-and-rewrap sites all discarded the original error's identity into a plain message string before it ever reached `classifyCreateReserveError`, so a genuine `AmbiguousConfirmationError` could never match its own `instanceof` check once wrapped -- classification silently fell through to the conservative "deterministic" default, which told the Creator a plain retry "will not resolve itself." Fixed by giving `CreateReserveStepError` an optional `cause` (threaded through all 6 real throw sites) and unwrapping it in `classifyCreateReserveError`; also gave the Resume panel's ambiguous-outcome toast its own non-destructive "Outcome unknown -- verifying" copy instead of sharing "Resume failed"'s alarming styling with a real failure. Confirmed via `close_reserve.rs`/`initiate_wind_down.rs` that CHARLIE (still `assetsInitializing`) has no on-chain path to cancellation/rent-reclaim at all under the currently-deployed program -- completing seeding (via a correctly-labeled Resume) or leaving it tracked as-is are the only two options right now; nothing was closed, cancelled, or resubmitted this pass. 784/784 offline tests passing (5 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Client-side messaging/classification fix only -- CHARLIE itself was not touched. See DEC-0144.

Directly verified the specific reported signature never actually landed on-chain (checked live) and CHARLIE's state is unchanged/safe to retry -- ordinary transient network behavior, not a symptom of DEC-0142's new logic. Added a clusterLabel parameter (defaulting to "DevNet", unaffecting every pre-existing caller) and threaded the real label through all 7 real throw sites across 6 files. 773/773 offline tests passing, `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0143.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), unstuck a real Mainnet Reserve ("CHARLIE", 6 assets) permanently stuck in Resume by adding an Address Lookup Table fallback for seeding (DEC-0142).** `seed_reserve` deposits every asset and mints the initial Reserve Tokens in ONE atomic call, so a Reserve with enough assets (confirmed live: CHARLIE's 6-asset seed transaction was 1432 bytes against Solana's 1232-byte limit) could never fit no matter how many times Resume was retried -- DEC-0136's earlier create-and-register fix couldn't apply here since there's only one instruction and it must stay atomic. Added `signAndSendPossiblyOverLimit`: the existing fast legacy-transaction path is unchanged for the common case, but when an instruction is genuinely too large it now creates and warms up an Address Lookup Table and submits as a v0 VersionedTransaction instead -- the standard Solana mechanism for this, already proven safe in this app via Jupiter's own swap transactions. This pass's own regression test caught a real gap before shipping: `Connection.getSlot` (needed by the new logic) was missing from both Mainnet and DevNet rpc-proxy allowlists, the same silent-rejection bug class DEC-0130 already fixed once for a different method -- fixed both. 771/771 offline tests passing (7 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Not live-verified against CHARLIE's actual stuck deployment (no browser-automation tool, and this session cannot sign on the Creator's behalf) -- Creator's own next Resume click is the real confirmation. See DEC-0142.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), fixed a real fund-safety bug (not just a display bug): every Mainnet caller computing how much real SOL a Create-Reserve creator wraps used the DevNet zap's fixed $20/SOL test peg instead of a real price (DEC-0141).** Creator reported the Review & Deploy Wallet Cost Summary showing a wrong USD estimate ("$2.64" for 0.13177 SOL, i.e. exactly $20.04/SOL) and asked for a quick pass elsewhere. The requested display fix led to a deeper find: `seedRawAmountForAsset` -- which computes the ACTUAL lamport amount wrapped and deposited for a wrapped-SOL leg, not an estimate -- had no cluster check at all, and wrapped SOL is the one seed asset explicitly excluded from the existing Jupiter-swap self-correction loop (funded by direct wrap instead), so its amount stayed pinned to the stale $20 peg all the way to the real on-chain transfer. At a real SOL price far above $20, a creator including SOL in their Reserve was asked to wrap many times more real SOL than intended (worked example: $100 target asks for 5 SOL at $20/SOL vs. ~0.556 SOL at $180/SOL -- a ~9x overcharge). Fixed at the source: `solPriceUsd` is now a required, caller-supplied parameter with no fallback to the wrong constant; CreateDTR.tsx fetches a real live price on mount (same endpoint DEC-0134/0137/0140 already use) and threads it through cost estimation, creation, and resume. Audited every other SOL_TEST_PRICE_USD usage -- confirmed all remaining ones are genuinely DevNet-scoped. 768/768 offline tests passing (4 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0141.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), built the real multi-asset Buy path (DEC-0140), requested ASAP by Creator immediately after DEC-0139's gate shipped.** A multi-asset Mainnet Reserve (e.g. BETA) can now be bought with a single USD amount: it funds a proportional in-kind deposit of every registered asset (wrap the depositor's own SOL for a wrapped-SOL leg, swap the depositor's USDC via real Jupiter routes for every other leg -- mirroring createReserveClient.ts's proven seed-funding pattern exactly, not a new mechanism) and mints Reserve Tokens in one final multi-leg on-chain call (the program already supported this shape -- DevNet's zap has always used it; the gap was purely client-side). Every funding step is idempotent/retry-safe and the final mint is atomic (reverts whole, never partial). Sell/redeem for a multi-asset Reserve remains not-yet-supported, deliberately deferred. 764/764 offline tests passing (5 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. **Not live-tested in a browser** (no browser-automation tool in this environment) -- this is real, untested-in-browser code moving real value via real Jupiter swaps; reviewed carefully for safety properties, but a small test purchase is strongly recommended as its first real-world exercise. See DEC-0140.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), found and gated a real architectural gap: Buy/Sell has no working path for a multi-asset Mainnet Reserve at all (DEC-0139).** The very next report after DEC-0138 deployed ("Buy Failed" on BETA again) traced, via a direct live read of the Mainnet program (not a guess), to BETA genuinely having 4 registered assets -- and the entire direct Buy/Sell mechanism being hard-scoped to single-asset Reserves only (throws before ever building a transaction), with no working multi-asset alternative on Mainnet (DevNet's zap depends on a swap authority with no Mainnet equivalent). Gated Buy/Sell to show an honest "Not Yet Supported" for any multi-asset Mainnet Reserve instead of an always-failing control, added the same gate to Sell (which had none before), and fixed the underlying error text, which had pointed at a nonexistent Mainnet fallback. Building the real multi-asset mechanism (a Jupiter-swap-based zap or N sequential legs) is flagged as a separate, larger feature needing its own design pass, same conclusion as DEC-0138's USDC-buy-currency deferral -- now confirmed to be the same underlying gap. 759/759 offline tests passing (3 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0139.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), fixed Buy always showing "Insufficient SOL Balance" for a SOL-registered Reserve despite a real, sufficient wallet balance (DEC-0138).** Root cause: the balance check read an SPL wrapped-SOL token-account balance (nothing a normal wallet holding native SOL ever funds), and separately, the actual mint transaction never wrapped the deposited SOL at all -- the same gap DEC-0124 already fixed for Create Reserve's seed funding, never ported to the ongoing Buy flow. Fixed both, mirroring createReserveClient.ts's established wrap-SOL pattern. The separately-requested "buy with USDC" capability was investigated but not built -- it reverses an explicit prior scope decision (Mainnet Reserves deliberately single-settlement-asset "for simplicity for now") and needs a real Jupiter-swap leg on live value, which deserves its own design pass rather than a same-pass addition. 756/756 offline tests passing, `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0138.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), fixed Reserve Composition showing $0 for real Mainnet assets outside the DevNet fixture price table, added a Price column, fixed the silently-failing Additional Fee Recipients Add button, and removed the AUM/Prem-Discount tiles (DEC-0137).** Three more road-to-mainnet MCR-01 reports against a real Mainnet Reserve ("BETA"): Fartcoin/STONK/Cupsey all showed $0 Value in Reserve despite real 25% holdings (root cause: the composition table used the DevNet-only `TEST_ASSET_PRICES_USD` fixture table instead of the real Pyth/Jupiter prices AUM already correctly used -- same bug class as DEC-0134, a spot that pass didn't reach); the Additional Fee Recipients "Add" button had 5 silent-failure guards with zero user feedback (most likely trigger: the Primary Fee Destination auto-fills to the connected wallet, so re-adding that same address as an "additional" recipient silently no-ops); and AUM/Prem-Discount were removed per explicit request since Market Cap already shows the same number. 756/756 offline tests passing (7 new), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0137.
- **2026-08-24 (Mon), fixed Create Reserve genuinely failing for ~7+ assets ("Transaction too large: 1830 > 1232", nothing created on-chain), by splitting registration across as many transactions as their real measured size needs instead of always bundling into one (DEC-0136).** Creator reported this live via road-to-mainnet MCR-01 while creating a 10-asset Reserve. Root cause: `createReserveClient.ts` bundled `createReserve` + one `initializeReserveAsset` per asset into a single transaction, a design tradeoff explicitly documented as scoped to "the realistic 1-3 asset case" (DEC-0031) that the Reserve Asset picker's later expansion to the full Jupiter catalogue (DEC-0121-0123) made stale. The on-chain program was already designed for this to be resumable/multi-step (`ReserveStatus`'s own doc comment: "Tracks resumable multi-step creation", `Created -> AssetsInitializing -> Active`) -- the bug was purely client-side over-bundling. Fixed with `packInstructionsBySize` (real, measured transaction-size packing, never reordering instructions) plus a new `resume-from-registration` state in the existing partial-deployment-resume logic, so a batch failing partway through registration (e.g. a declined wallet approval) is cleanly resumable rather than refused as an unsafe mismatch. Known accepted gap: Manager Fee Recipients/Additional Managers aren't retried automatically if their batch specifically fails after earlier registration batches landed (addable afterward via Manage Reserve). 749/749 offline tests passing (8 new), `tsc -b`, `oxlint`, `npm run build` all clean. Not yet live-verified against a real Mainnet 10-asset creation (no browser-automation tool in this environment) -- Creator's own retry is the next confirmation. See DEC-0136.
- **2026-08-21 (Fri), fixed two real post-deploy bugs in the just-shipped Mainnet pricing layer, redeployed to production, live-verified stable (DEC-0135).** Immediately after DEC-0134 deployed, direct live verification of `POST /api/mainnet/asset-prices` against production (not just trusting the prior pass's own reported evidence) showed SSR/ALPHA still resolving as `{usdPrice: null, source: "unavailable"}` -- the fix had not actually landed live. First (later corrected) hypothesis: `JUPITER_API_KEY` was invalid, based on a local script 401ing against Jupiter with the key pulled via `vercel env pull` -- added a defensive unauthenticated-fallback for a 401 and shipped it. That fallback is harmless but wasn't the real bug: a temporary live debug trace on the actual deployed handler showed the authenticated Jupiter request succeeding (200, real price) -- the local 401 had been caused by a parsing bug in the diagnostic script mangling the key, not an invalid production key. The REAL root cause: `validateJupiterPrice`'s block-recency check (`MAX_BLOCK_LAG_SLOTS = 15,000`, ~100 minutes) rejected a genuinely real, valid SSR price because Jupiter's `blockId` for a thin-liquidity pump.fun token like SSR only updates on actual trades, which can legitimately be hours apart -- live-confirmed a real ~19,956-slot (~133 minute) gap. Widened to 216,000 slots (~24 hours), still a real ceiling against a genuinely broken/ancient response. Redeployed; live-verified 3 consecutive calls all returning real, consistent AUM ~$10.17/Token Price ~$1.02/Market Cap ~$10.17 for ALPHA, not intermittent. 1 new regression test reproduces the exact real ALPHA slot numbers. See DEC-0135. This is a reminder that a subagent's own "live-verified" claim still needs independent re-verification before being reported as true -- see DEC-0135's rationale for the full account.
- **2026-08-21 (Fri), implemented the Mainnet USD pricing layer (Pyth Core + Jupiter Price V3), fixed ALPHA's $0/~0.00 display bugs, deployed to production (DEC-0134).** Creator reported the real "alpha" Reserve (100% SSR) showing Token Price/AUM $0 and a Buy "Est. You Receive ~0.00 ALPHA" despite a real past purchase having produced ~9.90 ALPHA. Root cause: `onChainReserve.ts`'s `TEST_ASSET_PRICES_USD` -- a fixed, DevNet-fixture-only test-price table -- was also pricing every Mainnet asset; any real mint outside it (i.e. everything except real USDC) priced as $0, so AUM/NAV/Token Price all deterministically read $0, and the Buy estimate (`numBuyAmount / nav`) divided by that same 0. Live-diagnosed ALPHA directly: reserve `H7NDKmf9pKow6v7eQfBchxRtPb8thP1STsRARGPWwo73`, sole asset SSR (`BpdHpqznEgYPXZNrJVRZvBhdWoafYLVVuLxTQo34pump`, 6 decimals), vault 21,132.446514 SSR, supply 9.95 ALPHA, 1% Mint Fee (matches Creator's report) -- confirmed via live Hermes query that Pyth has NO feed for SSR, and via live Jupiter Price V3 query that it DOES have a real current price (~$0.00048). Built `api/mainnet/asset-prices.ts` (new server endpoint: Pyth Core primary with verified feed IDs/staleness/confidence validation, Jupiter Price V3 fallback with decimals/block-recency validation, never trusts a client-supplied price, never fabricates $0 for an unpriced asset, flags material source deviation) and `packages/sdk/src/pricing.ts` (pure, unit-tested validation/hierarchy logic). Removed the redundant "NAV per Token" card everywhere, replaced with Market Cap (computed independently as supply x Token Price, not AUM relabeled); added a price-source + last-updated caption. Reconciled the Buy estimate directly against the program's own math (`computeDirectReserveTokensRequested` + `computeNetMintOutput`, the exact functions the real transaction uses) instead of a USD-price-derived formula, eliminating any possibility of the displayed quote disagreeing with what's submitted; shows "Quote unavailable" (never a fabricated 0) when live data isn't available. Also caught and fixed two further ALPHA-specific bugs while tracing this chain: Buy/Sell both hardcoded USDC as the only `fetchReserveOnChain` candidate mint (would resolve zero assets and fail outright for any non-USDC Reserve), and the "Settlement asset"/balance UI was unconditionally USDC-labeled/checked regardless of the Reserve's real deposit asset. 32 new/updated regression tests, 737/737 offline tests passing, `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` clean. Deployed to production (`vercel --prod`, https://ssr-fun.vercel.app) and live-verified post-deploy: the live pricing endpoint and a live end-to-end recomputation against ALPHA's real on-chain state both confirm AUM ~$10.17, Token Price ~$1.02, Market Cap ~$10.17 -- no longer $0. No Mainnet program or IDL change (pricing is entirely off-chain/display-layer). See DEC-0134.
- **2026-08-21 (Fri), implemented the USDC fee-settlement redesign (DEC-0133) -- NOT deployed to Mainnet.** Following DEC-0132's deferral, Creator gave a precise spec: lazy continuous TVL-fee accrual (already substantially implemented -- untouched this pass), crystallization into a dedicated fee vault, USDC settlement via redeem-then-Jupiter-swap-then-distribute with slippage/route validation, idempotent keeper-compatible settlement kept separate from accrual, safe retry for a route-less asset. Presented a full design (accounts/instructions/fee math/security/costs/migration/compatibility/deployment plan) via plan mode and got explicit approval before writing code. Implemented 4 new instructions (`redeemFeeVaultShares`, `approveSettlementSwap`, `distributeFeeUsdc`, `setFeeSettlementKeeper`) and 3 new additive PDA account types across `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind`/`accrue_fees` (crystallization destination changed, math untouched), the SDK, and a new (unscheduled) keeper endpoint. **Caught and fixed a serious self-introduced design bug before it ever compiled**: the first draft added the new keeper field directly onto the live, already-initialized `ProtocolConfig` singleton -- which would have broken every single instruction in the program on upgrade (Anchor can't deserialize a bigger struct against a still-smaller on-chain account, and a realloc can't run before a successful deserialization). Fixed by moving it into a new, separate `SettlementKeeperConfig` singleton PDA instead. Also caught and fixed two of its own IDL-regeneration mistakes (wrongly camelCased the committed IDL; two tests had stale hardcoded expectations) before reporting done. 30 new regression tests (13 Rust + 17 TypeScript), 707/707 TS + 13/13 Rust passing, `cargo check`/`clippy`/`cargo-build-sbf`/`tsc`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean. Per Creator's explicit instruction: no Mainnet program upgrade, no live settlement transaction. See DEC-0133 for the full pre-deployment report (fee math, security, costs, migration, deployment plan) and `docs/project/FEE_SETTLEMENT_RUNBOOK.md` for the operational procedure once approved.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed two live-reported Mainnet display bugs, deployed to production (DEC-0131, later the same day still); manager-fee-currency request investigated and deliberately deferred pending Creator confirmation (DEC-0132).** Creator reported: (1) Reserve cards still said "Live on Solana DevNet" on Discover, even for genuinely Mainnet-deployed Reserves; (2) a Mainnet Reserve ("alpha", 100% SSR) showed its composition entry as generic "Asset" instead of "SSR"; (3) manager fees should be charged/claimable in USDC, not the Reserve's own token. Fixed (1): found and cluster-fixed roughly a dozen hardcoded "DevNet" strings, including the ENTIRE global nav/footer/wallet-chip/wallet-panel/connect-modal shell (Shell.tsx/WalletPanel.tsx/WalletModal.tsx), which wraps every page and had never been touched by the Mainnet-launch pass at all. Fixed (2): threaded the Jupiter asset catalogue's mint->symbol map into Reserve discovery (RealReserveSync.tsx's new mainnetMintMeta) so a real Mainnet asset resolves its actual symbol instead of a generic placeholder. Investigated (3) before writing any code: confirmed fees are minted as additional Reserve Token supply on-chain today (an original, deliberate design, not a bug) -- changing this is a real protocol-level Rust redesign of a live Mainnet program with existing pending fee balances and no local test-validator coverage, with two open product questions (where would a 100%-SSR Reserve's USDC fees even come from; what happens to already-accrued pending fees) that need Creator's answer before any code is written. 10 new tests (690/690 total passing), full validation clean, live-verified. See DEC-0131/DEC-0132.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), CONFIRMED ROOT CAUSE of the recurring "seeding step reads zero" incident, deployed to production (DEC-0130, later the same day still).** Creator gave specifics that broke the case open: SSR mint BpdHpqznEgYPXZNrJVRZvBhdWoafYLVVuLxTQo34pump read as 0 for a wallet that actually held ~191,000 SSR (~9x the ~21,000 required), and repeated attempts kept buying MORE SSR while seeding still read zero. Live, read-only forensics (getTokenAccountsByOwner, getAccountInfo, getTransaction, and a direct proxy-method probe -- no signature ever submitted) traced it to `Connection.getTokenAccountBalance` -- the ONLY way the client reads a real balance -- being missing from BOTH api/mainnet/rpc-proxy.ts's and api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts's method allowlists since Jupiter-swap-funded seeding was introduced (DEC-0124); every call was silently rejected and swallowed into a reported balance of 0, deterministically, every single time -- meaning the SAME-DAY DEC-0127/DEC-0128 pass's "RPC eventual consistency" diagnosis for this symptom class was wrong. Fixed by adding the method to both allowlists (confirmed live: the identical proxy call now returns the real 191,598.743106 SSR balance); also reworked seed-funding to swap only the genuine deficit against target instead of the full budget every retry (new `scaleUsdcBudgetForDeficit`), added exponential balance-poll backoff, and excluded Token-2022 mints from the Mainnet Reserve Asset picker (every SDK instruction builder hardcodes the classic Token program, a real separate gap surfaced during this diagnosis). 19 new tests including a self-auditing check that scans actual Connection method usage against both allowlists (680/680 total passing), full validation clean, live-verified. See DEC-0130.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), temporarily disabled the site-wide password gate at Creator's explicit request, deployed to production (DEC-0129, later the same day still).** Added a reversible `SSR_SITE_GATE_ENABLED=false` env toggle rather than deleting Gate 1's code, so it's a one-line env change to re-enable. The internal `/road-to-mainnet`/`/internal/*` dashboard gate is completely unaffected -- still requires its own separate password. Live-verified both halves (public site open, internal dashboard still gated). See DEC-0129.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed the "genuinely successful seed reported as a failure" architecture gap, deployed to production (DEC-0128, later the same day still).** Creator reported: seeding delivered the correct Reserve Token amount, but the UI still said "received less than expected: 0%"/`SeedAmountTooLow` and marked deployment incomplete. Read-only diagnosis (no Reserve state touched or resubmitted): decoded the actual failing transaction's raw instruction data (fixing a base58-vs-base64 decoding bug of my own along the way) and confirmed `seed_amounts=[0]` was genuinely submitted despite the named wallet (6BjTPAWGjUYjL2Hrvz7iVmzWv8yKHNDqUAif5DEPWZen) holding ~128,431 SSR at the time -- independently confirmed via preTokenBalances and a fresh RPC read. Could not fully prove the exact client-side trigger (best-supported, unproven hypothesis: a stale browser tab spanning several same-day deploys) -- stated honestly rather than claimed as certain. Independent of that one transaction, the real architecture gap the report describes is fixed regardless: CreateDTR.tsx now re-checks AUTHORITATIVE on-chain Reserve state (via a new, structurally read-only `checkReserveGenuinelyComplete` -- it cannot fund, swap, or seed anything) BEFORE ever reporting "deployment incomplete," in both the Resume and fresh-submit failure paths; a Reserve that's actually already complete is now finalized as a success through the same UI a normal completion uses, never resubmitted. 14 new regression tests (661/661 total passing), full validation clean, deployed, live-verified (site/create page both 200, no error logs). MCR-01 comment not posted this pass (both site/dashboard passwords came back masked via `vercel env pull`) -- flagged to Creator directly. See DEC-0128.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed a stale post-swap balance read (RPC eventual consistency) and added pre-flight seed-amount validation (DEC-0127, later the same day).** Creator hit a real on-chain revert (`SeedAmountTooLow`) immediately after a shortfall toast claiming a Jupiter swap "delivered 0" SSR -- flagged as "weird" with a specific wallet to check. Directly verified live via RPC: that wallet genuinely holds 107,147.42 SSR right now, proving the swap(s) had actually succeeded; the app's "0" was a stale read immediately after confirmation racing ahead of RPC propagation -- the exact same root-cause class as DEC-0115's earlier redemption-read false alarm. Fixed: the post-swap balance read now retries (up to 6x, 1s apart) until it genuinely reflects the swap instead of trusting one immediate read; and a new pre-flight check (plus the SDK's existing but never-wired-in `validateSeedPlan`) refuses to submit a seed transaction that's still below the protocol's real minimum, naming the exact asset, instead of wasting a transaction on a guaranteed revert. 647/647 offline tests passing (4 new), full validation clean, deployed, live-verified (no funds were ever lost -- confirmed via direct on-chain balance check). Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0127.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed a misleading Jupiter swap failure message and enabled dynamic slippage (DEC-0126, later the same day).** Creator hit a real on-chain swap rejection (`InstructionError`/`Custom(52)` from an external AMM in the Jupiter route) whose explanation was wrongly ssr_protocol-flavored ("check whether the deployed program binary has drifted from this IDL") -- that decoder is scoped to ssr_protocol's own IDL, which the swap transaction never touches. New `describeJupiterSwapError` explains a swap failure honestly (names the real code, states the likely cause -- price moved beyond slippage tolerance between quote and execution -- without claiming certainty about an external program this repo has no IDL for). Also enabled Jupiter's `dynamicSlippage` (previously a fixed 1.5%, too tight for this token's real volatility -- live-confirmed Jupiter independently computes a different, adaptive bound per trade), which should reduce how often this failure happens at all. 643/643 offline tests passing (3 new), full validation clean, deployed, live-verified. Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0126.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), Jupiter swap shortfalls no longer block Reserve creation (DEC-0125, later the same day).** Creator hit DEC-0124's new "resulting balance is still short of the target" error live -- traced to comparing the real swap result against the quote's optimistic estimate rather than Jupiter's actual on-chain slippage guarantee, so routine price movement (normal on a thin-liquidity token) was being treated as a failure. Per Creator's direction ("whatever the resulting value is just create with it... if too short just warn"), removed the block entirely: the Reserve is now always created/seeded with whatever the swap actually delivered, with a non-blocking toast warning only when the result is more than 5% short of quote. 640/640 offline tests passing (4 new), full validation clean, deployed, live-verified. Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0125.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed all 3 MCR-01-reported Create Reserve issues, including real Jupiter-swap-funded Reserve creation (DEC-0124, later the same day).** Creator live-tested Reserve creation on strategic-super-reserve.fun and posted 3 comments on MCR-01: fee-destination field not visibly pre-filled; creating an SSR-backed Reserve with USDC failed demanding the creator already hold SSR directly ("no faucet on Mainnet"), when USDC should trade into it via Jupiter automatically; the Wallet Cost Summary's total didn't match what Phantom asked for one transaction. All confirmed real and fixed: (1) the fee field now backfills once the wallet resolves, without ever overwriting a manual edit; (2) new `api/mainnet/jupiter-swap.ts` builds a real, unsigned Jupiter swap transaction the connected wallet itself signs and submits (never server-custodied) -- `fundSeedAssetsIdempotent` now swaps a USDC shortfall into any non-USDC/non-wrapped-SOL asset automatically, which also surfaced and fixed a real pricing bug (`seedRawAmountForAsset` assumed every asset is worth exactly $1, which is only true for USDC -- the actual reason the error demanded exactly "10" of an asset trading nowhere near that); (3) the cost summary now explicitly explains it's a multi-transaction total (Phantom only ever shows one transaction's cost per popup) and its transaction/fee counts now include the new swap step(s), which they'd undercounted before. 636/636 offline tests passing (11 new), full validation clean, deployed, live-verified (a real Jupiter quote+swap-transaction build for USDC->SSR succeeded). Posted a fix summary as a comment on MCR-01 per Creator's request. See DEC-0124.

Directly follows DEC-0122/0121 -- with thousands of catalogue tokens now selectable, name-only search wasn't enough to confidently find/confirm a specific one. 625/625 offline tests passing (5 new), full validation clean, deployed and live-verified. Raised `api/ledger/asset-catalogue.ts`'s caps from 2000/500 to 6000/5000 rows, comfortably covering the real ~2,600-token verified list -- a general fix (any low-organic-score verified token is now findable via the existing search box), not a single-token carve-out. Live-verified: catalogue response went from 500 to 2,522 tokens, SSR present with correct mint/symbol/decimals. See DEC-0122.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), fixed both live production 500s, DevNet/Mainnet data mixing, and restored the Jupiter Reserve Asset catalogue (DEC-0121, later the same day).** Both reported 500s (homepage, Create-Reserve Wallet Cost Summary) traced via live `vercel logs` to one root cause: `api/mainnet/` was the only api/ subdirectory missing its own `package.json` ({"type":"commonjs"}) to override the root's ESM setting, so `api/mainnet/rpc-proxy.ts`'s compiled output crashed on import (`ReferenceError: exports is not defined`) on every request -- fixed with that one file. Also fixed: the homepage's holder/volume stats and a newly-created Reserve's on-chain `metadata_uri` were unconditionally hitting `/api/devnet/*` routes even on Mainnet (new `api/mainnet/landing-stats.ts`/`api/mainnet/reserve-metadata.ts`, both call sites made cluster-aware); a duplicated homepage warning banner; several hardcoded "DevNet"/"devUSDC" strings shown regardless of actual cluster. Restored the Reserve Asset selector from USDC-only to the full Jupiter Tokens API V2 verified-token catalogue (`api/ledger/asset-catalogue.ts`, new) -- the existing weekly-snapshot code (DEC-0110) had never actually run in production (`JUPITER_API_KEY` was never configured; Creator provided one this pass). Also fixed a second, independent bug found while wiring this up: discovery can only find a Reserve's assets among a caller-supplied candidate-mint list, so widening the picker without widening discovery's candidate list would have made a non-USDC Mainnet Reserve invisible right after creation -- closed via `api/ledger/known-asset-mints.ts` (mints actually used on-chain, not the whole catalogue, to bound live RPC cost) plus a local `useAppStore` registry for the creator's own immediate session. And a third, independent pre-existing bug in `lib/ledger/jupiterCatalogue.ts`: `shapeSnapshotRows` silently dropped Jupiter's `decimals` field, so the first live snapshot (2,577 mints) fetched successfully but the picker's safety filter then excluded every row -- fixed (`decimals`/`tokenProgram` now captured and persisted) with a new `?force=1` re-run option to backfill the columns without violating the snapshot table's per-day unique constraint. 620/620 offline tests passing (33 new/updated), `tsc -b`/`oxlint`/`npm run build` all clean, live-verified end to end post-deploy (real Mainnet RPC calls succeeding, real landing-stats data, 500 real deduplicated catalogue tokens with populated decimals). See DEC-0121 (supersedes DEC-0118's Reserve Asset composition scope only -- its USDC-only settlement design is unchanged).
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), site/dashboard password corrected to Creator's intended value (DEC-0120, later the same day).** Creator reported their expected password wasn't accepting. Checked locally (without printing either secret) and confirmed the value they expected had never actually been configured -- the rejection was correct, not a bug (11-character configured value vs. a 13-character expected one). After confirming with Creator, set both `SSR_SITE_PASSWORD` and `SSR_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` to the provided value and redeployed. Live verification initially looked like a dashboard-login failure (`POST /api/dashboard/login` from a fresh client -> 401) -- traced immediately to `middleware.ts`'s site-wide Gate 1 running in front of `/api/dashboard/login` itself (only `/api/site/login` and the 5 cron paths are exempt), rejecting the request before the dashboard's own password check ever ran. Re-tested correctly (site login first, then dashboard login reusing that session cookie) and both passwords, plus `/internal/status`, confirmed working. See DEC-0120.
- **2026-08-20 (Thu), strategic-super-reserve.fun's site-wide password merged with the internal dashboard password (DEC-0119).** Creator asked to password-protect the site "same password as the other urls" -- clarified via a direct question (given the site was already gated as of yesterday, DEC-0117) that this meant setting `SSR_SITE_PASSWORD` equal to `SSR_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD`, reversing DEC-0117's deliberate choice to keep them independent; Creator was shown that prior rationale and confirmed wanting the merge anyway. No code changed -- `vercel env rm`/`vercel env add` replaced the Production value, then `vercel --prod` redeployed (env var changes don't hot-apply to an already-built deployment).
