See DEC-0119; see new Risks entry below for the resulting access-scope change.
- **2026-08-14 (Fri), claimant-only Manager fee collection, instant Protocol fee transfers (mint AND weekly TVL), and a genuinely time-weighted TVL settlement -- program upgraded on DevNet, deployed live (DEC-0100/DEC-0101/DEC-0102/DEC-0103).** Explicit six-part follow-up task, requiring: (1) only a fee recipient's own wallet can collect its own accrued balance -- root Manager cannot collect for delegates/other recipients, enforced ON-CHAIN; (2) Protocol mint fees transfer instantly in the same transaction as every mint (including the mandatory initial seed), never left pending; (3) the Annualized TVL fee settles weekly using a real time-weighted average of TVL over actual elapsed time (not a latest-snapshot approximation), with the Protocol's share also transferring instantly; (4) remove the entire Protocol Fees section from Manage Reserve; (5) complete the Reserve Activity Log's event coverage. Part 1: `collect_manager_fee_share.rs`'s `recipient` is now a `Signer` (previously permissionless by design, DEC-0094) -- no other wallet can trigger a payout on a recipient's behalf; `ManageDTR.tsx`'s Manager Fee Recipients list now tracks each recipient's Collect button/pending state independently (clicking one never disables another) and shows a read-only "Claimable by this wallet" badge for a non-connected recipient. Part 2: `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind.rs`/`seed_reserve.rs` now CPI-mint the Protocol's fee share directly to treasury inside the mint transaction itself, replacing the same-day DEC-0099 weekly-sweep design for this specific portion (that design's mechanism remains in place only to drain any pre-this-pass legacy pending balance). Part 3: a new `TvlAccrual` PDA per Reserve holds a Uniswap-V2-style cumulative `supply x elapsed_seconds` accumulator; every mint/redeem/seed cheaply checkpoints it, but only the standalone, permissionless `accrue_fees` instruction ever actually settles (mints Protocol's share to treasury, credits Manager's share) -- the weekly keeper (`api/devnet/accrue-fees-cron.ts`) now calls it unconditionally for every Reserve each run, since an active Reserve's fees would otherwise never actually get paid out. A necessary consequence: `update_fee_recipients`'s routing-change gate was rewritten (a continuing recipient's pending balance now carries forward instead of requiring everyone's to be zero, since the old design relied on the now-impossible "Manager collects on everyone's behalf" bundling). Parts 4-5: the Protocol Fees section (pending balance + "Send to Treasury Now") deleted outright from `ManageDTR.tsx`; the Activity Log decoder gained mint/redeem (previously deliberately excluded), instant Protocol transfers, weekly TVL settlements, and Manager fee accrual/claim/routing-change events. **Live-verified on real DevNet** with 2 distinct fee-recipient wallets (root Manager 60% / a second recipient 40%) through a full cycle -- initial seed mint, a subsequent mint, a third mint mid-TVL-period, independent claimant-only collections (including a rejected cross-recipient claim attempt and a rejected duplicate claim), and two back-to-back weekly TVL settlements proving no double-charge on retry -- every on-chain delta matched an independently-computed expected value exactly, real signatures recorded for all of it (`docs/protocol/DEVNET_CLAIMANT_ONLY_TVL_SETTLEMENT_CHECKLIST_2026-08-14.md`, 17/17 items passed). 594 offline tests passing, `cargo check`/`cargo-build-sbf`, `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. Program upgrade signature `WGkDTY31YPqewUcw5doaH2L5Gpd7D4NhYAcTboW5RjYhdp3WwaX99KzmdYAE5RPiXTV3jwHcT9Fa3cNMRTMo2KY` (slot 483836553). See DEC-0100/DEC-0101/DEC-0102/DEC-0103.
- **2026-08-14 (Fri), the initial seed mint is now fee-charged like any other mint, and the Protocol is a genuinely passive fee claimant -- program upgraded on DevNet, deployed live (DEC-0099).** User reported 0 collectible fees right after creating a Reserve, correctly diagnosing that the inception seed is itself a mandatory mint that the program was special-casing as fee-free. Asked directly (confirmed via clarifying question before touching the deployed program, given the blast-radius difference) whether "the Protocol never has to claim its fees" should cover only the new inception fee or all three accrual sources (seed, ordinary Buy mints, annualized TVL fee) -- user confirmed all three, plus a specific cadence requirement: the TVL fee "accrued on a weekly basis, every Monday at 00:00 UK time." `seed_reserve.rs` now charges the same effective Protocol/Manager mint-fee split as `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind.rs` (the SSR.fun fee formula, DEC-0094), minting the creator only the net amount after the fee. A new, fully additive `collect_protocol_fee` instruction lets anyone sweep ONLY the Protocol's pending share to its fixed treasury ATA (Manager's own pending balance untouched, still collected on their own schedule via the existing UI) -- deliberately NOT a same-transaction CPI-mint inside mint/redeem/accrue (which would have touched every live Buy/Sell's account list), so the Protocol becomes passive via a scheduled sweep instead: `api/devnet/accrue-fees-cron.ts`'s existing weekly TVL-accrual keeper now also calls this for every Reserve with a nonzero pending Protocol balance, and its cron schedule moved to `0 0 * * 1` (00:00 UTC Monday, the closest fixed-UTC match to UK midnight). ManageDTR.tsx's Protocol Fees section is reworded honestly and its manual button now calls the new protocol-only collection instead of the old one that silently force-collected the Manager's balance too. **Live-verified on real DevNet, both parts, in one Reserve creation:** seeding at a 2% mint fee netted the creator exactly 9.8 of the 10 tokens requested with both Manager and Protocol pending shares reading 100,000 immediately (the reported bug, confirmed fixed); calling `collect_protocol_fee` grew the real Protocol treasury balance from 0 to 100,000, zeroed the Protocol's pending share, left the Manager's completely untouched, and a second collection attempt correctly failed with `NoPendingFees`. Two real integration bugs found and fixed live during this same pass: a missing hand-patched IDL entry for the new account (traced to `anchor idl build` itself failing in this environment on an unrelated mingw linker issue), and Anchor's client not auto-resolving a not-yet-initialized optional account as None (needed the documented program-ID sentinel explicitly). 505/505 offline tests passing (490 prior + 15 new), `cargo check`/`clippy`/`build-sbf`, `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. Program upgrade signature `5RuJiMBAEAKRxNiDth2FMfYS7KFmahsySX3yEt9AHwpSV9VuZ8SqkvQKbrtdEwXtL9mxZ4Cdyx3UPfpDUcggHogb`. See DEC-0099.
- **2026-08-14 (Fri), Create Reserve's "Additional Managers" now actually grants real on-chain delegates, and a same-day metadata-display regression from DEC-0097 is fixed too (DEC-0098).** User reported a delegate added during Reserve creation never appeared on the Manager Dashboard ("No delegates found on-chain"), while the same wallet added AFTER creation via ManageDTR.tsx worked correctly -- their own diagnosis: "add delegate on reserve creation is not doing anything," confirmed exactly right. Root cause: CreateDTR.tsx's "Additional Managers" list was collected into local UI state and shown on Review implying it would be applied, but `createReserveOnChain` had no parameter for it at all -- purely decorative, never submitted. Fixed by bundling one real `add_delegate` instruction per additional manager into the SAME create-and-register transaction (same pattern DEC-0094 already uses for Manager fee recipients -- no extra wallet approval), granted as a RESTRICTED delegate with exactly the permissions CreateDTR.tsx's own copy already promises ("rebalance, manage fees, and pause the reserve, but won't be able to manage other delegates"). Separately, the user's own repro also surfaced "Unnamed Reserve (#57)"/"RSV57" -- traced to a same-day regression from DEC-0097 (shipped hours earlier): `parseReserveMetadataUri` only ever understood the OLD inline `data:` URI convention, so it returned null for every Reserve created after DEC-0097 switched to permanent HTTPS URLs, silently falling back to the honest-but-wrong "Unnamed Reserve" placeholder. Fixed with a new async `resolveReserveMetadata` that tries the synchronous data: case first, then fetches an http(s) URL when needed (bounded, best-effort, cached for 24h -- metadata is immutable in practice). 490/490 offline tests passing (472 prior + 18 new). **Live-verified on real DevNet, both bugs together, in one Reserve creation:** a freshly generated, never-before-connected wallet was confirmed as a real on-chain Delegate account (permissions=222 exactly matching the promised capability set, restricted=true) immediately after creation with zero action from that wallet itself, and the Reserve's metadata correctly resolved to its real name/ticker instead of "Unnamed Reserve" -- create-and-register signature `4HkiNNLVxhr2C4EPwqj6unXCTM8tiRhwU7gBCjz3NLpgPhAT8XMAB7Wi1zarXiuA5vneEV7yEpoQQqmM3nfw1B6W`, Reserve `3DJsyPZj7cTKowWgu6Gfa5upAsS6gLz8Ju2uzjz45v2a`. `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0098.
- **2026-08-14 (Fri), Create Reserve's `SsrError::MetadataUriTooLong` failure fixed, live-reproduced and live-verified on real DevNet (DEC-0097).** User reported the failure at Step 1/2 (create-and-register), nothing created on-chain. Confirmed root cause by live reproduction: CreateDTR.tsx submitted the Reserve's entire name/ticker/description/category/buyTaxPct/sellTaxPct payload inline on-chain as a `data:application/json,...` URI (343 bytes for a realistic description) against the on-chain 200-byte `MAX_METADATA_URI_LEN` -- instead of uploading it somewhere permanent and submitting only a short link. The 200-byte limit itself was verified sufficient for a real HTTPS/IPFS/Arweave URL (worked examples in DEC-0097) and left unchanged, per explicit instruction. Fix: a new off-chain Reserve Metadata Store (`lib/reserve-metadata/`, Postgres via the same Neon database DEC-0088 already provisioned) plus a new public endpoint (`api/devnet/reserve-metadata.ts`) that stores validated metadata under a deterministic, content-hashed id (retry-safe -- identical content always reuses the same row/URL) and serves it back; CreateDTR.tsx now uploads metadata as soon as Name/Ticker are filled in, shows the resulting permanent URL on the Review & Deploy step, and disables Launch until it's ready. A new shared constant (`packages/sdk/src/metadataUri.ts`) mirrors the on-chain byte limit exactly (genuine UTF-8 bytes, never JS's UTF-16 `.length`) and is enforced in two places: CreateDTR.tsx before ever opening the wallet, and inside `createReserveOnChain` itself as the true single choke point every caller goes through. **Live-verified on real DevNet, both directions:** Part A reproduced the exact original failure -- a real create-and-register transaction with the old-style 343-byte URI was genuinely rejected with `MetadataUriTooLong` (custom error 0x1770/6000), and `reserve_count` was confirmed unchanged before/after (zero partial state). Part B proved the fix -- real metadata uploaded to the live endpoint (83 bytes), round-tripped correctly, then a real create-and-register transaction succeeded (signature `5R71YzQ6YLpX4WDDuSJ3UwL7h2um113L6Z7GdbR42ZvMY6scZi4pMQ23zzQCXEA22JkMBHwmfct2zccQnYaU7wT5`, Reserve `9dMmiwMVtkyQrGcKpdGLMcB5yVoHJcW9D5FWNrYaHg2S`, reserveId 56), with the on-chain `metadata_uri` confirmed to match exactly. 472/472 offline tests passing (445 prior + 27 new), `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0097.
- **2026-08-14 (Fri), delegated-Reserve discovery fixed -- a genuinely on-chain delegate grant now reliably appears on the Manager Dashboard (DEC-0096).** User reported that after adding a delegate, the Delegates tab still showed "1 delegate(s) reported on-chain, but none matched this discovery pass's candidate wallets." Root cause: `discoverDelegatesForReserve` can only resolve a delegate wallet it's told to check (a `Delegate` account is a PDA seeded by `(reserve, wallet)` with no on-chain reverse index, and the public DevNet RPC blocks the `getProgramAccounts` scan that would otherwise enumerate them), but every caller's candidate list was a small hardcoded set (manager + 2 DevNet fixture wallets + whichever wallet happened to be connected) -- a delegate granted to any OTHER wallet (the ordinary real case) was invisible forever. Compounding it, `ManageDTR.tsx`'s/`DTRDetail.tsx`'s manual "refresh now" path never re-ran delegate discovery at all (it silently preserved the last-resolved list), and an existing store action for writing fresh delegate results (`setOnChainDelegates`) was defined but never actually called anywhere. Fix: a new local, per-browser, per-Reserve "known delegate wallets" registry (`src/merge/lib/delegateDiscoveryCandidates.ts`, same established pattern as `delegateLabels.ts`) records every wallet this browser grants delegate status to, the instant the grant confirms -- the app already knows that exact address, it just never remembered it -- and feeds it into a single shared candidate-list builder now used identically by the background poll (`RealReserveSync.tsx`) and both pages' manual refresh, which is also now wired to actually call `setOnChainDelegates`. Every entry is still only ever a discovery HINT, always re-verified against a genuine on-chain fetch/decode before being trusted -- a revoked delegate (closed account) is never shown, and no permission is ever read from anywhere but the freshly decoded on-chain account. The connected delegate wallet's own view was already structurally correct (a direct PDA lookup is authoritative, not a guess) and remains so, now also kept accurate on manual refresh. 444/444 offline tests passing (430 prior + 14 new regression tests), `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0096.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), DEC-0094 follow-up: removed the forced 0.5% minimum fee for a genuine 0% configuration, re-enabled Buy/Sell tax as forward-looking secondary-market configuration (DEC-0095).** Immediately after DEC-0094 shipped, the user reviewed the live dashboard and asked for two changes. **(1)** A manager who genuinely configures 0% Mint fee or 0% Annualized TVL fee now gets a real 0% effective fee -- Protocol and Manager both receive nothing -- rather than the fee being floored up to 0.5%. The 0.5% Protocol floor still applies to any NONZERO configured fee, unchanged. Fixed in `fee_math::split_configured_bps` (special-cases `configured_bps == 0` to `(0, 0)`) and its TS mirror; also fixed a real `DivisionByZero` bug this change would otherwise have introduced in `split_total_fee`/`splitTotalFee` (the effective-total-bps divisor can now legitimately be 0). **(2)** Buy Tax/Sell Tax sliders (0-2%, previously locked to a fixed 0% display for real Reserves after DEC-0094) are re-enabled and manager-configurable again. Confirmed directly with the user this is forward-looking configuration for a FUTURE secondary market (e.g. a DEX listing) -- not enforced by minting or redeeming directly from the Reserve today, since no such on-chain mechanism exists and none was built this pass. Stored in the Reserve's `metadataUri` JSON (extending the existing name/ticker/description/category convention), read back via `parseReserveMetadataUri`, defaulting to 0 for Reserves created before this field existed; every visible label now says plainly this isn't yet enforced, rather than implying it is. Program rebuilt and upgraded on DevNet (`3nVMFCETD87LfgWruJNJdQ6Ke5BPQuRnovfyAMdKFihEHQWQsr2KDFDt2S4Ftf6oqjTS7agXgKNppp8vVPqWogY1`). 430/430 offline tests passing (existing tests corrected in place for the new, deliberately different 0%-case behavior), `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. **Live-verified on real DevNet** (`scripts/verify_zero_fee_floor_removed_devnet.ts`): a fresh Reserve configured at 0% Mint fee (reserveId 53) had a real Buy mint the depositor's full requested amount with zero fee deducted and both pending fee fields read exactly 0 on-chain. See DEC-0095.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), SSR.fun fee formula (0.5% Protocol minimum, 50/50 above it) + native up-to-10 Manager fee recipients per Reserve, program upgraded on DevNet, committed, pushed, and deployed live to production (DEC-0094).** User requested a complete audit of SSR.fun's fee configuration/accounting plus on-chain support for up to 10 Manager fee recipients per Reserve. Replaced the old caller-chosen manager/protocol split ratio entirely with a formula computed fresh at every mint/accrual (`protocol_bps = max(50, configured_bps / 2)`, `manager_bps = max(configured_bps - protocol_bps, 0)`), applied independently to the Mint fee and the Annualized TVL fee, uniformly to every Reserve old and new (confirmed with the user, since a Reserve configured below the 0.5% floor now sees its effective fee rise). Added a new, fully additive, fixed-size `ManagerFeeRecipients` account (up to 10 slots, largest-remainder apportionment, credited at accrual time so routing changes can never reallocate already-accrued fees) rather than growing `Reserve`/`FeeConfig` in place -- this codebase has zero realloc precedent and ~40 live Reserve accounts, so a separate account sidesteps all migration risk; every accrual/collection/closure instruction takes it as a genuine `Option<Account>` (verified real in the pinned `anchor-lang 1.1.2` by reading the actual crate source), so a not-yet-opted-in Reserve keeps working with zero migration, zero disruption. New instructions `initialize_manager_fee_recipients`/`update_fee_recipients`/`collect_manager_fee_share` (gated by the existing-but-previously-unused `MANAGE_FEES` delegate permission); `collect_fees.rs` deliberately left untouched (an initial split-it-up draft was reversed on review once its existing `if shares > 0` guards were confirmed to already degrade correctly). Also implemented the user's mid-session TVL-cadence request: the existing pro-rata accrual now piggybacks for free on every real mint/redeem transaction, plus a new weekly `CRON_SECRET`-gated Vercel Cron endpoint (`api/devnet/accrue-fees-cron.ts`) as the permissionless fallback for dormant Reserves, with a ~25-day trigger threshold well inside the 30-day requirement. Found and fixed 3 incidental bugs blocking this pass's own build in `execute_rebalance_leg.rs` (a `CpiContext` account-vs-key mixup, an elided-lifetime handler signature, and a genuine BPF stack-frame overflow fixed by boxing the largest account) -- pre-existing, unrelated to this pass's scope, first ever `cargo build-sbf` run for that instruction. 429/429 offline tests passing (395 prior + 34 new in `tests/phase_manager_fee_recipients.ts`), `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. Program rebuilt (`cargo-build-sbf`) and upgraded on DevNet (`DVoW5bEj5u2VkRwpTLvRVzaf3wQK3H8Byd64Ty5kwN9GWZYRqCSDxuewdpVNPqvpF5mWkeqUTfC2wuiJJHydpt6`, data length grew 657,848 -> 807,712 bytes). **Live-verified end-to-end with real signed DevNet transactions** (`scripts/verify_manager_fee_recipients_devnet.ts`, ALL PARTS PASSED): the exact formula table for configured 0%/1%/5% mint fee against fresh Reserves; a 3-recipient (50/30/20) split with exact largest-remainder apportionment and independent self-serve + third-party-permissionless collection; and a genuinely PRE-EXISTING, pre-upgrade Reserve (reserveId 28, ~8 real days since its last accrual) accruing a real nonzero TVL fee via the new formula with zero migration step -- direct proof of backward compatibility. Full transcript in `docs/protocol/DEVNET_FEE_RECIPIENTS_VERIFY_OUTPUT_2026-08-13.txt`. **Not done this pass**: the on-chain IDL account (Solana Program Metadata standard, DEC-0092's precedent) was not republished -- deprioritized for DevNet verification given session time; app functionality is unaffected (reads the IDL from `packages/sdk/idl/` directly), only Solscan's own instruction-decoding UI is stale. See DEC-0094.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), complete Wind-down/Closure flow fixed -- program upgraded on DevNet, committed, pushed, and deployed live to production (DEC-0093).** User reported four failures: Sell blocked during Wind-down despite Buy correctly disabled; `collect_fees` failing with `Custom 2040` (outside ssr_protocol's error range); a real 100 devUSDC Buy with a configured 50% protocol fee share producing an observed ~80/20 Manager/Protocol split; and no assurance fees/closure were safe around wind-down. **Bug #1**: the on-chain program was already correct (`require_redemption_allowed` has always permitted WindDown redemption) -- the actual bug was `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`'s server co-signer endpoint using one Active-only status gate for both Buy and Sell; split so Sell now correctly allows Active/Paused/WindDown via a newly-exported canonical `isRedemptionAllowedForStatus`. **Bug #2**: root-caused exactly -- `2040` is Anchor's OWN framework error `ConstraintDuplicateMutableAccount`, firing whenever a Reserve's `manager_fee_destination` happens to equal the protocol treasury (confirmed live across all 40 real DevNet Reserves: 11 have this exact collision, including the reported reserveId 37 "VVVV"). Fixed with Anchor's own `dup` constraint escape hatch in `collect_fees.rs` -- safe because both mutations are independent CPIs, not Anchor struct write-backs. **Bug #3**: no calculation bug existed -- the reported Reserve's 80/20 split is its own genuine, immutable, pre-DEC-0089 configuration, proven config-driven (not hardcoded) by creating a fresh Reserve with a 30/70 split live on DevNet and confirming the on-chain fee shares landed at exactly 300,000/700,000. A real, separate rounding-completeness bug WAS found and fixed during this investigation: independent floor-rounding on both fee shares in `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind.rs`/`accrue_fees.rs` could leave 1 raw unit of fee unallocated; fixed via floor+exact-remainder (manager floor-rounded, protocol is the exact remainder), now guaranteed valid since `create_reserve.rs` requires the two share bps to sum to exactly 10,000. **Bug #4**: `accrue_fees`/`collect_fees` were already unconditional on Reserve status -- confirmed nothing erases/freezes fees during wind-down; UI transparency improved instead (configured split % now shown in the Manage dashboard). **Bug #5**: found and fixed a real, previously entirely-unguarded gap -- `close_reserve` checked Reserve Token supply but never checked pending fee shares, so a manager could close a Reserve with real uncollected fees, permanently stranding them; added a new `PendingFeesNotCollected` check (error 6043). Live-proven both ways: closure correctly rejected while fees are pending, and (a genuinely non-obvious finding) still correctly rejected immediately after collecting, since `collect_fees` mints new outstanding Reserve Token supply to the recipients that must ALSO be redeemed -- a second, fully self-controlled test Reserve completed the full cycle to a genuine close success. Required user-facing copy wired in for all four failure classes (raw errors now console-only); Close Reserve now pre-checks pending fees client-side before submitting. Program rebuilt (`cargo-build-sbf`) and upgraded on DevNet (`EQKWBsze7FLXWt1kYBL9vGbbGSdahd6v9comaaqvVzRPQAHDBJ88qUWbNXofDBSfiYzCHvTNmgRA2o2T1MSa8uJ`). 395/395 offline tests passing (20 new), `npm run build`/`npm run lint` clean. **Live-verified end-to-end with real signed DevNet transactions** including collecting fees on the exact real, previously-broken reserveId-37 collision Reserve (`5jeAvnbkKoMTDeVB9heUdsQZfxEwaKvX4bdoATqhNMNTsih1okA3uYf1doBMaDbzzELqFLvmbW31LfDQNfksK1Pu`, no more Custom 2040) -- full signature list and honest per-item checklist evidence in `docs/protocol/DEVNET_WINDDOWN_CLOSURE_FEES_CHECKLIST_2026-08-13.md`. See DEC-0093.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), full 23-instruction ssr_protocol naming/Solscan-identifiability audit (DEC-0092): committed, pushed, and deployed live to production.** User asked to audit every deployed instruction's name for clarity/consistency and make the program properly identifiable on Solscan. Read every one of the 23 currently-deployed instructions' Rust source, accounts, and doc comments, cross-referenced the committed IDL and every frontend/SDK call site. **Conclusion: no instruction needed renaming** -- all 23 names were already clear, consistent, Reserve/SSR-terminology names (`create_reserve`, `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind`, `initiate_wind_down`, etc.), with zero legacy DTR/BYOR/BOR contamination, matching CLAUDE.md's pre-existing rule that Anchor instruction names are exempt from the terminology migration absent a separate real reason. Fixed the one safe finding: a stale comment in `reserve_asset.rs` citing an already-superseded terminology mapping (comment-only). The real reason Solscan may not decode this program turned out to be that **no on-chain IDL had ever been published for it** -- fixed by publishing `packages/sdk/idl/ssr_protocol.json` via the Solana Program Metadata standard, signed by the real upgrade authority, independently re-fetched and confirmed byte-identical. Mathematically verified all 23 discriminators (no build needed) and confirmed the deployed program's bytecode length is unchanged since DEC-0048 -- program/IDL/frontend are provably consistent. Exercised 20 of the 23 instructions with real signed DevNet transactions against one disposable test Reserve (`scripts/verify_instruction_audit_devnet.ts`); cited existing historical signatures for the 3 that shouldn't be re-run (`initialize_protocol`: one-time genesis; `update_protocol_config`: shared protocol-wide singleton; `close_reserve`: this pass's test Reserve permanently retains a tiny protocol-fee-share balance this session can't redeem, so it stays open on DevNet -- harmless, documented). Every signature's decoded instruction-name log line was independently confirmed via `solana confirm -v`; Solscan's own rendered UI could not be visually re-confirmed (gated behind an interactive Cloudflare human-verification checkbox, not bypassed -- links provided for a one-time manual click-through). Also refreshed `docs/protocol/INSTRUCTION_REFERENCE.md` (was missing the 6 instructions added since Phase F/G, and had several stale "Frontend use" lines) and produced the full checklist at `docs/protocol/DEVNET_INSTRUCTION_AUDIT_2026-08-13.md`. No program rebuild, no redeploy, no discriminator changed; nothing touches Mainnet (none exists for this program). 375/375 offline tests passing (unchanged), `npm run build`/`npm run lint` clean, `cargo check -p ssr_protocol` clean for all 23 real instructions. **Committed (`7ca86cc`), pushed to `origin/main`, and deployed live** via `vercel --prod` (deployment `dpl_FG8FfucjGVpbUoWnQutKsm5zAz8x`, aliased to `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`www.strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun.vercel.app`); post-deploy `curl -I` returned `200`, `vercel logs --since 3m` showed zero `error`/`fatal`/`fail`-matching lines (no frontend behavior actually changed this pass -- deployed because explicitly requested). See DEC-0092.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), global Reserve search bar rebuilt as a real live autocomplete (DEC-0091): committed, pushed, and deployed live to production.** User reported the navbar search "displays the entire Reserve list regardless of what the user types" -- root cause was that the old search bar never actually searched anything: it just navigated to `/discover?q=<query>` on submit, and `Discover.tsx` never read that `q` param at all. Replaced `Shell.tsx`'s old `NavSearch` with a new dropdown component (`src/components/ReserveSearch.tsx`) backed by a pure, offline-tested ranking module (`src/lib/reserveSearch.ts`). Matches by full/partial name, ticker, or Reserve Token contract address (`onChain.reserveTokenMint`, falling back to the Reserve account address), case-insensitive, ranked exact > prefix > partial (ties broken by AUM then name), live with no debounce (pure in-memory filtering, no RPC). Reads reactively from `useAppStore`'s `dtrs` -- the same authoritative, already-eligibility-filtered dataset Discover.tsx uses -- so a newly created Reserve is searchable immediately with zero extra wiring. Empty query shows a labeled "Popular Reserves" list (top 5 by AUM) instead of the full roster; no matches shows "No Reserves found"; matched substrings are highlighted; full keyboard support (arrows wrap at both ends, Enter selects, Escape closes); closes on outside click. Also added a mobile entry point that didn't exist before -- the old CSS hid the search bar outright below 1000px with no replacement at all -- via a small icon toggle that opens a full-width overlay row. A bug in an early inline keyboard-nav implementation (ArrowUp from "nothing highlighted" landed on the wrong item) was caught specifically because the nav-index math was extracted into its own tested pure function rather than left as inline component state logic. 375/375 offline tests passing (342 prior + 33 new in `tests/phase_reserve_search.ts`), `npm run build` and `npm run lint` both clean, plus live Playwright verification (desktop + mobile viewports) against both the dev server and the deployed production site. **Committed (`698145f`), pushed to `origin/main`, and deployed live** via `vercel --prod` (deployment `dpl_7zGvSuMjow77uUE9C9QaofyrNuo3`, aliased to `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`www.strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun.vercel.app`, confirmed via `vercel inspect`); post-deploy `curl -I` returned `200` and `vercel logs --since 3m` showed zero `error`/`fatal`/`fail`-matching lines. See DEC-0091.
- **2026-08-13 (Thu), transaction-confirmation notifications now link to Solscan with a copyable full signature (DEC-0090): committed, pushed, and deployed live to production.** User reported the notification shown after actions like Submit Rebalance truncates the signature, has no way to copy the full value, and isn't linked to Solscan. Added a shared `TransactionConfirmedCard` (`src/merge/components/TransactionConfirmation.tsx`) and a cluster-aware `solscanUrl()` helper (`src/merge/lib/solana-config.ts`, mirroring the existing `explorerUrl()`'s devnet/mainnet logic) and applied it to every transaction-success notice site-wide: Buy/Sell confirmed (`DTRDetail.tsx`), Submit Rebalance/Collect Fees/Grant Delegate/Fund Asset/etc. (`ManageDTR.tsx`, whose toast previously showed a literal `signature.slice(0, 20) + "..."` with no link at all), Reserve deployed/resumed (`CreateDTR.tsx`, whose toasts previously interpolated an explorer URL into a plain string -- rendering as inert raw text, not even a clickable link), and the devUSDC/DevNet SOL faucet-claim confirmations (`DevnetOnboarding.tsx`). The whole card is a single clickable, keyboard-operable (Enter/Space) region with plain-language text ("X confirmed -- View on Solscan") that opens the real, never-truncated signature on the correct Solscan cluster in a new tab; a separate copy button (own tooltip/aria-label) copies the full signature without also navigating. A live-driven headless-browser test (Playwright against `npm run dev`, temporary trigger added and fully reverted before committing) caught and fixed a real keyboard-only bug: pressing Enter while the copy button itself was focused also opened Solscan, because its keydown bubbled up to the card's own handler -- fixed by having the card ignore keydowns not targeting itself and having the copy button stop its own Enter/Space from bubbling. `npm run build` (typecheck + build) and `npm run lint` both clean. **Committed (`fb0739f`), pushed to `origin/main`, and deployed live** via `vercel --prod` (deployment `dpl_7UT3LTteZ4GtyLgrPML5Sh3D2oqA`, aliased to `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`www.strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun.vercel.app`, confirmed via `vercel inspect`); post-deploy `curl -I` returned `200` and `vercel logs --since 3m` showed zero `error`/`fatal`/`fail`-matching lines. See DEC-0090.

Also investigated the reported Delegates-tab edit/remove-greyed-out issue: the gating logic (`isRoot`/`hasOnChainPermission`/`canAddRestrictedDelegateOnChain`/`canRemoveRestrictedDelegateOnChain`) correctly implements the documented, already-live-verified on-chain permission model in every traced path -- root manager is never blocked in the current code -- so this was NOT changed pending clarification on which wallet/Reserve exhibited it, rather than risk weakening real access control on a guess. 342/342 offline tests (unchanged), `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint`, `npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0089.
- **2026-08-12 (Wed), Reserve Activity Log now Postgres-backed instead of live-RPC-per-view; removed "Locked" sidebar badges (DEC-0088).** User reported the Activity tab's live RPC dependency directly ("shouldnt rely on RPC... internal to the website... all the log since reserve creation... keep it light"). Added a small Postgres index (`reserve_activity_log`/`reserve_activity_cursor`, reusing the Neon Postgres already provisioned for `lib/road-to-mainnet` -- no new service) and a resumable, per-request-bounded indexer (`lib/reserve-activity/indexer.ts`): an incremental top-up (one page from the newest signature) plus, only while incomplete, one bounded backfill step (2 pages) continuing from a per-Reserve cursor -- never an unbounded scan, converges to a Reserve's COMPLETE history since creation over a handful of views. A new public endpoint (`api/devnet/reserve-activity.ts`) syncs best-effort then always reads from Postgres -- an RPC failure during sync is reported (`syncError`) but never blocks returning what's already cached, which is the actual fix for the reported "Could not read on-chain history right now (DevNet RPC congestion)" hard failure. `ManageDTR.tsx`'s Activity tab now fetches from this endpoint instead of walking ~200 RPC calls live from the browser on every tab-open (capped at ~100 recent entries before this pass). Also removed the "Locked" badges next to the Delegates/Rebalance sidebar tabs (the tabs were never actually disabled -- clicking either already showed a permission-appropriate read-only view; only the badge is gone) and fixed activity-entry links to use the shared `explorerUrl` helper instead of a hardcoded literal. RPC health itself was explicitly NOT investigated this pass, per the user's own instruction to deprioritize it. **Live-verified** via `scripts/verify_reserve_activity_index.ts` against the real persistent Gate-9 fixture Reserve: indexed 19 real governance events matching DEC-0083's own live evidence for this Reserve, `backfill_complete` correctly true, and a second immediate call left the row count unchanged (idempotent against real data). Migration applied for real against the live Neon database. 342/342 offline tests passing (7 new), `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint`, `npm run build` all clean. See DEC-0088.
- **2026-08-12 (Wed), Reserve Composition card cleanup + mainnet-ready P&L % column (DEC-0087).** The trade page's Reserve Composition card (pie chart + table) now hides any asset a rebalance has driven to exactly 0% target weight (it stays registered on-chain per `update_targets`' config-only design, but no longer clutters this display) -- filtered locally in `DTRDetail.tsx` only, since `ManageDTR.tsx`'s Rebalance tab still needs to show a 0%-weight asset so it can be edited. Added a new "P&L %" column after "Value in Reserve", backed by two new pure functions (`referenceAssetPriceUsd`/`calcReserveAssetPnlPct`, `calculations.ts`) that compare an asset's current price against a reference price -- both currently read the same DevNet-fixed `TEST_ASSET_PRICES_USD` table (no live oracle or historical entry-price snapshot exists yet), so every asset correctly shows 0.00% today. This is a deliberate, explicit product decision: ship the real formula/plumbing now, honestly reporting "no movement possible yet" rather than an "N/A" placeholder, so it's ready to work the moment Mainnet pricing exists -- see PROJECT_STATUS.md's own Mainnet-Readiness Gaps ("Oracle/pricing"). Also corrected "Value in Reserve" (for real on-chain Reserves) from a target-weight-derived estimate to the asset's actual current balance x price, so it's internally consistent with the new P&L % column sitting next to it. Confirmed, per explicit user request, that "log history of rebalance changes and delegates added" already exists in full (Manage Reserve's Activity tab, DEC-0083) -- no code change needed there. 334/334 offline tests passing (3 new), `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, `npm run build` all clean. - **2026-08-12 (Wed), Rebalance-tab follow-up fix from live user feedback (DEC-0085): committed (`f2d1f67`), pushed, and deployed live to production** (`vercel --prod`, deployment `dpl_4cq8kk4C59bujNhYg6KD9MYBWkjA`, aliased to `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`www.strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun.vercel.app`, confirmed via `vercel inspect`; post-deploy `curl -I` returned `200`, `vercel logs --since 3m` showed zero `error`/`fatal`/`fail`-matching lines). Fixed a real bug reported right after DEC-0084 went live: reducing an existing asset to 0% and driving a newly-added asset to 100% could show a false "must total exactly 100%" error immediately after a genuine, successful Phantom approval. Root cause: Submit Rebalance's success handler unconditionally pruned the just-registered asset out of `sessionAddedAssets` the moment the signature confirmed, assuming the very next on-chain refresh would already show it -- but an RPC read moments after "confirmed" can still lag behind (a real read-after-write consistency gap), so the asset could briefly vanish from `proposedAssetRows` entirely (no longer a session row, not yet an on-chain row), silently dropping its weight from the summed total on a proposal that genuinely WAS 100%. Fixed by pruning `sessionAddedAssets` only from truth -- inside the same seeding effect that already reacts to confirmed on-chain data -- never optimistically. Also fixed `handleSliderChange` reading its "current composition" snapshot from a render-time closure instead of the latest state inside its `setState` updater (could let edits fired faster than a re-render build on a stale starting point), and uniformized the per-asset copy from "Current X% -> $Y projected" to "Current X%, -> Y% proposed ($Z balance projected)" per direct user feedback. 331/331 offline tests passing (net +1 vs. DEC-0084's 330 -- two initially-added tests were found not to actually reproduce the reported defect, which lives in React state timing rather than the pure redistribution function, and were corrected). `tsc -b --force`, `oxlint`, full `npm run build` all clean. Not live-verified against a real Phantom approval in this environment (no browser-automation/wallet tool available, same documented gap as every prior pass) -- the original defect itself WAS reported by a real user against the live production deploy, which is why this fix exists. See DEC-0085.
- **2026-08-12 (Wed), Rebalance-tab slider redesign + add_reserve_asset_active root-cause fix (DEC-0084): typechecked, linted, unit-tested offline, committed, pushed, AND deployed live to production.** Root cause of the reported `add_reserve_asset_active` custom-program-error: the prior AR-01 (DEC-0083) Add-Asset flow called `add_reserve_asset_active` with whatever nonzero weight% the user typed for a NEW asset while every EXISTING asset still held its old on-chain weight, so on any already-~100%-allocated Reserve (the normal case) any nonzero Add weight breached the on-chain 10,000bps cap (`SsrError::TargetWeightExceedsTotal`, confirmed against `add_reserve_asset_active.rs`'s exact `require!` order). **Fixed at the root, not hidden**: new assets now always register at `target_weight_bps=0` (which can never breach the cap), and every asset's real final weight -- new and existing together -- is set via exactly one `update_targets` call afterward. Both are now batched into ONE wallet-signed transaction (`executeSubmitRebalance`, `src/merge/lib/managementClient.ts`), replacing the old per-click-immediate-transaction Add/per-click Submit flow. The entire Rebalance tab (real on-chain branch only; the separate, wallet-free simulated/demo branch is unchanged) is now a true local-preview editor: adding, removing, and adjusting an asset's weight are pure client state with zero wallet prompts, via a new pure function (`applySliderWeightChange`, `src/merge/lib/rebalanceSlider.ts`) implementing a devUSDC-priority cash-bucket model -- reducing a non-devUSDC asset feeds devUSDC, increasing one draws from devUSDC first then proportionally from other assets, dragging devUSDC itself is symmetric, and the proposed composition always sums to exactly 10,000bps by construction (13 new offline tests, `tests/phase_rebalance_slider.ts`, cover every rule including the 100%-drains-everything-else-to-exactly-0 case and rounding-remainder determinism). Every asset row now has a shadcn `Slider` (mirroring `CreateDTR.tsx`'s existing Basket Composition pattern) with a yellow marker at its real current on-chain target weight, a live projected USD value (proposed weight x total Reserve USD value) alongside its real current balance, and a "New" badge for a not-yet-registered asset. The button is renamed "Submit Rebalance" (from "Submit Target Weights (real DevNet tx)") and is the ONLY action in this tab that prompts a wallet signature -- Fund and permanently-removing an already-registered empty/last-index asset remain separate, clearly-labeled maintenance actions with their own individual approvals, unchanged, per explicit product scope decision. Separately, wired `describeOnChainError` (existing, previously only used by `createReserveClient.ts`) into `managementClient.ts`'s shared `signAndSend`, so every management action in this file -- not just the new one -- now surfaces an honestly decoded on-chain error instead of a raw blob. Also removed the remaining developer-facing UI-copy leaks across `ManageDTR.tsx` ("(real DevNet tx)" suffixes, raw snake_case instruction names shown as visible text or toast labels, "Phase E" roadmap jargon) and added a new durable CLAUDE.md rule ("Interface Copy Standards") requiring plain-language UI copy going forward. **330/330 offline tests passing** (317 prior + 13 new), `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint` (exit 0), full `npm run build` all clean. **Not live-verified**: no browser-automation/wallet tool is available in this environment (same documented gap as every prior pass) -- `executeSubmitRebalance`'s real batched signed transaction was not exercised against live DevNet. Verification for this pass is offline unit tests of `applySliderWeightChange`, full typecheck/lint/build, and direct code-path reading confirming no `runOnChainAction`/`signAndSend`/`wallet.signTransaction` call exists in the redesigned branch outside the Submit Rebalance handler (and the pre-existing, separately-labeled Fund/Remove maintenance actions). **Committed, pushed, and deployed**: two commits pushed to `main` (`8519870` the redesign itself, `e90efd1` a doc-status correction), then `vercel --prod` deployed `e90efd1` live as `dpl_9KEt7maJoTBnKfYCM8vncCUKmNiR`, aliased to `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`www.strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun.vercel.app` (confirmed via `vercel inspect`). Post-deploy: both custom domains return `200`, `rpc-proxy`/`landing-stats` return healthy real data, and `vercel logs --since 5m` showed real live traffic with zero errors. See DEC-0084.

- **2026-08-11 (Tue), discovery/indexing reliability + resume-registration + sendTransaction throttle pass (DEC-0082).** Traced live-reported "Portfolio Holdings frequently stuck on Syncing holdings.../DevNet unreachable" and "Reserve creation frequently fails at Step 1/2, then the Reserve appears anyway after waiting/refreshing or briefly shows Reserve Not Found" to three real bugs -- NOT to the resumable-deployment/ambiguous-transaction-reconciliation system (createReserveClient.ts/rpcResilience.ts), which was re-verified already correct and left untouched. **(1)** `packages/sdk/src/discovery.ts`'s `discoverAllReserves` fetched every candidate Reserve account SEQUENTIALLY (already ~37 reserveIds) with the top-level per-reserve fetch carrying NO retry at all -- now batched via Anchor's `fetchMultiple`/`getMultipleAccountsInfo` (chunked, new `chunkArray`) and retried per chunk; a live timing probe against the public DevNet endpoint resolved 36/37 candidate reserveIds in 652ms with 0 issues, moments after a separate, unrelated verification script logged dozens of 429s against the SAME endpoint under its own per-account pass. **(2)** `CreateDTR.tsx`'s `handleResumeDeployment` never registered a resumed Reserve into the `dtrs` store (unlike the fresh-creation success path), so navigating straight to the Reserve page after a successful Resume hit a stale, empty local store. **(3)** `resolveDtrPageState` treated "not yet in the local store" identically to "genuinely doesn't exist," with no discovery-status awareness -- the root cause that actually surfaced bugs #1/#2 as a dead-end "Reserve Not Found" page; `DTRDetail.tsx`/`ManageDTR.tsx` now show a "Verifying on DevNet..." panel (new `indexing` `DtrPageState`, plus one bounded direct on-chain existence check for the rarer already-completed-a-pass case) instead. Also added a dedicated, GLOBAL (not per-IP) `sendTransaction` throttle in `api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts`, conservatively under Helius's paid-plan 5 sendTransaction/sec cap (a shared budget the existing per-IP throttle didn't protect). 19 new offline tests (`tests/phase_discovery_reliability.ts`) plus 1 new/5 updated in `tests/phase_reserve_eligibility.ts` bring the combined suite to **299/299 passing**; `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint` (exit 0), full `npm run build` all clean. **Live-verified (read-only)**: the discovery timing/correctness probe above, against live DevNet, zero transactions sent. **Not verified**: no browser/Phantom click-through was possible (no browser-automation tool in this environment, consistent with every prior pass) -- offline tests plus the live read-only probe are the full extent of verification this pass. Separately, `mergeDiscoveredReserves` (`src/merge/lib/onChainReserve.ts`) only ever excluded a FULLY-resolved-but-unsupported Reserve, never an UNDER-resolved one (a deliberate anti-false-positive design for a transient RPC hiccup that never accounted for a PERMANENT hint-list gap) -- this is why the 9 broken Reserves leaked into the public catalogue instead of being cleanly excluded, and `api/devnet/landing-stats.ts` independently duplicated a near-identical, equally incomplete version of the same filter inline. **Fixed with one new canonical, fail-closed eligibility function** (`packages/sdk/src/reserveEligibility.ts`, `evaluateReserveEligibility`) checking asset-count match, supported-mint composition, permitted lifecycle status, non-zero seeded supply, and the existing `HIDDEN_RESERVE_ADDRESSES` registry -- now the ONLY place this decision is made, called identically from `mergeDiscoveredReserves` (Discover/Featured/Portfolio/Manage, via the frontend store) and `api/devnet/landing-stats.ts` (public Reserve counts), so they can never silently disagree again. 19, 20, 21, 24 -- wrapped-SOL compositions with no genuine devUSDC Buy/Sell route, or never-seeded zero-supply abandoned creations), **10 functional, retained as-is with no code change needed** (reserveId 17 does not exist on-chain at all -- consistent with a completed `close_reserve` reclaiming the account, not a bug). **Direct-link quarantine**: `DTRDetail.tsx` now shows "This legacy - **2026-08-05 (Wed), visual-regression restoration for Reserve controls (later the same day, DEC-0079).** The pass immediately below (DEC-0078) introduced a real, user-visible regression: every merge-scoped control -- the Price History range buttons, all 16 information-tooltip triggers, the Buy/Sell tabs, and the 25/50/75/Max percentage buttons -- rendered with raw user-agent button chrome (gray fills, `outset` bevels, inset shadows, square corners). **This was the second regression in the same `button {}` reset in src/index.css**, and the cause was the fix from the first: DEC-0078 scoped that reset away from `.merge-scope` to stop it clobbering Tailwind utilities, but `src/merge/merge.css` deliberately skips Tailwind's Preflight, so that global reset was the ONLY native-appearance reset those controls had. Radix's `TooltipTrigger` (a completely class-less `<button>`) and shadcn's inactive `TabsTrigger` (which styles only its ACTIVE state) had nothing else standing between them and native chrome, which is why the info icons and inactive Buy/Sell tab looked worst. **Correct fix, replacing both prior attempts:** keep the reset applying document-wide but move it into `@layer base`, and declare in index.css the same cascade-layer order merge.css already declares -- Tailwind's utilities live in the last (`utilities`) layer, so they now cleanly override the reset while any control lacking such a utility still gets native chrome stripped. Extended it to mirror Preflight's own button handling (`appearance: none` kills the widget bevel; `border-width: 0` + `border-style: solid` stops Tailwind's border widths inheriting the UA's `outset` style). Also centralised the 16 duplicated `<TooltipTrigger><Info/></TooltipTrigger>` sites into one accessible `InfoTip` component (circular, `aria-label`led, design-system border and focus ring -- fixing a real missing-accessible-name gap, not just the look), and restyled the range buttons and percentage chips onto existing tokens (purple `--primary` selected, lavender `--border` inactive, `--ring` keyboard-only focus, border present in both states so hover never shifts layout). **Chart functionality from DEC-0078 is fully preserved** -- `buildLineSeries`, the centralised fallback, time windows, the 7d default (now an exported constant so a test can pin it), range switching, tooltips and axes are untouched, confirmed by grepping the diff for every chart identifier (zero hits). 12 new tests bring the suite to **242/242**, 4 of which pin the CSS reset contract itself (layer order, reset inside `@layer base`, not scoped away from `.merge-scope`, and the specific native properties neutralised) precisely because component-level tests missed this class of bug twice. `tsc -b --force`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `oxlint` (exit 0), `vite build` all clean. Verified in the BUILT and then the DEPLOYED CSS that the reset ships inside `@layer base` ahead of the utilities layer and that no `button:not(.merge-scope *)` rule remains. **No browser-based visual verification was possible** (no browser-automation tool in this environment -- a standing gap); a human visual pass at desktop and mobile widths remains outstanding and is not claimed as done. No changes to the separate `ssr.fun`/`ssr-fun-final` project, domain, or repository.
- **2026-08-05 (Wed), Reserve charts, Featured cards, and Helius Buy-flow corrective pass (DEC-0078).** Corrects a wrong conclusion from the very next entry below: 2026-08-04's pass claimed the Price History range-selector buttons were "already correctly fixed by an earlier pass" based on reading the JSX alone -- that was wrong. **Real root cause, found by tracing the actual CSS cascade, not just the component code**: a global, UNLAYERED `button {}` reset in `src/index.css` (padding: 0, background: none, border: none) always beats Tailwind's own LAYERED utility classes inside `.merge-scope` pages regardless of specificity -- this exact codebase already has one documented prior example of the identical bug class (`.container:not(.merge-scope *)`, same file) but the `button` reset never got the same exclusion. Fixed by adding it. Extracted the range selector into its own small, dependency-free `ChartTimeframeSelector` component (added explicit gap/wrap/focus-visible styling as defense in depth, changed the default from 24h to 7d) specifically so a real regression test could render it (via `react-dom/server`) and assert the actual output contains 10 separate `<button>` elements, not one concatenated control -- this is the literal test the button-count claim needed and the prior pass never had. **Price History chart now always renders when a Reserve has a valid current NAV**: `calculations.ts`'s `buildLineSeries` redesigned into the single centralized fallback helper shared by both `DTRDetail`'s chart and Featured Reserves' sparkline -- genuine history renders as before; fewer than 2 real points now renders a client-side-only flatline (never persisted) anchored to the real current NAV (or the one real point's own value if exactly one exists) instead of an "Insufficient price history" empty state; truly nothing valid (no history AND no valid NAV) shows an honest "Price unavailable" state instead of a fabricated $0 line. **Featured Reserve cards' "excessive empty space" had the same root cause** (the sparkline component returns nothing below 2 points, which was the common case pre-fix) -- fixed as a side effect of the same centralized fallback, plus `reserveCardProps.ts` gained the same NaN-on-zero-NAV guard DEC-0074 already added to `DTRDetail.tsx`, and `selectFeaturedReserves` now also requires `assetsResolvedFully`, `isReserveTradable`, and a real (non-"Unnamed Reserve") name. **Traced and fixed the false "DevNet RPC congested" Buy failure to two real bugs, not a Helius-routing misconfiguration** (confirmed via `vercel env ls production`: `VITE_SOLANA_RPC_URL` is correctly unset, so the browser does route through `/api/devnet/rpc-proxy` -> Helius as designed): `swap-sign.ts`'s catch-all checked `isRateLimitError(e)` BEFORE `describeUnknownSignerError(e, ...)`, so a real build/signer error could be silently relabeled as congestion; separately, `isRateLimitError` did a bare `.includes("429")` substring check that could false-positive on a base58 pubkey coincidentally containing that digit run. Both fixed (reordered classification; `isRateLimitError` now uses a word-boundary `\b429\b` regex). `zapClient.ts`'s non-JSON-response handling no longer unconditionally labels itself "rpc_congested" -- only when the HTTP status is genuinely 429. Added `redactRpcSecrets` as defense-in-depth against ever echoing the configured Helius URL/API key into a client-facing error message or server log. 25 new/updated offline tests bring the combined suite to 230/230 passing; `tsc -b --force`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified** via new `scripts/verify_chart_and_buy_fixes.ts` (8/8 checks, real signed transactions): created a genuinely fresh 100%-devUSDC Reserve (createAndRegister `36AcYALZXK7A8cXVKW1wRwTvzEttHsoUbnyzPRKVkF4LvMPpjFVLiQeNjtM5J37n3s5dW5voNpbquAwq7rL1bcWS`, seed `GVVV4C8CHm6Y11aJHUgpLFLBcH8Vj1sm99cm7pi8pQQnkMSxXJCoptGVp1BcKqN8t3QRzjL2AZvLvEhdauLfxwx`), confirmed a valid real on-chain NAV (1.0), a genuine Buy through the real fixed `swap-sign.ts` handler succeeded (signature `4Bw5uWAq3oa77owUMiQ7JubuPmENGDtgSDNiL63rhFAdcFYt9YN12x6vwPssGyWHY7HcHUQguuSZcGw5rLytDju`, `legSources` correctly `user-devusdc-balance`, zero fabrication), Reserve Token balance genuinely increased (20,000,000 -> 21,990,000 raw), refresh-persistence re-confirmed via a real re-fetch, and a deliberately-provoked genuine `InsufficientFunds` on-chain failure was correctly reported as a plain on-chain failure, never "rpc_congested" -- the same run's own real, organic public-RPC 429s were also observed being correctly retried and resolved, not misreported. **No browser/Phantom click-through was performed** (no browser-automation tool available in this environment, consistent with every prior pass) -- the button/chart/card layer is verified via the new component-rendering tests instead, and the Buy/error-classification layer via the live script above. No changes to the separate `ssr.fun`/`ssr-fun-final` project, domain, or repository.
- **2026-08-04 (Tue), platform-wide DevNet corrective pass (later the same day, DEC-0074 through DEC-0077).** A comprehensive pass covering terminology, discovery/UI bugs, delegate wiring, and a scoped attempt at closing DEC-0054's fabricated-Buy/Sell-legs gap. **Terminology reversed (DEC-0074):** "Reserve" now means the whole basket/product, "reserve assets" its holdings -- explicitly supersedes DEC-0072, which had declined this exact change hours earlier the same day; CLAUDE.md rewritten, user-facing copy swept (headings/buttons/toasts in CreateDTR.tsx/ManageDTR.tsx/Manage.tsx/Home.tsx/DTRDetail.tsx/onChainReserve.ts). **3 live UI bugs fixed:** a NaN%-rendering Prem/Discount stat (`DTRDetail.tsx`'s `premiumDiscount` had no zero-guard on `dtr.nav`, which can be 0 when a Reserve's assets are under-resolved despite nonzero token supply -- now renders "--"); a missing retry on `discovery.ts`'s per-asset reads (root cause of the "N registered, only M resolved" banner appearing under ordinary RPC congestion for fully-supported Reserves -- `withRateLimitRetry` moved into `packages/sdk` so the SDK's own discovery code can share it, not just the frontend); a stale Buy-toast string describing the old pure-devUSDC-only gating rule. Price History/range-selector buttons were investigated and found ALREADY correctly fixed by an earlier pass -- not touched. **Delegate management fully wired end to end (DEC-0075), live-verified with real signatures**: add/update/remove-delegate SDK builders and client functions added; fixed a real placeholder bug (`executeUpdateTargets`/`executeAddReserveAsset`/`executeRemoveReserveAsset` all previously passed the connected wallet's own pubkey as the delegate-account placeholder instead of its real derived PDA, which only ever worked by accident for the root manager); `ManageDTR.tsx`'s gating replaced `isRoot` with a real on-chain permission check for every genuinely delegate-capable action. Live-verified against the persistent Gate-9 fixture Reserve One and its two real existing delegates: a genuinely non-root delegate successfully submitted `update_targets` for the first time ever through this code path (signature `5t11fNo2Zq2TLuf5rWDGv3aWFVkVvdut6xd3gjtD1Mje5wCbmZrriN8RDdNDQSH7DxCZtsAzwA6tj611aQW1Mc8C`); a wrongly-permissioned delegate's attempt was genuinely rejected on-chain with the correct `DelegatePermissionDenied` error; a full add-update-remove cycle against a fresh delegate wallet completed with 3 real signatures. **`ssr_devnet_amm` reworked (WSOL hub -> devUSDC hub) and merged into the working tree (DEC-0076), plus a new `execute_rebalance_leg` instruction designed for `ssr_protocol` (DEC-0077) -- both real source changes, NEITHER BUILT NOR DEPLOYED.** This environment has no cargo/rustc/anchor/solana on PATH at all (confirmed via direct check before writing any Rust, not discovered partway through) -- a harder gap than the SOL-funding/Windows-Developer-Mode blockers already tracked below. Per explicit user decision, proceeded anyway: wrote the Rust/SDK changes as real, reviewable diffs for a future toolchain-equipped session to build/deploy/verify, rather than fabricating a false compile/deploy/test result. **Buy/Sell themselves are UNCHANGED this pass** -- still server-minted, not routed through the (unbuilt) AMM; DEC-0054 remains open. 17 new offline tests (`tests/phase_delegate_wiring.ts` x10, `tests/phase_rebalance_execution.ts` x7; 205/205 total passing); `tsc -b --force`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified**: the delegate-wiring script above (13/13 checks, 5 real transaction signatures) -- see DEC-0075's evidence. **Not live-verified and not possible to verify here:** anything requiring the Rust toolchain (DEC-0076/DEC-0077's program changes) or an actual Phantom wallet approval. No changes to the separate `ssr.fun`/`ssr-fun-final` project, domain, or repository.

Configured `www.ssr.fun` as a real 308 permanent redirect to `ssr.fun` (canonical) via the Vercel API (`PATCH /v9/projects/.../domains/www.ssr.fun`), which had not been set. Final state, all live-verified: `https://ssr.fun` returns 200 with the correct content and a valid Let's Encrypt certificate (`CN=ssr.fun`, verified against the default trust store, no `-k` needed); `http://ssr.fun` -> 308 -> `https://ssr.fun/`; `https://www.ssr.fun` -> 308 -> `https://ssr.fun/` with its own valid certificate. `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`ssr-fun` re-confirmed untouched at every step (identical domain-record timestamps before/after, live `200 OK` health checks). **Not yet done**: the Coming Soon repo isn't pushed to a hosted Git remote (no `gh` CLI/token/credential helper available in this environment) -- see Next Actions. - **2026-08-04 (Tue), resumable Reserve-deployment fix.** A reported DevNet failure (50% SOL / 50% MOCKX Reserve, creates the Reserve account successfully, then fails during "Funding seed assets (DevNet)" with "InstructionError / Custom 6400," and the UI then incorrectly tells the user to create another Reserve) traced first to the reported error code itself: `ssr_protocol`'s entire custom-error range (Anchor numbers them sequentially from 6000) is 6000-6040 (41 variants) -- confirmed against both `errors.rs` and the built IDL -- and the sibling `ssr_devnet_amm` program's range is 6000-6011; 6400 exists in neither and cannot be a genuine custom error from any program deployed in this repo as currently written. The real, reproducible root cause was architectural: `createReserveClient.ts`'s own header comment already documented "Full step-level resumption ... is a natural v1.1 follow-up, not implemented here" -- any failure after create-and-register landed cleared the one persisted pointer to the real, half-built on-chain Reserve and left the user on a blank "Launch a Decentralized Token Reserve" form whose only action creates a genuinely SEPARATE Reserve (a new `reserve_id`, since `ProtocolConfig.reserve_count` had already incremented) -- the same failure mode DEC-0059 had already found real evidence of (the abandoned reserveId 19/20 pair). **Fixed:** new pure decision module `src/merge/lib/createReserveResume.ts` (`determineDeploymentResumePoint`, `computeFundingShortfall`, `isWalletRejectionError`) plus a new `resumeReserveDeploymentOnChain` orchestration function that reads real on-chain state first, classifies it (start-fresh / resume-from-funding / already-complete / asset-count-mismatch -- the last one refuses to auto-resume at all rather than guessing), funds only the genuine per-asset shortfall (idempotent seed-funding, shared by both the fresh and resume paths), and re-checks status immediately before AND after seeding so success is never reported without a fresh confirming read. `CreateDTR.tsx` now shows a dedicated "Resume Deployment" panel instead of dead-ending the user, blocks a fresh submission whenever a pending marker already exists for the wallet (also guards a second-tab/fast-click race, since `localStorage` is shared per-origin), and classifies wallet-popup rejection as its own case (never treated as ambiguous). Also added `packages/sdk/src/errors.ts` (`decodeSsrProtocolError`/`extractCustomErrorCode`/`describeOnChainError`) so any future raw on-chain `Custom(N)` gets decoded against the real deployed IDL instead of ever being guessed at again. `PendingReserveDeploy`'s prior 10-minute staleness expiry was deliberately removed -- a real half-built Reserve stays just as safe to resume an hour or a week later, since every resume decision re-reads authoritative on-chain state regardless of the marker's age; keeping the expiry would have silently reintroduced the same dead-end for any failure the user didn't return to within 10 minutes. See DEC-0072. User-facing terminology was explicitly NOT changed in this pass -- the task's request to use "Reserve"/"reserve assets" for the whole product was declined in favor of this file's own "Mandatory terminology" section (2026-07-29, round 3), per direct instruction. 24 new offline tests (`tests/phase_reserve_deploy_resumability.ts`, covering all 7 required regression scenarios; 188/188 total passing); `tsc -b --force`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified (read-only)**: a temporary script fetched real Reserve id 9 (`GFP9nJQyFWurTkJCEYYkBxjksUQUXLt9i3ZoUDncTy5C`, status Active, 3 registered assets) from the live deployed program, confirming `fetchReserveOnChain`'s new `mintFeeBps`(50)/`tvlFeeBps`(100)/`feeDestination` fields decode correctly against real on-chain data, and confirming `determineDeploymentResumePoint` correctly returns `asset-count-mismatch` given a stale/incomplete candidate-mint list and `already-complete` given the real, full list; a non-existent Reserve address correctly returned `start-fresh`. **No live signed-transaction resume was performed** -- that requires an actual wallet approval popup this environment cannot provide; flagged transparently rather than fabricated (see Risks/Next Actions). No Rust/program code touched; `docs/journey-map/` untouched.
- **2026-08-03 (Mon), tradable-Reserve catalogue + multi-asset Buy/Sell + Price History corrective pass (later the same day).** Added a single shared eligibility function (`packages/sdk/src/tradableAssets.ts`, `isReserveTradable`) restricting every visible/tradable Reserve site-wide to compositions built entirely from the 4 configured DevNet test mints (devUSDC `Djn4aGJ3JTgqGpGdQFkmq73gG8KvkwRswP7pNaouuw4k`, mockX `2KBajm7Xufj8UaFQbKqLquhMRqeqjLZdDuXtoqYkSUgu`, mockY `9ERxFYyuNTMjsduY24MCp1bvUDz7PkhoycjBx76Zh8Lv`, mockZ `GqNfJAmAMBVrYbJ3az38kBBrz8vyYdoFpwYmRBJd6zoS`) -- wired into `mergeDiscoveredReserves` (discovery/merge, so an ineligible Reserve never enters the app's catalogue at all), `api/devnet/landing-stats.ts`, `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`'s asset allowlist, and removed wrapped SOL from `CreateDTR.tsx`'s selectable asset list (a newly-created Reserve can no longer end up holding an unsupported asset and be immediately hidden). **Fixed the "Buy only works for 100% devUSDC Reserves" limitation**: removed `swap-sign.ts`'s server-side rejection of non-pure-devUSDC compositions for `buy-devusdc` -- `buildBuyZapInstructionsDevUsdc` already correctly minted non-devUSDC legs (mockX/Y/Z) directly to the buyer via the swap authority's own mint authority; the rejection was the only thing blocking it. **Added genuine devUSDC-settled Sell for mixed/multi-asset Reserves**: new `buildSellZapInstructionsDevUsdc` (`packages/sdk/src/zapInstructions.ts`) redeems in-kind for real, leaves any genuine devUSDC entitlement with the seller untouched, and converts every other asset's entitlement into freshly-minted devUSDC paid to the seller -- replacing the prior fixed-rate SOL settlement for these compositions (DEC-0054/0065's long-standing open gap) and removing the swap authority's SOL balance as a Sell dependency for this path entirely. **Fixed the Price History range-selector bug**: `buildLineSeries` previously fabricated an identical 2-point flatline for every single range button whenever a Reserve had fewer than 2 real recorded price points (the common case for a freshly-created or lightly-traded Reserve) -- every button LOOKED unresponsive because the output was coincidentally the same regardless of range, not because the buttons themselves were broken. Now reports an explicit `insufficientHistory` state instead, rendered as an honest empty state in `DTRDetail.tsx` rather than a fabricated chart. 18 new offline tests (163/163 total passing); `tsc -b`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `vite build` all clean. **Live-verified** via new `scripts/verify_multi_asset_tradability.ts` against 4 real, persistent on-chain Reserves (100% devUSDC, 100% mockX via the existing TestLo fixture, multi-asset mockX/Y/Z via DevNet Reserve Two, mixed devUSDC+mockX via the Phase C Reserve): real Buy-then-Sell against each with exact vault/supply/NAV deltas confirmed (including correct handling of mockZ's differing 9-decimal mint), explicit cross-Reserve isolation confirmed (untouched Reserves' state re-read byte-identical to their own post-trade snapshots after other Reserves traded), and real holder/volume refresh confirmed across all 4 (2-5 holders, $11.98-$76.98 24h volume each). **New risk surfaced, not fixed**: a 0%-devUSDC Reserve's Buy-then-Sell path lets a user mint real devUSDC for free with no rate limit of its own (see Risks). No manual Phantom UI click-through performed (pre-existing environment gap, no browser-automation tool available); `tests/ssr_protocol.ts` remains blocked (Cargo not on PATH). See `docs/project/ENGINEERING_TIMELINE.md`'s new Fixes section for the dashboard-visible summary.
- **2026-08-03 (Mon), devUSDC Buy signer-crash fix + AUM/holder/volume corrective pass.** Traced a reported `Buy Failed -- unknown signer: Ef7vbQ...` (truncated) against Reserve "123"/ABC (reserveId 20, `7F6VvzW8avFhvGP8sLprc6zSVVTzvgtRAoXiZS5ZpTAy`) precisely: `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`'s `buy-devusdc` action unconditionally called `tx.partialSign(swapAuthority)`, but `buildBuyZapInstructionsDevUsdc` never references the swap authority as an account at all once every leg is genuinely devUSDC-funded (guaranteed for `buy-devusdc`, since a mixed-asset Reserve is already rejected earlier in the same handler) -- web3.js's `Transaction.partialSign` throws exactly `unknown signer: <pubkey>` when asked to sign with a key absent from the transaction's own required-signer list. The truncated pubkey (`Ef7vbQghn7Fc4LzUnyJsvov1f5f9aRSfWksiaSmWpquj`) is the shared DevNet swap-authority/devUSDC-mint-authority keypair (`devnet-fixtures/manager-keypair.json`), not any PDA or a user account -- confirmed via `docs/protocol/DEVNET_FIXTURES.md`/fixture files, not guessed. **This was never specific to Reserve 20 -- every pure-devUSDC Reserve's Buy was broken this same way.** Fixed by making the `partialSign` call conditional on `legSources` actually containing a `devnet-test-asset-faucet` leg; added a new `describeUnknownSignerError`/`describeUnknownSignerMessage` pair (server + client) that maps any future "unknown signer" failure to the account's real role (Reserve, Reserve Token mint, mint/vault authority, DevNet swap adapter, connected wallet) instead of a bare pubkey, wired into `DTRDetail.tsx`'s Buy/Sell error toasts. **Investigated Reserve 20's real on-chain history (`getSignaturesForAddress` + Anchor event decoding, not assumption)**: genuinely seeded with exactly 100 devUSDC -> 100 Reserve Tokens on 2026-07-31, then fully redeemed (100/100, 0 fee) via a real Sell **on 2026-08-03, the same day as this pass** -- so the reported "$0 AUM despite 100 devUSDC inception" was this Reserve's real, currently-empty state, not a formula bug; `mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind`'s `total_supply_before > 0` guard means this specific Reserve can now never accept another Buy (a real, separate, protocol-level limitation, independent of the signer fix). Audited the AUM/NAV/composition pipeline (`onChainReserve.ts`) end to end and found the formula already correct (`aumUsd = vaultBalanceRaw / 10^decimals * priceUsd`, `nav = supply > 0 ? aumUsd/supply : 1`) -- confirmed empirically, not just by inspection, via a full live reproduction on a fresh throwaway Reserve (below). **Fixed a real holder-count double-counting bug**: `api/devnet/landing-stats.ts` summed each Reserve's holder COUNT independently, over-counting any wallet holding more than one Reserve's token; replaced with a new `fetchReserveTokenHolderOwners`/`collectHolderOwners` (SDK) returning the actual owner-address SET per Reserve, unioned across Reserves for a genuinely deduplicated global count, with a `perReserve` breakdown added to the same endpoint's response so the Reserve-detail page and the landing page read the identical source of truth (no competing counting systems). Replaced `DTRDetail.tsx`'s permanent "Holder count not indexed" with real loading/ready/stale/unavailable states sourced from a new shared `src/merge/hooks/useLandingStats.ts` hook (also now used by `Home.tsx`, replacing its own local copy); added a "24h Volume" stat card to the Reserve-detail stats grid (the underlying `fetchReserve24hVolumeUsd` computation was already correct -- excludes seeding, never nets Buy against Sell, timezone-safe via Unix epoch math -- it simply wasn't displayed per-Reserve yet). Added `?force=1` cache-bypass support to `landing-stats.ts` so a confirmed Buy/Sell refetches real numbers immediately rather than waiting out the 60s cache. **Live-verified end-to-end on a fresh throwaway Reserve** (single-asset devUSDC, mirroring "123"/ABC exactly, since Reserve 20 itself can never Buy again): Create `2EV5pyvpyLi2HyfVToAVRZGRyTWqesSiKxTzSqS9DxiUjz2q8ZsuR4ZMECCfguPTBSW4wCarXz4EGeqK6mVEjeBs`, Seed 100 devUSDC `4kUhTxVdF6g1BAgUx1ETnGqjREbqmBQDTupCaHwGK75xrheiRCaEhavEhxVAHJ5CT3SYt5nf4Yrc4XDvFURQqogB` (AUM $100.00/NAV $1.00 confirmed), Buy 10 devUSDC via the fixed `buy-devusdc` path -- required signer list confirmed to contain ONLY the buyer's own pubkey -- `XeeUpsmqMXCbSMNrD9548SauoULRBReabjBKTUrQxUu6VBHtA4Fkz5dGRAP3KLfvy19EbtVJ4WHHGQR3qTNDfB2` (AUM $110.00, NAV $1.000455 -- correctly above $1 from the mint fee accruing to holders), Sell half `62SqZnNrnUK7NKoKU5aPfa1kg1qmq1NQhRRh2a97EQjvUiHG4SaU4pNQGqogho2xjtKqipW8ByhPtKgj3i5iH7VD` (AUM $55.00, **NAV unchanged at $1.000455** -- confirmed economically correct for a proportional zero-fee redemption, not a bug). **Correcting a stale 2026-07-31 risk entry**: local signed-transaction verification does NOT actually require pulling `HELIUS_RPC_URL`/`DEVNET_SWAP_AUTHORITY_SECRET_KEY` from Vercel -- the already-committed `devnet-fixtures/manager-keypair.json` fixture IS the swap-authority/devUSDC-mint-authority keypair and is sufficient on its own (confirmed live-balance 9.23243462 SOL today, healthy, up from the 0.2345 SOL recorded 2026-07-30/31 -- the user has since topped it up). 145/145 existing offline tests still pass (pre-existing, unrelated TS errors in 3 `tests/phase_*.ts` files confirmed via `git stash` to predate this pass, not touched); `tsc -b`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `vite build` all clean. `tsc -p tests/../tsconfig.tests.json` was NOT run against `tests/ssr_protocol.ts` -- confirmed that file genuinely cannot execute in this environment (`anchor.workspace` shells out to `cargo metadata`; `cargo` is not on PATH in this session, a pre-existing environment gap, not introduced here) -- verification instead used this repo's own established `scripts/verify_*.ts` pattern (`scripts/verify_corrective_pass.ts`, new; plus a one-off `scripts/investigate_reserve_history.ts` used only for diagnosis). - **2026-07-31 (Fri), devUSDC Buy/Sell architecture correction (later the same day):** A user-provided architecture review of the Buy flow (with screenshots) found the UI disclosing "This Reserve has no devUSDC leg, so quick-select isn't balance-gated" and that SOL/mockX legs were faucet-funded during a Buy labeled devUSDC-settled. Traced `buildBuyZapInstructionsDevUsdc` precisely: the trader's FULL devUSDC input sizes the entire mint, but only the Reserve's own devUSDC-weighted fraction (if any) is actually debited -- every other leg is fabricated for free by the swap authority in the same transaction; a Reserve with no devUSDC leg had 100% of its mint backed by assets nobody paid for. **Corrected per the authoritative product model (devUSDC is the universal purchasing currency, never required to be a Reserve Asset):** quick-select buttons now always derive from the real devUSDC balance regardless of Reserve composition (removed the "no devUSDC leg" gating entirely); Buy execution is now gated to Reserves backed 100% by devUSDC (`isReservePureDevUsdc`), enforced both client-side and server-side (`api/devnet/swap-sign.ts` now rejects any mixed-asset Reserve for `buy-devusdc`) -- eliminating all hidden faucet/swap-authority funding during Buy. **While tracing the inverse (redemption), found the exact mechanism behind the earlier-logged "3.75 SOL settlement" report (DEC-0054)**: `redeem_reserve_tokens_in_kind` already deposits the redeemer's real proportional entitlement directly into their own wallet -- for a pure-devUSDC Reserve, that IS real devUSDC -- but the existing Sell zap then took it straight back and gave fixed-price synthetic SOL instead. Fixed with a new zero-fabrication `buildRedeemToDevUsdcInstructions` (single-signer, no swap-authority co-signature, no SOL leg) used whenever a Reserve is 100% devUSDC-backed; mixed-composition Sell is unchanged in this pass (still the existing SOL zap, still logged as an open gap). Deliberately did NOT activate the unmerged `ssr_devnet_amm` and did NOT extend the Sell fix to mixed Reserves (a real, likely-safe follow-up identified but out of this session's explicit scope) -- see DEC-0067. 20 new offline tests (`tests/phase_devusdc_buy_architecture_fix.ts` + updates to the prior pass's test file; 145/145 total passing); `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `tsc -p packages/sdk`, `tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.json` all clean. No live signed-transaction verification performed (same local-secrets limitation as the prior pass); a live composition-check script (`scripts/check_reserve_compositions.ts`) was written but blocked by public-RPC rate-limiting this session. No Rust/program code touched; `docs/journey-map/` untouched.

- **2026-07-31 (Fri), focused corrective pass covering 9 live-testing issues (later the same day):** Fixed the Buy quick-select (25/50/75%/Max) buttons falling back to a hardcoded 100 instead of the trader's real devUSDC balance when a Reserve has no devUSDC leg -- now disables the buttons in that case with an honest reason, and derives Max from the real balance everywhere else (DEC-0060).
