- **2026-07-30 (Thu), Helius RPC integration + Buy 429/"insufficient SOL" diagnosis pass (later the same day):** Adopted Helius as the primary DevNet RPC provider everywhere (DEC-0055): every `api/devnet/*.ts` handler now resolves its Connection through a new shared `resolveRpcUrl()` (Helius when `HELIUS_RPC_URL` is set, falling back to the prior public-endpoint default) instead of each duplicating its own literal default; the browser's Connection is now pointed, in production/preview builds only, at a new narrow server-side proxy (`api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts`) instead of ever receiving a raw provider URL/API key -- the proxy allowlists only the 9 JSON-RPC methods this app's own Connection genuinely calls (confirmed by an exhaustive grep, including Anchor/spl-token's internal `getAccountInfo` dependency), validates/bounds every payload, applies a best-effort per-IP throttle, and falls back once to the public endpoint on a genuine transport failure -- never on a 429, and never for `sendTransaction` (a send is never retried against a second endpoint; reconciliation is always by signature). `HELIUS_RPC_URL` was added directly to the linked Vercel project via `vercel env add` (Production/Preview/Development), never committed to any tracked file. **Diagnosed the reported 50-devUSDC Buy failures** by tracing the exact unsigned transaction end-to-end: devUSDC's mint/decimals (6, correctly used throughout -- no double-scaling, no lamports confusion), every instruction, the fee payer (always the connected wallet, confirmed), and every lamport transfer. Found two real, confirmed bugs, both unrelated to decimals: (1) `swap-sign.ts`'s `fetchReserveOnChain` call sat OUTSIDE its own try/catch, so a 429 there (confirmed live: the public endpoint returned the exact raw `"429 Connection rate limits exceeded"` string this session while investigating) escaped as an unhandled exception instead of a formatted error -- fixed by moving it inside the try block and wrapping every RPC read in this endpoint (previously un-retried, unlike the frontend's own reads) in the existing `withRateLimitRetry` helper (DEC-0056); (2) Buy's wrapped-SOL leg (when a Reserve has one) and 100% of Sell's payout are funded from the DevNet swap authority's OWN SOL balance, never the user's -- with no sufficiency check, a low swap-authority balance produced a transaction Phantom's own preflight simulation would reject for a signer OTHER than the connected wallet, which Phantom's generic UI reports as "insufficient SOL" with no way to tell it apart from the user's own balance. A new `assertSwapAuthorityHasSol` check now fails closed with an honest, correctly-attributed error before ever returning such a transaction (DEC-0056). Also migrated the two remaining `connection.confirmTransaction` (websocket-based) call sites (`managementClient.ts`, `createReserveClient.ts`) to the already-proven bounded `confirmSignatureBounded` (DEC-0057) -- required since the HTTP-only proxy has no persistent websocket to subscribe through. Client-side: `zapClient.ts`'s error now carries a structured `code` (`rpc_congested`/`swap_authority_low_sol`/`build_failed`); `DTRDetail.tsx`'s Buy/Sell show distinct, accurate messages per code and pre-check the trader's own real devUSDC/Reserve-Token balance before any network call. 17 new offline tests (`tests/phase_helius_rpc_and_buy_fix.ts`; 100/100 total passing). `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint`, `tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.json` all clean. **Live-verified**: a new `scripts/verify_helius_buy_fix.ts` ran the exact `executeBuyZapDevUsdc` function DTRDetail.tsx calls, genuinely through the Helius endpoint, buying 50 devUSDC (the exact reported amount) against the Phase C Reserve -- signature `hGDWXhLtLFHrsJssUd7YzQxXcyY4oQJdqVjTxLVMoNsrYCqHMdxJK1zcDNHvJTvunYkJr13j5Acr1fQacTLj1Sa`, trader SOL 0.050000 -> 0.045911 (only 0.004089 SOL spent on ATA rent + network fee -- confirms the connected wallet's own SOL need is tiny and was never the real constraint), devUSDC 100,000,000 -> 65,000,000 raw (35 devUSDC spent on the 70%-weighted leg, exactly as expected -- no scaling bug), Reserve Token 0 -> 49,595,879 raw minted, `legSources` correct. The same run also read the swap authority's REAL live balance: **0.2345 SOL** -- low enough that the new sufficiency check is not hypothetical; flagged as an operational follow-up (see Risks), not fixed by this pass (no protocol/trade-mechanics change). No Rust/program code touched; `docs/journey-map/` untouched throughout.

Not a regression or a known defect: Sell shares the identical, already offline-tested `signSubmitAndConfirm`/`confirmSignatureBounded` code path Buy uses (same function in `zapClient.ts`), and Sell has its own separate live evidence from earlier passes (DEC-0032, DEC-0043). Flagged transparently as still-open rather than claimed complete. No Rust/program code touched.
- **2026-07-29 (Wed), Buy/confirmation RPC-resilience pass (later the same day):** A follow-up user report ("why are buys hitting connection rate limits?") traced to a real contention bug the prior corrective pass hadn't covered: `RealReserveSync.tsx`'s discovery poll ran an unconditional `setInterval(15s)` forever (dozens of sequential RPC calls -- discovery + per-Reserve delegate lookups + balance reads -- every 15 seconds regardless of what else was happening), `DTRDetail.tsx` added its own uncached balance reads on mount and after every trade, and a Buy's own confirmation used `connection.confirmTransaction`'s websocket-subscription strategy, which was observed live throwing `ws error: Unexpected server response: 429` under congestion. **Fix, centralized in a new `src/merge/lib/rpcResilience.ts`** (reused by every caller instead of each inventing its own retry logic): `nextPollDelay` (adaptive backoff, doubles+jitters on a real 429 capped at 120s, steps back down gradually on success) replaces the fixed interval; `getCached`/`invalidateCached(Prefix)` (bounded-TTL cache + in-flight de-dup) replaces every ad hoc balance/discovery cache; `withReadConcurrencyLimit` caps concurrent reads at 4; `confirmSignatureBounded` replaces the websocket-based confirmation with bounded, retryable `getSignatureStatuses` polling that treats a 429 mid-poll as inconclusive (never a failure) and only reports `failed`/`expired` on a genuine on-chain answer, throwing a new `AmbiguousConfirmationError` (carrying the real signature) when bounded polling is exhausted with no definitive answer -- never fabricating success or failure. `RealReserveSync.tsx` now has an overlap guard (a slow pass is never joined by a second) and defers entirely while a new `useAppStore.txInFlight` flag (set for the duration of any Buy/Sell) is true, so the background poll never races a trade's own RPC traffic. `DTRDetail.tsx`'s Buy/Sell each track a `TxPhase` state machine (`canSubmitNewTransaction` gates re-submission; `txPhaseLabel` drives the exact required UX strings -- "Preparing transaction", "Waiting for wallet approval", "Submitted -- confirming on DevNet", "DevNet RPC is temporarily busy -- your transaction is still being verified"), catches `AmbiguousConfirmationError` to run a one-shot `reconcileByBalanceChange` re-check (a fresh, uncached real balance read -- reports confirmed only if the balance genuinely moved as expected) and shows a persistent pending-verification banner with the signature/Explorer link and a manual "Check status" retry otherwise; `refreshRealReserveNow` is unchanged in scope (still only the one Reserve that traded) but now invalidates+repopulates the shared cache instead of reading around it. `zapClient.ts`'s `signSubmitAndConfirm` still submits exactly once (never auto-retried) and now surfaces the signature via `onProgress` the instant `sendRawTransaction` succeeds, before confirmation even starts; `requestSignedZapTransaction` also learned to detect a rate limit from the response body's own error text, not just the HTTP status, after live testing surfaced that `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`'s catch-all normalizes every internal error (including a genuine upstream 429 hit while building the transaction) to HTTP 500 -- a real, narrow bug in the SAME feature, fixed in the same file rather than left as a silent verification-blocker. 27 new offline tests (`tests/phase_rpc_resilience.ts`; 71/71 total passing across all phase test files); `npx tsc -b`, `npx tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.json`, `npx tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json`, `npx vite build`, `npx oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified**: `scripts/verify_rpc_resilience_buy.ts` ran the exact `executeBuyZapDevUsdc` function DTRDetail.tsx calls (a disposable trader wallet standing in for a connected wallet, the closest available production-equivalent path -- no browser-automation tool exists in this environment) against the live Phase C devUSDC Reserve; after several earlier attempts were legitimately blocked by the same sustained public-RPC congestion this pass fixes (each attempt getting further, informing the fixes above), a clean run confirmed a real Buy end-to-end: signature `61xiFz6QwFmVoybDY7yeHF1bcbvukiXMJDN8UCPJa1FVUfFQy7HwV7hFtnEBQivhwp9PHNcAuW1UGMLPD3LzEiB7`, devUSDC balance 20,000,000 -> 16,500,000 raw (3.5 devUSDC genuinely spent on the 70%-weighted leg), Reserve Token balance 0 -> 4,975,956 raw minted, `legSources` correctly reporting the devUSDC leg as `user-devusdc-balance` and the mockX leg as `devnet-test-asset-faucet`, `onProgress` firing `awaiting-wallet` then `submitted` exactly as designed, final status `confirmed`. No Rust/program code touched this pass.

direct proportional redemption remains the canonical, only Sell path, unchanged. **Root cause of the 429 launch failure:** `estimateCreateReserveCost` fired an uncached, undebounced 4-call rent-exemption RPC burst on every keystroke/slider-tick in the Create-Reserve form; fixed with a shared 10-minute rent-constant cache + in-flight request de-duplication (`getRentConstants`), a bounded exponential-backoff-with-jitter wrapper for genuine 429s only (`withRateLimitRetry`/`isRateLimitError`), a 400ms debounce on the cost-estimate effect, and an ambiguous-submission guard in `CreateDTR.tsx` that reads `ProtocolConfig.reserveCount` before and after a failed launch to distinguish "definitely nothing submitted" (safe to retry, shown verbatim) from "status unclear, do not retry" (shown instead of inviting a duplicate launch) -- the failed launch that prompted this pass left no partial on-chain state (confirmed: it failed during cost estimation, before any transaction was built or signed). **Reserve inventory:** confirmed 5 genuine, already-existing, actively-healthy on-chain Reserves already covered Stage 4's requirement with excellent compositional diversity (mockX/Y/Z + devUSDC + wrapped SOL all represented) -- no new Reserve needed to be created; see Recently Completed for the full list with addresses. Added 12 new offline tests (`tests/phase_data_integrity.ts`; 44/44 total passing), all confirmed clean: `npx tsc -b`, `npx tsc -p scripts/tsconfig.json --noEmit`, `npx tsc -p api/devnet/tsconfig.json --noEmit`, `npx vite build`, `npx oxlint`. **Live-verification caveat:** `scripts/verify_data_integrity_pass.ts` was run repeatedly against live DevNet; the public RPC endpoint was under severe, sustained congestion throughout this pass (the exact `429`/"Too many requests" condition being fixed), which itself validates the bug but also limited how much of the live chain could be re-confirmed in one run -- see Technical Health and Risks for exactly what was and wasn't completed live. No Rust/program code was touched this pass.

## Recently Completed
- **Mainnet authority-model code blockers closed: two independent Protocol Admins + working protocol-wide pause, built and offline-verified (2026-08-18, DEC-0112/DEC-0113/DEC-0114).** DEC-0112 confirmed the final temporary Mainnet authority/Treasury structure and independently verified the Squads Multisig Account/Treasury vault on-chain via real Mainnet RPC calls (threshold=2, exact 3-member match, vault PDA re-derived and confirmed off-curve) -- read-only, no funds moved, no keys touched. That investigation found two real pre-Mainnet code gaps: `ProtocolConfig` supported only one admin wallet, and no instruction anywhere could ever set the documented global emergency pause. DEC-0113 fixed both: added `ProtocolConfig.admin_2` + `is_admin()` (both admins act independently, neither gains any path to Reserve vault custody -- unchanged from before), and added `set_protocol_paused`. DEC-0114 closed out full validation: fixed an `anchor build` toolchain panic (worked around via the already-documented direct `cargo-build-sbf` command), fixed a PATH-ordering bug that pointed the GNU linker at the wrong (space-containing) MinGW install instead of the already-correct `C:\devtools\mingw64`, and regenerated `packages/sdk/idl/*` from the exact current source using Anchor's own `convertIdlToCamelCase` -- which surfaced and fixed a real, pre-existing IDL/source drift (the committed IDL was missing the entire `execute_rebalance_leg` instruction, whose previously-documented compile error, DEC-0077/DEC-0092, no longer reproduces -- unrelated dependency change, not investigated further). Result: `cargo-build-sbf` clean (0 warnings), `cargo test -p ssr_protocol` 7/7, `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx oxlint` clean, offline suite 596/596 (`tests/phase_*.ts`), `npm run build` clean. Not yet built via a live validator or deployed anywhere -- `solana-test-validator` was re-confirmed still blocked by the same pre-existing Windows privilege gap (see Blockers), and no Mainnet program exists yet to exercise this code against.

- **Resumable Reserve-deployment fix (2026-08-04).** A DevNet deployment failure past create-and-register (reported as "InstructionError / Custom 6400" during seed-funding) no longer strands the user -- confirmed the reported error code doesn't exist in either deployed program's custom-error range, and fixed the real root cause: no step-level resume path existed at all, so any failure after the Reserve account was created led to a dead-end that funneled the user into creating a genuinely separate, duplicate Reserve. Deployment now reads real on-chain state, detects the exact incomplete step, and resumes safely (idempotent seed-funding, never re-registers assets, never re-seeds an already-Active Reserve, refuses to auto-resume on a state mismatch). 24 new tests (188/188 total); live read-only DevNet verification confirmed the resume-decision logic against a real deployed Reserve. See DEC-0072.

Fixed a real global-holder-count double-counting bug in `api/devnet/landing-stats.ts` (summed per-Reserve counts instead of deduplicating owner addresses across Reserves); added a `perReserve` breakdown so the Reserve-detail page's real holder count and the landing page's global count share one source of truth; replaced the permanent "Holder count not indexed" placeholder with real loading/ready/stale/unavailable states; added a "24h Volume" stat to the Reserve-detail page (the underlying calculation was already correct, just not displayed per-Reserve). 145/145 offline tests still pass; `tsc -b`/`vite build`/`tsc -p api/devnet` all clean. **Live-verified** via a new `scripts/verify_corrective_pass.ts`: Create `2EV5pyv...`, Seed `4kUhTxV...` (AUM $100.00), Buy `XeeUpsm...` (AUM $110.00, single required signer confirmed), Sell `62SqZnN...` (AUM $55.00, NAV unchanged at $1.000455 -- confirmed economically correct for a zero-fee proportional redemption). Caught and fixed two real bugs via live pre-deploy testing: a silent per-Reserve zero-out under rate-limiting (fixed with bounded retry), and an event-name-casing mismatch (Anchor's `EventParser` uses lowercase-first camelCase, not the IDL's PascalCase) that made volume always compute as 0 until caught by decoding a known real transaction directly. 11 new offline tests (111/111 total); all typechecks/build/lint clean. **Live-verified** via new `scripts/verify_landing_stats.ts`: 22 genuine holders, $1,121.43 genuine 24h volume, 17 Reserves counted. No Rust/program code touched.
- **Helius RPC integration + Buy 429/"insufficient SOL" diagnosis pass (2026-07-30).** Helius is now the primary DevNet RPC provider (DEC-0055): every `api/devnet/*.ts` handler resolves its Connection through a new shared `resolveRpcUrl()`, and the browser's Connection is pointed (production/preview builds only) at a new narrow, method-allowlisted, payload-validated server-side proxy (`api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts`) instead of ever holding a raw provider URL/API key -- `HELIUS_RPC_URL` was added to the linked Vercel project via `vercel env add`, never committed. Diagnosed the reported 50-devUSDC Buy failures: devUSDC's decimals/scaling/fee-payer were all already correct; the real causes were (1) `swap-sign.ts`'s `fetchReserveOnChain` call sitting outside its own try/catch, letting a raw 429 escape unformatted (confirmed live -- the public endpoint returned that exact string this session), and (2) no sufficiency check on the swap authority's own SOL balance, which funds Buy's wrapped-SOL leg and 100% of Sell's payout -- a low balance there produced a transaction Phantom's simulation rejects for a signer OTHER than the connected wallet, misreported generically as "insufficient SOL" (DEC-0056). Both fixed; also migrated the two remaining websocket-based `connection.confirmTransaction` call sites to the already-proven bounded `confirmSignatureBounded` (DEC-0057), required for the new HTTP-only proxy. 17 new offline tests (100/100 total); `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified** via new `scripts/verify_helius_buy_fix.ts`: a real 50-devUSDC Buy genuinely through Helius -- signature `hGDWXhLtLFHrsJssUd7YzQxXcyY4oQJdqVjTxLVMoNsrYCqHMdxJK1zcDNHvJTvunYkJr13j5Acr1fQacTLj1Sa`, trader SOL spent 0.004089 (fee+rent only), devUSDC 100,000,000 -> 65,000,000 raw, Reserve Token 0 -> 49,595,879 raw minted. Also read the swap authority's real live balance: **0.2345 SOL** -- confirming the new check is not hypothetical (see Risks). No Rust/program code touched.
- **Landing-page/wallet/DevNet-token-model correction pass (2026-07-30).** Featured Reserves + KPI strip re-sourced onto the same on-chain-verified discovery store Discover Reserves uses (new shared `src/merge/lib/reserveCardProps.ts`), fixing a dead Featured-card link and dropping two KPI tiles with no genuine data source. Portfolio's mockX/Y/Z faucet-like "Get DevNet Test Assets" button removed (DEC-0053) without touching the genuine Reserve-creation seeding path that shares its API endpoint. Landing page's long marketing copy/fee-table replaced with a mandated "How It Works" section; remaining "Browse Reserves" CTAs fixed to "Discover Reserves". Wallet chip now opens a real dropdown panel (new `src/components/WalletPanel.tsx`) instead of disconnecting on click, showing genuine SOL/devUSDC/Reserve-Token balances via the existing RPC-resilience cache. Central audit finding -- Buy still mints non-devUSDC legs from the swap authority rather than the user's holdings, and Sell always pays SOL, not devUSDC -- deliberately left open rather than patched with a workaround; see DEC-0054. Also fixed `package.json`'s `test:program` script, which pointed at the wrong tsconfig and made the entire offline test suite fail to even start. 9 new offline tests (83/83 total); `tsc -b`, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. No Rust/program code touched.
- **Honest token-supply reads under transient RPC failure (2026-07-30).** Fixed `packages/sdk/src/readOnly.ts`'s `fetchReserveOnChain` silently reporting a real Reserve's supply as `"0"` on a transient `getTokenSupply` RPC failure (indistinguishable from a genuinely-unseeded Reserve, and observed to make a real Buy fail with a misleading error) -- new bounded rate-limit-only retry (`getTokenSupplyWithRetry`) fixes it without changing the function's existing null-safety contract. 3 new offline tests (74/74 total); all typechecks/build/lint clean. A follow-up live Sell verification (closing the one gap the prior pass's Buy-only run left open) could not complete today after 5 attempts, each blocked by a different symptom of markedly worse public DevNet RPC congestion than the day before -- not a known defect (Sell shares Buy's already-verified code path and has separate earlier live evidence). No Rust/program code touched.
- **Buy/confirmation RPC-resilience pass (2026-07-29).** Fixed the real contention between `RealReserveSync.tsx`'s background discovery poll, `DTRDetail.tsx`'s balance reads, and Buy/Sell's own submission+confirmation -- the actual answer to "why are buys hitting rate limits" after the prior pass only addressed Create-Reserve's launch burst. New shared `src/merge/lib/rpcResilience.ts`: adaptive poll backoff (`nextPollDelay`), cache+dedup (`getCached`/`invalidateCached(Prefix)`), a 4-concurrent-read cap (`withReadConcurrencyLimit`), and bounded polling-based confirmation (`confirmSignatureBounded`, replacing the websocket-based `confirmTransaction` observed live throwing `ws error: Unexpected server response: 429`) that throws `AmbiguousConfirmationError` (never a fabricated result) when polling is exhausted without a definitive on-chain answer. `RealReserveSync` gained an overlap guard and pauses entirely while a new `useAppStore.txInFlight` flag is true; `DTRDetail`'s Buy/Sell each track a `TxPhase` state machine driving the required UX strings, reconcile an ambiguous outcome via one real (uncached) balance re-check, and show a persistent signature/Explorer-link pending-verification banner with manual "Check status" otherwise -- never inviting a duplicate submission while the first is unresolved. Also fixed a real bug surfaced only by live testing: `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`'s catch-all normalizes every internal error to HTTP 500, including a genuine upstream 429 -- `zapClient.ts` now also detects a rate limit from the response body's own error text, not just the HTTP status. 71/71 offline tests pass (27 new, `tests/phase_rpc_resilience.ts`); `tsc -b`, both `scripts`/`api` tsconfigs, `vite build`, `oxlint` all clean. **Live-verified**: `scripts/verify_rpc_resilience_buy.ts` called the exact `executeBuyZapDevUsdc` function DTRDetail.tsx uses (closest available production-equivalent path -- no browser-automation tool exists in this environment) against the live Phase C devUSDC Reserve; after several attempts were legitimately blocked by the same sustained public-RPC congestion this pass targets (each getting further and directly informing the fixes above), a clean run produced a real confirmed Buy -- signature `61xiFz6QwFmVoybDY7yeHF1bcbvukiXMJDN8UCPJa1FVUfFQy7HwV7hFtnEBQivhwp9PHNcAuW1UGMLPD3LzEiB7`, devUSDC 20,000,000 -> 16,500,000 raw, Reserve Token 0 -> 4,975,956 raw, `legSources` and `onProgress` phases exactly as designed. No Rust/program code touched. `docs/journey-map/` untouched throughout.
- **Corrective DevNet data-integrity + Reserve-launch pass (2026-07-29).** Purged the fictional/mock demo catalog and every simulated Buy/Sell/wallet-balance path (root cause of the reported "25,000 USDC -> 1,125 BLUE" incident -- confirmed zero on-chain activity, ever); the app now only discovers and displays genuine on-chain DevNet Reserves, fail-closed. devUSDC (`Djn4aGJ3JTgqGpGdQFkmq73gG8KvkwRswP7pNaouuw4k`) is now the default DevNet settlement asset for Buy/mint, with a real transfer for the devUSDC leg and honest faucet-funded labeling for any other leg. Fixed the reported `429` Reserve-launch failure at its root (uncached/undebounced rent-cost RPC burst) with caching, de-duplication, bounded backoff, debouncing, and an ambiguous-submission guard against duplicate launches. Confirmed 5 genuine, distinct, already-healthy on-chain Reserves satisfy Stage 4's requirement (no new Reserve needed): **DevNet Reserve One** (`GFP9nJQyFWurTkJCEYYkBxjksUQUXLt9i3ZoUDncTy5C`, mockX 60%/mockY 40% at fixture creation, live-confirmed with 3 registered assets after later Phase F/G composition testing added a 3rd), **DevNet Reserve Two** (`H1U22fK3fMfsmz1WirJ4H63xBDcTEHgXjtSzw73tEfcJ`, mockX 40%/mockY 30%/mockZ 30%, 3 assets), **StrategicSolReserve "TestLo"** (`Hj8uifcUHAmTpwySQJgfo4F6B8Y68X2b48BmTKv89xSX`, 100% mockX, reserve_id 13, the real user-created Reserve found by Phase A discovery), **Phase C devUSDC Reserve** (`HAaoBxSVAnaxEusxxYUnPpAJAyjRzti4zuqxrLWYS4VE`, 70% devUSDC/30% mockX), and the **E2E corrective-pass Reserve** (`BuHRWKzzXQXhjL3WCsmHTT7qDooh2437DvXuxyExpiWg`, from DEC-0032's original fresh-Reserve verification, 2 assets) -- all 5 individually reconfirmed live and `active` via direct `fetchNullable` account reads on 2026-07-29 despite severe same-day public-RPC congestion. 44/44 offline tests pass (12 new); `tsc -b`, both `scripts`/`api` tsconfigs, `vite build`, and `oxlint` all clean. Live end-to-end Buy/Sell/fresh-launch verification through the exact UI code path was attempted repeatedly against live DevNet but could not be driven to full completion in one run due to sustained public RPC congestion (the same condition this pass fixes) -- partial live evidence obtained instead (see Technical Health): all 5 Reserves reconfirmed, and a real devUSDC faucet-funding transaction landed with a real signature before the run's buy-submission step itself hit repeated 429s and aborted safely (no partial state; the abort occurred before/during a fresh throwaway-wallet transaction, never touching existing protocol state). No Rust/program code changed. `docs/journey-map/` untouched throughout.
- **Session closeout + DEC-0045 architecture decision (2026-07-29).** DEC-0045 (open decision on a real on-chain DevNet swap mechanism) was resolved: DEC-0051 records the decision to build a dedicated, separate, DevNet-only constant-product AMM program (`ssr_devnet_amm`), structurally isolated from `ssr_protocol` (own program ID, disjoint PDA namespace, zero shared accounts or CPI). Implementation was started (program written, `cargo check`/`clippy` clean, deployed to DevNet at `AJbXGWSU1x9LtJW7uRKJCwS3JZYqXwqHXpX6erY7dS6c`) but is **incomplete** -- never initialized (no `AmmConfig`, no pools, no liquidity), never wired into the SDK-facing frontend, never verified end-to-end. Deliberately deferred rather than rushed: preserved on branch `experimental/devnet-amm-swap` (commit `d6d1979`), clearly labeled experimental/incomplete/DevNet-only/not production-ready, and explicitly NOT merged into `main`. `main` itself is untouched by this -- it only gained the DEC-0045/DEC-0051 decision-log entry (docs-only, commit `61440f2`) on top of the already-deployed Phase F/G state. Direct proportional redemption remains the canonical Sell path; no unfinished AMM control is exposed anywhere in the live site.
- **SDK + ManageDTR UI wiring for Phase F/G (2026-07-29).** Composition management (add/fund/remove asset, real `update_targets`) and wind-down (initiate/close) are now real, signed, root-manager-only actions in the live website, not just a Node verification script. See DEC-0049.
- **Phase F/G: composition management + wind-down, deployed and live-verified (2026-07-29).** 5 new instructions (`add_reserve_asset_active`, `fund_new_reserve_asset`, `remove_reserve_asset`, `initiate_wind_down`, `close_reserve`), 2 new `ReserveStatus` variants, 3 new errors, 5 new events, one existing method extended/renamed (`require_redemption_allowed`). Deployed via a real program upgrade and exercised end-to-end with 8 real signed transactions against live DevNet -- see DEC-0046/DEC-0048.
- **Phase E: Jupiter DevNet feasibility -- proven infeasible (2026-07-29).** Real API evidence: Jupiter's aggregator has zero DevNet awareness (returns real Mainnet-only quotes regardless of mint). Flagged as a genuine, unresolved product-decision blocker (whether/how to build a real on-chain swap mechanism) rather than building an AMM unilaterally -- see DEC-0045 for the decision needed. Does not block Phase F's composition-management or Phase G.
- **Phase D: fee/treasury verification for the devUSDC path (2026-07-29).** Confirmed fee accrual/collection is composition-agnostic by design (no protocol change needed). Live `collect_fees` call against the Phase C Reserve routed 800 pending manager + 200 pending protocol Reserve Token shares correctly. See DEC-0044.
- **Phase C: devUSDC as a real Reserve asset, live-verified (2026-07-29).** Zero protocol changes needed -- devUSDC added to the existing real-asset allowlists (CreateDTR, mint-test-assets faucet, swap-sign zap, discovery candidate mints). Live-verified via `scripts/verify_devusdc_reserve.ts`: a fresh 70% devUSDC / 30% mockX Reserve created, seeded, Bought, and Sold, all real signatures/confirmations. See DEC-0043.
- **Phase B: real devUSDC settlement token + faucet + DevNet SOL onboarding, live-verified (2026-07-29).** Real DevNet mint created and verified (`Djn4aGJ3JTgqGpGdQFkmq73gG8KvkwRswP7pNaouuw4k`); real faucet + sponsorship endpoints built, tested, and live-verified against a disposable test wallet (real signatures, real confirmed balance changes, real cooldown rejection). Zero new required secrets. See DEC-0042.
- **Live-DevNet verification of Phase A discovery (2026-07-29).** `scripts/verify_discovery.ts` (read-only, zero transactions) confirmed the canonical discovery layer works against the deployed program: all 16 existing Reserves enumerated (not just the 2 fixtures), zero integrity issues, and TestLo/MOCX genuinely found via general discovery (reserveId 13, ~1,004.975 supply, backed by mintX/"MOCX", name/ticker resolved from its own on-chain metadataUri). See DEC-0041.
- **Phase A of the new A-H DevNet delivery plan (2026-07-29): canonical on-chain Reserve discovery + mock-state purge.** `packages/sdk/src/discovery.ts` enumerates every deployed Reserve directly from `ProtocolConfig.reserveCount` (no `getProgramAccounts`); `RealReserveSync.tsx` runs it on a poll and merges results by real on-chain address; Discover/DTRDetail/Portfolio/ManageDTR are unified on one source of truth with real-vs-simulated Reserves clearly badged; the Sell tab shows real in-kind redemption estimates instead of a blended synthetic-SOL figure (DEC-0039); ManageDTR's delegate CRUD and rebalance-execute are disabled (not faked) for real on-chain Reserves pending Phase D/F. Full record and remaining Phase B-H scope in `docs/project/DEVNET_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_2026-07-29.md`; terminology finalized in DEC-0037; architecture in DEC-0038.
- **Gate 1 (research):** Full frontend inspection + Reserve Protocol Folio analysis (`docs/protocol/RESERVE_REFERENCE_ANALYSIS.md`, `FRONTEND_INTEGRATION.md`).
- **Gate 2 (architecture lock):** `SSR_ARCHITECTURE.md`/`ACCOUNT_MODEL.md`, DEC-0009 through DEC-0019.

Real BPF/SBF compilation (`cargo build-sbf`) succeeded after installing Visual Studio Build Tools, producing a genuine 538,056-byte deployable `.so`.
- **Gate 8: SSR Protocol is deployed and live on Solana DevNet.** Program ID `2dURvmSdHeyaFES5rxaE1zgPSHCBLW5BLNguJ2Tu1mkW`, deployment signature `arj6tzkUCqgSeJBs9inJv3nLcyoK5smasygGEZmDZ5hk84QukwLsJKR9mbaZryJz56uFRTkeXoGtTFSBSMWuaju`, verified on-chain via `solana program show` (correct upgradeable-loader owner, correct authority, correct data length, rent-exempt balance matching the exact `solana rent` estimate). Full record in `docs/protocol/DEVNET_RUNBOOK.md`.
- **Gate 9 (core): the full test suite has executed against the live DevNet program -- 14 passing, 0 failing.** Real Reserve creation, two-asset registration, seeding, proportional mint/redeem across two holders, pause/unpause, a scope-restricted delegate, and the cross-Reserve vault-substitution isolation test (the plan's most security-critical test) all confirmed working on-chain, not just typechecked. Full record in `DEVNET_RUNBOOK.md`'s "Test execution record."
- **Gate 9 (fixtures): persistent, documented DevNet fixtures now exist**, built via `scripts/devnet_fixtures.ts` -- a root manager, 2 restricted delegates with distinct permission scopes, 2 holders, a 2-asset Reserve and a 3-asset "multi-asset" Reserve (both seeded, both with real proportional holder balances), and a pause/unpause cycle demonstrating both Active and Paused states. Full record in `docs/protocol/DEVNET_FIXTURES.md`. Building this also surfaced a real, confirmed limitation of the public DevNet RPC endpoint: it fully blocks `getProgramAccounts` (403) and rate-limits heavily under sustained same-day use (429) -- the fixture script is checkpoint-resumable as a result.
- ~15 real compiler-caught bugs fixed (Anchor lifetime-elision across all handlers, an Anchor-1.0 `CpiContext` API change, a missing Cargo feature, a macro glob-re-export requirement) -- see `DEVNET_RUNBOOK.md`.
- A real security gap fixed: `collect_fees`'s `protocol_fee_destination` now validated against `ProtocolConfig`.
- Two more adversarial tests written (repeated-init, excess-redemption), plus the previously-stubbed cross-Reserve vault-substitution test (the most important one in the plan) fully implemented -- all now passing live, not just written.
- Two real test bugs found and fixed only by actually running against live state: a stale pre-transaction snapshot assertion, and a singleton-init test that assumed resettable local-validator state rather than persistent DevNet state.
- **Gate 6 (SDK):** `packages/sdk` typechecks clean against real installed packages.
- **Gate 10-11 (frontend integration): real wallet connection, real on-chain reads, real Buy/Sell zap, and real Create Reserve are all wired into the existing frontend with its design unchanged.** See `docs/protocol/FRONTEND_INTEGRATION.md` for the full technical record and DEC-0027 for the architecture decision. Verified against live DevNet via `scripts/verify_zap.ts` (Buy 0.005 SOL then Sell half the resulting balance against the Reserve One fixture -- vault balances, Reserve Token supply, and both parties' SOL all moved exactly as computed) and `scripts/verify_create_reserve.ts` (a brand-new Reserve created/registered/seeded from a fresh keypair with zero prior state, confirmed `active` on-chain).
- Full documentation set under `docs/protocol/`, kept current with the verified state throughout.
- Dedicated branch `protocol/devnet-v1`, pushed to origin; prior uncommitted dashboard work preserved untouched throughout.
- (Unrelated) Dashboard: password-gated `/internal/status` with configurable login rate limiting (DEC-0008).
- **Corrective pass (2026-07-28): dynamic Reserve/asset validation, SOL as a real asset, signature reduction + cost transparency, subtle DevNet status messaging, and a real end-to-end fresh-Reserve verification script -- see DEC-0029 through DEC-0032 and `scripts/verify_e2e_fresh_reserve.ts`.** Fresh Reserve `BuHRWKzzXQXhjL3WCsmHTT7qDooh2437DvXuxyExpiWg` (Reserve Token mint `AQ6i33grhTZk3vA9Ph4paGGnsdFR9nif7trEaK1aNJ2d`) created, seeded, Bought from, and Sold from with real signatures and no hardcoded registration anywhere in the path.
- **DevNet protocol treasury (DEC-0033/DEC-0035) is deployed and verified live.** `update_protocol_config` instruction shipped via a real DevNet program upgrade (signature `JuNiHri3m5wuCwv7aKaYHnMLvoMSEPUuJjCehainCrZRUg6RqfZMdUeVxosxjbAzn9hxSFc8nThXcoVGEx9BvVK`); `ProtocolConfig.default_protocol_fee_destination` set to `EME96L9JK7VQvMg76txApB8Kb9npdyUfFcpQKDqYupmq` (signature `2cEtFTEPa5qiEWdPWZ16bTVdQaDZ7XyhUK6zjJpwUKkLvKYwwE8fD11gseoyZHgVRvRthD1d6spzy8VaYRZGcmTC`); verified with a real `collect_fees` call (signature `3k4KKk9EdiuNigWgSjqWK3kiigedAKAP2ifmkX1cMXAzxE4wTk5F8jj5bbGE6sPaqUA2TEKSX11RLjRAbPFxw9Ww`) that paid the treasury 200 raw Reserve Token units (previously 0) and the Reserve's manager 800 raw units, matching the pending fee shares accrued by DEC-0032's Buy exactly.

## Blockers
- **(Resolved 2026-08-05, DEC-0081) The "no cargo/rustc/anchor/solana on PATH" blocker from DEC-0076/DEC-0077 is corrected, not just resolved** -- all four are installed on this machine (`~/.cargo/bin`, `/c/devtools/solana/solana-release/bin`), confirmed working this session (`cargo check -p ssr_devnet_amm` compiles clean, `solana balance` reads a real funded DevNet wallet), just not on the default shell PATH in prior sessions. This was a per-session PATH/environment state, not a missing installation. Building/deploying `ssr_devnet_amm` (DEC-0076) and `execute_rebalance_leg` (DEC-0077) is consequently no longer blocked -- just not yet done (see Current Objective).
- **(Resolved 2026-07-31, DEC-0069) `/internal/feedback`'s Google service-account setup was blocked by organization policy** (service-account key creation and/or cross-domain Drive sharing). Resolved by dropping the automated Drive/Sheets integration entirely in favor of a simple gated link to a manually-maintained Sheet -- no credentials needed, no blocker possible.
- **(Resolved 2026-07-29) Phase F/G program upgrade was blocked on a DevNet SOL shortfall** -- the user resolved it with a direct 5 SOL transfer to the deployer wallet; the upgrade is deployed and live-verified. See DEC-0047 (blocker) and DEC-0048 (resolution).
- **`solana-test-validator`/`anchor test` do not run on this machine** without Windows Developer Mode enabled (`ERROR_PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD` on ledger symlink creation) -- blocks fully adopting DEC-0034's localnet-by-default policy until resolved. Direct `cargo check`/`cargo-build-sbf` remain available as a fallback and were used to validate DEC-0033's new instruction before its deployment, and again this pass to confirm `ssr_devnet_amm` still compiles clean.
- The previously-tracked BPF/SBF toolchain gap (Visual Studio Build Tools) and the original DevNet SOL funding gap are both resolved -- see Decision Log DEC-0023/DEC-0024. The DevNet protocol treasury upgrade's funding gap is also resolved (the user transferred 3 SOL to the deployer wallet) and the upgrade itself is deployed and verified -- see DEC-0035.

## Dependencies
- Vercel (hosting, deployment, custom domain, and the environment variable used by the internal dashboard). **`DEVNET_SWAP_AUTHORITY_SECRET_KEY`** (a Sensitive/encrypted production env var) is now a hard runtime dependency for `api/devnet/swap-sign.ts`, `api/devnet/mint-test-assets.ts`, and (new, Phase B) `api/devnet/faucet-devusdc.ts`/`api/devnet/sponsor-sol.ts` -- Buy/Sell, Create-seeding, the devUSDC faucet, and DevNet SOL onboarding all depend on this one variable being set.
- **Helius (dedicated Solana DevNet RPC provider), new as of 2026-07-30 (DEC-0055).** `HELIUS_RPC_URL` (Sensitive/encrypted, Production+Preview; Non-sensitive on Development -- Vercel's Sensitive tier is Production/Preview-only) is now the primary RPC endpoint for every server-side DevNet action and, via the new `api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts`, for the browser's Connection in production/preview builds. The public `api.devnet.solana.com` endpoint remains as a bounded, read-only fallback inside the proxy only, and as the still-hardcoded default in `scripts/verify_*.ts` other than the new `verify_helius_buy_fix.ts`.
- Name.com (domain registrar and DNS for `strategic-super-reserve.fun`, the DevNet/beta site).
- **GoDaddy (domain registrar and DNS for `ssr.fun`, the public product domain), new as of 2026-08-04 (DEC-0073).** GoDaddy remains authoritative for `ssr.fun`'s DNS (not migrated to Vercel-managed nameservers) -- only 2 A records (apex) and 1 CNAME record (`www`) point it at Vercel. No credentials or automated access to GoDaddy exist in this environment; any future DNS change there needs to be reported as exact records for manual application, same as this pass.
- **Solana DevNet -- now an active runtime dependency, not just a target.** Program is deployed and live there; the frontend now reads/writes it directly. The real devUSDC mint (`Djn4aGJ3JTgqGpGdQFkmq73gG8KvkwRswP7pNaouuw4k`, Phase B) is also a live DevNet-only dependency with zero real value.
- Reserve Protocol's `reserve-index-dtf` reference repository (read-only, external to this repo) -- reference material only, not a runtime dependency.
- **Jupiter Tokens API V2 (`JUPITER_API_KEY`, Sensitive/Production), configured 2026-08-20 (DEC-0121).** Powers the weekly catalogue snapshot (`api/ledger/jupiter-snapshot-cron.ts`, already scheduled in `vercel.json` but non-functional until this key existed) that `api/ledger/asset-catalogue.ts` serves to the Mainnet Reserve Asset picker. A key outage/revocation doesn't break anything already-created -- it just freezes the catalogue at its last successful weekly snapshot (`ledger_asset_catalogue`, safe to fully rebuild from a fresh fetch at any time per `docs/protocol/LEDGER_ARCHITECTURE.md`).
- The public Solana DevNet airdrop faucet is unreliable from this environment (confirmed daily-limited, and confirmed rate-limited again this session across multiple retry attempts -- most recently a full lockout across 2/1/0.5/0.1 SOL requests, all refused, while attempting to fund the Phase F/G program upgrade) -- further DevNet SOL needs either the user's own wallet/faucet access or a direct transfer to the deployer wallet (`6idsSUE6u7fqHg6edrdMEjNTnG62wyCANAsJ2YBmeuHk`, 3.65985132 SOL as of the Phase F/G pass, ~0.85 SOL short of that upgrade's buffer + fee cost -- see DEC-0047), Phase B's new tightly-limited sponsored-SOL grant (`api/devnet/sponsor-sol.ts`), or the swap-authority/fixture-manager wallet directly (`Ef7vbQghn7Fc4LzUnyJsvov1f5f9aRSfWksiaSmWpquj`, **live-confirmed 0.2345 SOL as of the 2026-07-30 Helius/Buy-fix pass** -- see Risks).

## Risks
- **(New, 2026-08-20, DEC-0121) A Mainnet Reserve holding a non-USDC Jupiter-catalogue asset is only discoverable/visible to OTHER visitors within the Ledger's daily ingest-cron cycle -- not instantly.** The live Mainnet discovery poll (`RealReserveSync.tsx`, every ~15s) deliberately probes only mints the Ledger has seen used on-chain (`api/ledger/known-asset-mints.ts`, sourced from `ledger_events`, refreshed once daily by `api/ledger/ingest-cron.ts`) rather than the full ~500-entry Jupiter catalogue, to avoid multiplying live Mainnet RPC volume for assets nobody has used yet. The CREATOR's own browser sees its own just-created Reserve immediately (`useAppStore.mainnetKnownAssetMints`, populated client-side right after a successful launch) -- this gap only affects a different visitor loading the site before that day's ingest cron has run. Acceptable tradeoff per Creator's explicit choice between this and the RPC-cost alternative; not a data-integrity bug (the Reserve is genuinely on-chain and correct, just temporarily unresolved by discovery for other visitors).
- **(New, 2026-08-20, DEC-0119) The internal-team dashboard password now also unlocks strategic-super-reserve.fun's fund-moving Mainnet pages.** `SSR_SITE_PASSWORD` (the site-wide gate added by DEC-0117) was deliberately set equal to `SSR_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` at Creator's explicit, re-confirmed request, reversing DEC-0117's original separation. Anyone who has the internal dashboard password -- a broader, lower-trust distribution than "people allowed to use the live product" -- can now also reach Buy/Sell/Create/Manage on the live Mainnet product, not just `/internal/*`. The two passwords will also silently drift apart if either is rotated by hand without rotating the other. If this is ever unwound, that's a separate `vercel env rm`/`vercel env add` + redeploy back to an independent `SSR_SITE_PASSWORD` value.
- **(New, 2026-08-03) Buying a Reserve with 0% devUSDC composition costs the buyer $0 real devUSDC, and Selling it then mints the buyer real devUSDC with no cooldown or ceiling.** Confirmed live: buying into TestLo (100% mockX) via `buy-devusdc` never debited the buyer's devUSDC balance at all (the mockX leg is minted "for free" by the swap authority, sized to the requested notional -- this mechanic already existed for non-devUSDC legs, just was previously blocked from executing), then Selling converted the mockX proceeds into freshly-minted real devUSDC. This is consistent with the pre-existing DevNet test-asset model (mockX/Y/Z are worthless, freely-mintable convenience tokens, same as devUSDC's own faucet), but unlike `api/devnet/faucet-devusdc.ts` (500 devUSDC/claim, 2000 ceiling, 60s cooldown), this Buy-then-Sell path has no rate limit or ceiling of its own -- a user could repeat it to mint arbitrarily large amounts of devUSDC. Flagged transparently rather than fixed in this pass (a rate-limit/ceiling decision for this specific path is a product/security call, not something this pass's explicit scope covered) -- see `docs/project/ENGINEERING_TIMELINE.md`'s Fixes entry for this pass.
- **(Corrected, 2026-08-03) The 2026-07-31 entry below claiming local signed-transaction verification was blocked without Vercel-pulled secrets was incorrect.** The already-committed `devnet-fixtures/manager-keypair.json` fixture file IS the DevNet swap-authority/devUSDC-mint-authority keypair (matching `scripts/verify_swap_sign_endpoint.ts`'s pre-existing pattern) -- setting `process.env.DEVNET_SWAP_AUTHORITY_SECRET_KEY` from it locally is sufficient for a real signed Buy/Sell/Create verification with no Vercel access needed. Confirmed this session via `scripts/verify_corrective_pass.ts`'s real, live, signed Create/Seed/Buy/Sell chain. `HELIUS_RPC_URL` is genuinely Vercel-only, but the public `api.devnet.solana.com` endpoint (used by all `scripts/verify_*.ts` already) is sufficient for this kind of verification, just slower under congestion.
- **(Resolved, 2026-08-03) The swap-authority wallet's DevNet SOL balance, previously flagged at 0.2345 SOL (2026-07-30/31), is now live-confirmed at 9.23243462 SOL** -- the user has topped it up. Comfortably above the operational floor for its four duties (Sell payouts, Buy's wrapped-SOL legs, faucet ATA rent, SOL sponsorship). Still worth periodic monitoring under sustained testing traffic, but no longer an elevated/urgent risk.
- **The public DevNet RPC endpoint (`api.devnet.solana.com`) is confirmed unreliable under sustained same-day use**: it fully blocks `getProgramAccounts` (403 "Your IP or provider is blocked from this endpoint") and rate-limits heavily (429) under repeated sequential calls -- confirmed again this session (a direct request during this pass's own investigation returned a raw `429` immediately). **Update (2026-07-30, Helius integration pass):** Helius is now the primary RPC provider everywhere (server-side directly, browser via the new `api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts`), which should substantially reduce reliance on the public endpoint's capacity -- the public endpoint remains only as the proxy's bounded fallback. Combined with the 2026-07-29 pass's client-side mitigations (adaptive backoff, cache/dedup, concurrency cap, bounded polling-based confirmation), this is the strongest mitigation yet, though Helius's own DevNet-tier rate limits are a new dependency to monitor under heavier testing traffic.
- **No real browser + Phantom click-through has been performed** -- no browser-automation tool was available in this environment. Every on-chain code path (wallet-adapter wiring, the zap transactions, Create Reserve) was verified against live DevNet via direct scripts using a throwaway keypair standing in for a connected wallet, and all modules were confirmed to load/transform cleanly through the Vite dev server, but the literal UI click path is unverified by a human or automated browser.
- **No automated test suite exists for the dashboard/frontend either** (pre-existing risk, unchanged; the new integration logic has live-DevNet verification scripts instead, see `scripts/verify_*.ts`).
- **Main JS bundle exceeds 500 kB** (pre-existing, now larger with the Solana web3/wallet-adapter/Anchor libraries bundled client-side for the first time).
- **The general testing site relies on obscurity, not authentication** (see Decision Log DEC-0004/DEC-0005, unchanged, pre-existing).
- **Rebalance trade execution (an actual Jupiter-route CPI) does not exist on-chain at all in v1** (deliberate, per DEC-0017/DEC-0021). Separately, the Buy/Sell zap's SOL<->asset leg uses a DevNet-only fixed-price swap authority, not a real router -- see `FRONTEND_INTEGRATION.md`'s Mainnet-readiness notes below.
- **The upgrade authority is a single dev-controlled key** (`6idsSUE6u7fqHg6edrdMEjNTnG62wyCANAsJ2YBmeuHk`, DevNet only) -- must migrate to a multisig before any restricted beta, per DEC-0015. **(2026-08-17, DEC-0105 clarification):** this key is Creator (Claude User)'s wallet; keeping sole control with it for the current early stage (no boss access, no automatic multisig transfer) was an explicit confirmed decision, not an oversight -- see "Authority & Treasury Structure" below. This risk entry itself is unchanged and still stands as the documented eventual-migration goal. **(2026-08-18, DEC-0112 update):** the final temporary Mainnet target is Creator's Protocol Admin wallet `CgHFxD4XHZzmSGEomnMXipGo75ejqhVd5aNY4GHg4Rw8`, still a single key, transferable later, never made immutable -- this risk stands unchanged for Mainnet too. Additionally found during this investigation: the deployed/repository program has no instruction to pause/unpause the protocol at all (single-admin or dual-admin), a separate, pre-existing gap from the multisig-migration risk this entry describes -- see "Authority & Treasury Structure (Mainnet target)" below.

## Mainnet-Readiness Gaps (Gate 10-11 additions)
- **Swap routing**: the DevNet swap authority (fixed test pricing, mint-authority-based asset issuance) must be replaced with a real routing provider (e.g. Jupiter) before Mainnet -- isolated behind `packages/sdk/src/zapInstructions.ts`'s interface so the frontend itself needs no changes, only the instruction-building internals.
- **Asset allowlist**: Buy/Sell/Create-seeding currently only work for the 3 DevNet fixture test mints plus wrapped SOL (the swap authority's own mint authority and its own real SOL balance are what make this possible) -- a production allowlist needs a real asset-onboarding process instead. The allowlist check itself is now dynamic/per-asset-mint rather than per-Reserve-address (DEC-0029), which is the right shape for Mainnet too -- only the specific mints in the allowlist need to change.
- **Oracle/pricing**: `SOL_TEST_PRICE_USD` and the $1-per-fixture-asset constants are fixed testing values, not a price feed -- Mainnet needs Pyth (or similar) for any displayed USD value; core mint/redeem accounting itself never depends on price data (unchanged from the original protocol design).
- **Slippage protection**: the zap's `maxAssetAmounts`/`minAssetAmountsOut` use a flat 2% buffer computed client/server-side, not a user-configurable slippage tolerance.
- **Transaction atomicity**: already production-shaped (single atomic transaction per Buy/Sell), no gap here.
- **Security review/audit**: not started; DevNet-only testing infrastructure (the swap authority, faucet) has zero exposure to real value and would need to be removed entirely, not merely reviewed, before Mainnet.
- **Internal tooling for launch (2026-08-18, DEC-0108)**: `/internal/kpis` (protocol usage-stats dashboard, DEC-0107) is a confirmed Mainnet-launch requirement -- must exist and be operational by the time Mainnet goes live. `/internal/status` (this project-status/decision-log dashboard) is explicitly DevNet-only and does NOT need to carry over. No implementation plan yet exists for standing `/internal/kpis` up against a Mainnet deployment (new program ID, likely a separate/parallel `reserve_activity_log` scope) -- this is a stated requirement, not yet a plan. Separately, DEC-0107's rollout surfaced a pre-existing Borsh event-decode bug (`Invalid vec for "assetMints"`) affecting a subset of older DevNet Reserves' history backfill -- deliberately deferred, not fixed, per explicit instruction; does not block the dashboard's core function, but the underlying event-parser bug is real and could in principle resurface on Mainnet once enough real transaction diversity accumulates.

## Authority & Treasury Structure (current, 2026-08-17, DEC-0105)

**Terminology note:** "Creator (Claude User)" (shortened to "Creator" in the table below) refers specifically to the individual who currently controls and develops the SSR protocol. It does NOT mean a "Reserve creator" -- an application user who launches a Reserve through SSR.fun (see `docs/protocol/ACCOUNT_MODEL.md`'s `Reserve` seed derivation, which uses "creator" generically for that unrelated role). The two are never the same thing in this document.

Confirmed by Creator (Claude User) for the current early stage (full decision record: `docs/project/DECISION_LOG.md` DEC-0105):

| Authority or asset | Current controller |
|---|---|
| Program upgrade authority | Creator's wallet |
| Protocol admin | Creator's wallet |
| Deployment authority | Creator's wallet |
| Other protocol-wide operational authorities | Creator's wallet, where applicable |
| Company treasury | Treasury multisig containing Creator and the boss |
| Reserve Asset custody | Program-controlled PDAs |
| Individual Reserve management | Applicable Reserve managers and delegates |

What this distinguishes:
- **Authority over deployed program code** (upgrading `ssr_protocol`'s on-chain bytecode) -- Creator's wallet only.
- **Authority over protocol configuration and emergency functions** (`ProtocolConfig` admin actions: program-wide pause/unpause, global limits, default fee settings for new Reserves) -- Creator's wallet only; see `docs/protocol/SSR_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 7 and `docs/protocol/ACCOUNT_MODEL.md`'s ProtocolConfig section for what this authority can and cannot do.
- **Authority over company treasury assets** -- the treasury multisig (Creator + the boss), once created. Not created yet as of this entry.
- **Program-controlled custody of Reserve Assets** -- unchanged, non-custodial: every Reserve's assets sit in program-derived PDAs (`vault_authority`), never in any human-controlled wallet, including Creator's wallet and the treasury multisig. See `docs/protocol/SECURITY_INVARIANTS.md`'s "Upgrade / admin blast-radius summary."
- **Permissions belonging to individual Reserve managers and delegates** -- unaffected by this entry; governed entirely by the existing Reserve-manager/delegate architecture (`docs/protocol/ACCOUNT_MODEL.md`, `docs/protocol/SSR_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 7).

Security notes:
- Private keys and seed phrases must never be shared. The boss's treasury-multisig membership does not include, and must never require, access to Creator's private key, seed phrase, hardware wallet, or keypair files.
- Creator's authority wallet (program upgrade authority, protocol admin, deployment authority) should be treated as security-critical.
- The treasury multisig must not be described as controlling Reserve Assets -- it does not, and per DEC-0105 point 7/8 must not automatically gain that control.
- This is an interim, early-stage arrangement, not a final Mainnet governance design. The pre-existing `public/road-to-mainnet.html` checklist items MS-02 ("Multisig on the upgrade authority") and PM-08 ("Upgrade authority") remain open exactly as before -- this entry does not fulfill, schedule, or set a date/threshold for that eventual migration. Any future transfer of program upgrade authority, protocol-admin authority, deployment authority, or another protocol-wide authority requires a separate, later, explicit decision by Creator (Claude User), per DEC-0105 point 13.

**(2026-08-17, DEC-0106):** every situation where real SOL/money is locked, at risk, or recoverable (rent-exemption deposits, deploy buffers, abandoned accounts) is now tracked permanently in `docs/project/FUNDS_LEDGER.md`. Tracking is automatic; actually moving funds is never automatic and always requires a fresh, explicit, same-time confirmation of source/amount/destination.

**(2026-08-18, DEC-0112 supersedes the table above for Mainnet):** the section immediately below records the final, confirmed TEMPORARY Mainnet authority and treasury structure. DEC-0105's table above remains the accurate historical record of the early-stage/DevNet-era decision and is preserved unedited; it is not the current Mainnet plan. Terminology note: "Creator (Claude User)" above and "Creator" below refer to the same individual -- "Creator" is the shorthand now in use going forward. "The boss" above and "Boss" below likewise refer to the same second individual.

## Authority & Treasury Structure (Mainnet target, 2026-08-18, DEC-0112)

Confirmed by Creator for the final temporary Mainnet structure (full decision record: `docs/project/DECISION_LOG.md` DEC-0112). This is the structure intended to be CONFIGURED AT Mainnet genesis -- as of this entry, **no Mainnet program has been deployed and no authority has actually been transferred or configured anywhere; this is a documented target, verified for on-chain feasibility, not yet executed.**

| Authority or asset | Controller |
|---|---|
| Program upgrade/closure authority | Creator Protocol Admin wallet (`CgHFxD4XHZzmSGEomnMXipGo75ejqhVd5aNY4GHg4Rw8`) -- single-wallet, intentionally, transferable later, program will NOT be made immutable (no `--final`) |
| Canonical IDL authority | Creator Protocol Admin wallet (`CgHFxD4XHZzmSGEomnMXipGo75ejqhVd5aNY4GHg4Rw8`) -- same wallet as upgrade authority initially, but a distinct authority, documented separately |
| Protocol administration | Creator Protocol Admin (`CgHFxD4XHZzmSGEomnMXipGo75ejqhVd5aNY4GHg4Rw8`) AND Boss Protocol Admin (`PSpQGPvw7tZedKJvN21dJkh3vdDQeXkwA5n9DKBRZw5`), each independently -- **(2026-08-18, DEC-0113) code support now implemented, built, and offline-tested (`ProtocolConfig.admin_2` + `is_admin()`); not yet deployed anywhere. Boss does not hold upgrade/closure authority regardless.** |
| Emergency pause/unpause (protocol-wide) | Either Protocol Admin independently -- **(2026-08-18, DEC-0113) new `set_protocol_paused` instruction implemented, built, and offline-tested (see Code-support finding below); not yet deployed anywhere.** |
| Fee configuration | Either Protocol Admin independently, subject to code support (see below) |
| Protocol-fee destination configuration | Either Protocol Admin independently, subject to code support (see below) |
| Company Treasury | Squads Treasury vault `3CBpVMPDQD75b5bXgDunkpVJ3EeQWcwU9DCSLsTjWQL5` (vault index 0 of Squads Multisig `B2BUEztDn1SKFZ7Kh3hakEWsD5sVM8gxLAE4wqS9w2am`), governed 2-of-3 by that multisig's 3 members -- **verified on-chain, see below** |
| Protocol-fee receipt | Squads Treasury vault `3CBpVMPDQD75b5bXgDunkpVJ3EeQWcwU9DCSLsTjWQL5` -- **verified PDA/ATA-compatible with the deployed fee-collection instructions, see below; not yet configured as the live `default_protocol_fee_destination` anywhere** |
| Reserve Asset custody | Program-controlled PDAs (`vault_authority`), unchanged -- never the Treasury vault, never any Protocol Admin wallet |
| Individual Reserve management | Applicable Reserve managers and delegates, unchanged |

**The five distinct addresses in this structure (never to be confused with one another):**

| Address | Role | On-curve? |
|---|---|---|
| `B2BUEztDn1SKFZ7Kh3hakEWsD5sVM8gxLAE4wqS9w2am` | Squads **Multisig Account** -- configuration/verification/CLI/SDK only. Never a fund destination, never a program/protocol authority. | No (PDA-like Squads account, verified below) |
| `3CBpVMPDQD75b5bXgDunkpVJ3EeQWcwU9DCSLsTjWQL5` | Squads **Treasury vault** -- the actual company Treasury address and intended protocol-fee destination. | No (off-curve; independently verified below to be exactly the Squads vault-index-0 PDA of the Multisig Account above) |
| `CgHFxD4XHZzmSGEomnMXipGo75ejqhVd5aNY4GHg4Rw8` | Creator's Protocol Admin wallet (also program upgrade/closure authority, also canonical IDL authority) | Yes (ordinary keypair) |
| `PSpQGPvw7tZedKJvN21dJkh3vdDQeXkwA5n9DKBRZw5` | Boss's Protocol Admin wallet (protocol admin only -- no upgrade/closure authority) | Yes (ordinary keypair) |
| `EME96L9JK7VQvMg76txApB8Kb9npdyUfFcpQKDqYupmq` / `2cLftQ4ftvRaqLs3UYi3sxh4RUfQFZJRkJBBEmW5HqCX` / `EkQ84781DvGgp8y32EhxS48HABDsYJ2JSY692an4juAz` | The 3 Treasury multisig signers (Creator, company offline recovery, Boss respectively) -- 2-of-3 threshold | Yes (ordinary keypairs; independently confirmed as the multisig's exact on-chain member set, see below) |

**Code-support finding (the central technical question this decision required investigating):** `programs/ssr_protocol/src/state/protocol_config.rs`'s `ProtocolConfig` account has exactly ONE `authority: Pubkey` field -- not a `Vec<Pubkey>`, not a fixed 2-element array, not `admin` + `secondary_authority`, not a role/permission registry. The only admin-gated instruction, `update_protocol_config` (`update_protocol_config.rs`), checks it via a single Anchor `has_one = authority @ SsrError::NotProtocolAuthority` constraint. ~~The deployed and repository code does NOT currently support two simultaneous independent Protocol Admin wallets.~~ **(2026-08-18, DEC-0113, RESOLVED in source) `ProtocolConfig` now has a second field, `admin_2`, plus a `ProtocolConfig::is_admin(&self, key)` helper; `update_protocol_config`'s constraint now checks either admin. Rebuilt clean (`cargo-build-sbf`), IDL regenerated and confirmed to include `admin_2`, full offline test/lint/typecheck suite passing (see DECISION_LOG.md DEC-0113/DEC-0114). Not yet deployed to any network -- no live-validator/DevNet exercise of this code has been possible on this machine (see Blockers).** Because Mainnet has not been deployed yet, this change needed no live-state migration (no Mainnet `ProtocolConfig` account exists yet).

A second, independently significant finding from the same investigation: ~~there is currently no on-chain instruction anywhere in the program that can pause or unpause the protocol.~~ **(2026-08-18, DEC-0113, RESOLVED in source) New instruction `set_protocol_paused(paused: bool)` added, gated by the same `is_admin` check, emitting `ProtocolPausedSet`.** `ProtocolConfig.paused` is a real field, checked (blocks `create_reserve`/`mint_reserve_tokens_in_kind`/`seed_reserve` when true) -- previously it was only ever set once, to `false`, in `initialize_protocol`'s `ProtocolConfig::initial()`, with no instruction anywhere writing to it afterward. `docs/protocol/SSR_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 7 currently states ProtocolConfig authority "can pause/unpause the *entire program*" -- this now matches the repository code again as of DEC-0113. Not yet deployed to any network.

**Fee-destination PDA/ATA compatibility (Squads vault as `default_protocol_fee_destination`) -- verified safe:** `collect_protocol_fee.rs` and `collect_fees.rs` (the only two instructions that pay the protocol-fee destination) both derive `protocol_fee_destination_token_account` as `associated_token::authority = protocol_fee_destination`, where `protocol_fee_destination` is an `UncheckedAccount` -- never required to sign anything, and the ATA is funded via `token::mint_to` CPI (a mint operation, not a transfer requiring the destination's own signature). This works identically for an on-curve wallet or an off-curve PDA. Reserve Token protocol fees are the ONLY thing this destination ever receives on-chain -- there is no SOL-denominated protocol-fee path in the program. The one real edge case (the same account appearing twice as a mutable account in one instruction, Anchor's `ConstraintDuplicateMutableAccount`/error 2040, when a Reserve's `manager_fee_destination` happens to equal `protocol_config.default_protocol_fee_destination`) is already handled in `collect_fees.rs` via its `dup` attribute (documented fix from a 2026-08-13 corrective pass, unrelated to this decision) -- `collect_protocol_fee.rs` has only one destination account and cannot hit this case at all.

**Squads on-chain verification (public information only, no private keys requested or inspected):**
- `getAccountInfo` on `B2BUEztDn1SKFZ7Kh3hakEWsD5sVM8gxLAE4wqS9w2am` (Mainnet, `api.mainnet-beta.solana.com`): owner = `SQDS4ep65T869zMMBKyuUq6aD6EgTu8psMjkvj52pCf`, the real Squads V4 program -- confirms this is a genuine Squads multisig account, not an arbitrary address.
- The account's raw data was decoded directly (Squads V4 Multisig layout): **threshold = 2**, **exactly 3 members** -- `2cLftQ4ftvRaqLs3UYi3sxh4RUfQFZJRkJBBEmW5HqCX`, `EME96L9JK7VQvMg76txApB8Kb9npdyUfFcpQKDqYupmq`, `EkQ84781DvGgp8y32EhxS48HABDsYJ2JSY692an4juAz` -- an EXACT match, byte-for-byte, to the three given Treasury signer addresses, each with full (initiate+vote+execute) permissions. `config_authority` decodes to the zero-pubkey (no external controller of the multisig's own configuration).
- `3CBpVMPDQD75b5bXgDunkpVJ3EeQWcwU9DCSLsTjWQL5` is mathematically **exactly** the Squads vault-index-0 PDA derived from `findProgramAddressSync(["multisig", <multisig pubkey>, "vault", [0]], SQDS4ep...)` -- computed independently, not assumed from the task's claim. `PublicKey.isOnCurve()` confirms it is genuinely off-curve (no possible private key), consistent with being a PDA. Its on-chain state is currently owned by the System Program with a small SOL balance and no data -- consistent with an as-yet-unused vault, not evidence against the PDA finding (an unused PDA legitimately looks exactly like this until it first signs a CPI or receives an SPL token, at which point an ATA/token account would be created under it, never changing the vault address itself).

**What this distinguishes:**
- **Authority over deployed program code** (upgrading/closing `ssr_protocol`'s on-chain bytecode) -- Creator Protocol Admin wallet only, single-wallet by design, transferable later via a separate explicit decision, never made immutable.
- **Authority over protocol configuration** (`ProtocolConfig` admin actions -- currently just `update_protocol_config`) -- intended for both Protocol Admin wallets independently; **not yet supported by the deployed/repository code for two wallets** (see Code-support finding).
- **Authority over company Treasury assets** -- the Squads Treasury vault, 2-of-3 verified on-chain.
- **Program-controlled custody of Reserve Assets** -- unchanged, non-custodial; never any Protocol Admin wallet, never the Treasury vault/multisig.
- **Permissions belonging to individual Reserve managers and delegates** -- unaffected by this decision.

**Security notes:**
- Private keys and seed phrases must never be shared, requested, or inspected -- none were for this investigation; every verification above used only public on-chain data and independently-computable PDA math.
- Both Protocol Admin wallets should be treated as security-critical; the Creator Protocol Admin wallet doubly so, since it also carries program upgrade/closure authority.
- The Squads Multisig Account address (`B2BUEztDn1SKFZ7Kh3hakEWsD5sVM8gxLAE4wqS9w2am`) must never receive assets, never be used as a fee destination, and never be set as any program or protocol authority -- only the Treasury vault (`3CBpVMPDQD75b5bXgDunkpVJ3EeQWcwU9DCSLsTjWQL5`) fills those roles.
- The Treasury vault must never be described as holding or controlling Reserve Assets -- it does not, and per this decision must not automatically gain that control.
- This remains an early-stage, temporary Mainnet structure, not a final governance design. `public/road-to-mainnet.html`'s MS-02/PM-08 checklist items remain open exactly as before -- this decision does not fulfill, schedule, or contradict them (that frontend/database-backed checklist file was not modified by this documentation-only pass).

**Pending until verified or performed:**
- ~~Dual-Protocol-Admin code compatibility~~ -- **DONE in source (DEC-0113), built and offline-tested (DEC-0114); deploy still pending.**
- ~~A protocol-wide emergency pause/unpause instruction~~ -- **DONE in source (DEC-0113), built and offline-tested (DEC-0114); deploy still pending.**
- Squads PDA fee-destination compatibility -- code-verified safe (above); a live small Treasury transfer test and a live fee-routing test are still outstanding.
- Mainnet program deployment.
- Mainnet authority configuration (setting `ProtocolConfig.authority` to the Creator Protocol Admin wallet at `initialize_protocol` time, and the program's actual BPFLoaderUpgradeable upgrade authority to the same wallet).
- Canonical IDL publication under the Creator Protocol Admin wallet.
- Final on-chain verification of the above once performed.
