2026-08-21 (DEC-0134: implemented the Mainnet USD pricing layer, fixed ALPHA's $0 display bugs, deployed to production). `onChainReserve.ts` priced every Mainnet asset from a fixed DevNet-only test table -- any real asset outside it (i.e. everything but real USDC) read as $0, so AUM/Token Price and the Buy "Est. You Receive" all read $0/~0.00 for the real, live ALPHA Reserve (100% SSR). Live-diagnosed ALPHA directly (vault 21,132.446514 SSR, supply 9.95 ALPHA, no Pyth feed for SSR but a real Jupiter Price V3 quote confirmed live), then built a real server-side pricing layer: Pyth Core primary (verified feed IDs, staleness/confidence-validated) with Jupiter Price V3 fallback (decimals/block-recency-validated), never trusting a client-supplied price, never fabricating $0 for an unpriced asset, flagging material source deviation. Removed the redundant "NAV per Token" card (replaced with an independently-computed Market Cap); reconciled the Buy estimate directly against the program's own on-chain ratio math instead of a USD-price-derived formula, so the displayed quote can never disagree with the submitted transaction; fixed two further ALPHA-specific bugs surfaced while tracing that chain (a hardcoded-USDC-only candidate-mint bug that would have failed Buy/Sell outright for any non-USDC Reserve, and a USDC-labeled balance/insufficient-balance check that was wrong for a non-USDC deposit asset). 32 new/updated regression tests, 737/737 passing, full validation clean. Deployed to production and live-verified post-deploy: AUM/Token Price/Market Cap all confirmed real and nonzero (~$10.17/~$1.02/~$10.17) against ALPHA's live on-chain state through the deployed endpoint. No Mainnet program or IDL change. See DEC-0134.

2026-08-21 (DEC-0133: implemented the USDC fee-settlement redesign -- NOT deployed to Mainnet). Fees now crystallize into a shared per-Reserve fee vault instead of minting straight to final destinations, redeemed via a new permissionless instruction using the identical entitlement math a normal Sell already uses, swapped to USDC off-chain by a bounded, keeper-delegated Jupiter swap (the program never signs a swap), and distributed idempotently to the Protocol Treasury and Manager recipient(s) by a third new instruction that always reads the real current USDC balance rather than a remembered total. Presented the full design (accounts, instructions, fee math, security, costs, migration, compatibility, deployment plan) and got explicit approval before writing any code. Caught and fixed a serious self-introduced bug before it ever compiled: the first draft would have added the keeper wallet directly onto the live ProtocolConfig singleton, which every instruction reads -- breaking the entire program on upgrade. Fixed with a new, separate SettlementKeeperConfig singleton PDA instead, matching this codebase's own established "never grow an already-live shared account" lesson. Also caught and fixed two of its own IDL-regeneration mistakes before reporting done. 30 new regression tests (13 Rust, 17 TypeScript), 707/707 TS + 13/13 Rust passing, full validation (cargo check/clippy/cargo-build-sbf/tsc/oxlint/build) clean. No Mainnet program upgrade, no live settlement transaction -- per Creator's explicit instruction, this is implementation + local validation + the requested pre-deployment report only. See DEC-0133, docs/project/FEE_SETTLEMENT_RUNBOOK.md.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0131: fixed two live-reported Mainnet display bugs, deployed to production; DEC-0132: manager-fee-currency request investigated and deliberately deferred). Creator reported hardcoded "Live on Solana DevNet" labels on Reserve cards, a Mainnet Reserve's composition showing "Asset" instead of its real symbol (e.g. SSR), and asked that manager fees be charged/claimable in USDC instead of the Reserve's own token. Fixed the first two: cluster-fixed roughly a dozen hardcoded "DevNet" strings across Discover/Portfolio and the entire global nav/footer/wallet-UI shell (Shell.tsx/WalletPanel.tsx/WalletModal.tsx -- wraps every page, never touched by the original Mainnet-launch pass), and threaded the Jupiter asset catalogue's mint->symbol map into Reserve discovery so a real Mainnet asset resolves its actual symbol instead of a generic "AssetN" placeholder. Investigated the fee-currency request before writing code: confirmed fees are minted as additional Reserve Token supply today (the original, deliberate design), and changing this is a real protocol-level Rust redesign of a live Mainnet program -- with existing pending fee balances and no local test-validator coverage -- gated on two open product questions (where would a 100%-non-USDC Reserve's USDC fees come from; what happens to already-accrued pending fees) that need Creator's answer first. 10 new tests (690/690 total passing), full validation clean, live-verified. See DEC-0131/DEC-0132.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0130: confirmed root cause of "seeding step reads zero", deployed to production). Connection.getTokenAccountBalance -- the ONLY way the client reads a real Mainnet SPL token balance -- was missing from both api/mainnet/rpc-proxy.ts's and api/devnet/rpc-proxy.ts's method allowlists since Jupiter-swap-funded seeding was introduced (DEC-0124); every call was rejected and silently swallowed into a reported balance of 0, deterministically. This superseded/corrected the same-day DEC-0127/DEC-0128 pass's "RPC eventual consistency" diagnosis for this symptom class, which was incomplete. Confirmed live (getTokenAccountsByOwner, getAccountInfo, getTransaction decoding, and a direct proxy-method probe against production -- no signature ever submitted): wallet 6BjTPAWGjUYjL2Hrvz7iVmzWv8yKHNDqUAif5DEPWZen genuinely held ~191,598 SSR, ~9x the ~21,000 required, while the client kept re-swapping the full $10 budget on every attempt. Fixed the allowlist (confirmed live post-deploy: the identical proxy call now returns the real balance), reworked seed-funding to swap only the genuine deficit against target rather than the full budget every retry, added exponential balance-poll backoff, and excluded Token-2022 mints from the Mainnet Reserve Asset picker (every packages/sdk instruction builder hardcodes the classic Token program -- a real, separate, confirmed gap surfaced during this diagnosis, not itself this incident's cause). 19 new tests, including a self-auditing check that scans actual Connection method usage in createReserveClient.ts/rpcResilience.ts against both rpc-proxy allowlists (680/680 total passing), full validation clean, live-verified. See DEC-0130.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0129: site-wide password gate temporarily disabled at Creator's explicit request, deployed to production). Added a reversible `SSR_SITE_GATE_ENABLED=false` Production env toggle to middleware.ts's Gate 1 (the public site-wide password, DEC-0117/0119) rather than deleting the gate's code, so it can be turned back on later with just an env var change. The separate internal dashboard gate (`/road-to-mainnet`, `/internal/*`, SSR_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD) was NOT touched -- confirmed still requiring its own login. Live-verified: `strategic-super-reserve.fun`/`/create` now serve real content with no login required; `/road-to-mainnet` still shows the internal login page. See DEC-0129.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0128: fixed the "genuinely successful seed reported as a failure" architecture gap, deployed to production). Creator reported the UI marking a Reserve deployment incomplete (`SeedAmountTooLow`/"received less than expected: 0%") despite the Creator genuinely receiving the correct Reserve Token amount. Read-only on-chain diagnosis (decoded the actual failing transaction's instruction data, fixing a base58-vs-base64 decoding bug along the way) confirmed a real `seed_amounts=[0]` was submitted despite the named wallet holding ~128,431 SSR at the time -- the exact client-side trigger for that specific submission could not be fully proven (stated honestly as an unproven hypothesis, not a certainty). Independent of that one transaction, fixed the real architecture gap: CreateDTR.tsx now re-checks authoritative on-chain Reserve state via a new, structurally read-only `checkReserveGenuinelyComplete` (cannot fund/swap/seed anything) before ever reporting "deployment incomplete," in both the Resume and fresh-submit failure paths -- a Reserve that's actually already complete is now finalized as a success, never left showing a false failure or offered a retry that could double-submit. 661/661 offline tests passing (14 new), full validation clean, live-verified. No Reserve state was read-write-touched or resubmitted during diagnosis. MCR-01 comment not posted (site/dashboard passwords came back masked via Vercel CLI) -- flagged to Creator directly. See DEC-0128.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0127: fixed a stale post-swap balance read and added pre-flight seed-amount validation, deployed to production). Creator hit a real on-chain revert (`SeedAmountTooLow`) right after a toast claiming a Jupiter swap delivered 0 SSR; independently verified via a live RPC balance check that the swap had genuinely succeeded (the wallet holds 107,147.42 SSR) -- the app's "0" reading was stale, racing ahead of RPC eventual consistency immediately after confirmation, the same root-cause class as DEC-0115's earlier redemption-read false alarm. `fetchOwnedBalanceRawSettled` now retries the post-swap read until it genuinely changes (up to ~6s); a new pre-flight `assertSeedAmountsMeetMinimum` (plus the SDK's existing, previously-unwired `validateSeedPlan`) refuses to submit a seed transaction that's still below the protocol's real minimum, naming the asset, rather than wasting one on a guaranteed revert. 647/647 offline tests passing (4 new), full validation clean, live-verified -- no funds were ever at risk. Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0127.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0126: fixed a misleading Jupiter swap failure message and enabled dynamic slippage, deployed to production). Creator hit a real on-chain swap rejection (`Custom(52)` from an external AMM in the Jupiter route) explained through `describeOnChainError`, which is scoped to ssr_protocol's own IDL and produced nonsensical guidance for a transaction that never touches ssr_protocol. New `describeJupiterSwapError` (jupiterSwapClient.ts) explains it honestly instead -- names the real code, states the likely cause (slippage) without asserting certainty about a program this repo has no IDL for. Also enabled Jupiter's `dynamicSlippage` on the swap build (previously a fixed 1.5%), which computes a volatility-aware tolerance per trade instead -- live-confirmed it picks a genuinely different bound than the fixed default for this exact token/route. 643/643 offline tests passing (3 new), full validation clean, live-verified. Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0126.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0125: Jupiter swap shortfalls no longer block Reserve creation, deployed to production). Creator hit DEC-0124's new shortfall error live while testing; traced to comparing the swap's real result against the quote's optimistic estimate instead of Jupiter's actual on-chain slippage guarantee, so ordinary price movement was wrongly treated as a failure. Per Creator's direction, removed the block: `fundSeedAssetsIdempotent` now always seeds with the real post-swap balance, and only fires a non-blocking warning toast (via a new `onSwapShortfall` callback) when the result is more than 5% short of quote. 640/640 offline tests passing (4 new, including a new pure `computeSwapShortfallPct`), full validation clean, live-verified. Posted a follow-up comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0125.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0124: fixed all 3 MCR-01-reported Create Reserve issues, including real Jupiter-swap-funded Reserve creation, deployed to production). Creator live-tested Reserve creation and reported (via MCR-01 comments): the Primary Fee Destination field wasn't visibly pre-filled; creating a non-USDC (SSR) Reserve failed demanding the creator already hold that exact asset, when USDC should trade into it via Jupiter automatically; the Wallet Cost Summary total didn't match what Phantom asked for one transaction. All three confirmed and fixed: the fee field now backfills once the wallet resolves (never overwriting a manual edit); new `api/mainnet/jupiter-swap.ts` builds a real, unsigned Jupiter swap transaction the CONNECTED WALLET signs and submits itself (never server-custodied, same model as every other Mainnet write path here) -- `fundSeedAssetsIdempotent` now swaps a USDC shortfall into any non-USDC/non-wrapped-SOL asset, which also surfaced and fixed a real pricing bug (every non-SOL asset was assumed worth exactly $1, only true for USDC); the cost summary now explains its total is summed across every transaction (Phantom shows one popup's cost, not the total) and its counts now include the new swap step(s), previously undercounted. 636/636 offline tests passing (11 new), full validation clean, live-verified (a real Jupiter USDC->SSR swap-transaction build succeeded against production). Posted a fix summary as a comment on MCR-01. See DEC-0124.

2026-08-20 (DEC-0121: fixed both live production 500s, DevNet/Mainnet data mixing, and restored the Jupiter Reserve Asset catalogue, deployed to production). Root cause of both reported 500s: a missing `api/mainnet/package.json` let Node load `api/mainnet/rpc-proxy.ts`'s CommonJS build output as ESM, crashing every Mainnet RPC call at import time -- confirmed via live `vercel logs`, fixed with that one file. Added `api/mainnet/landing-stats.ts`/`api/mainnet/reserve-metadata.ts` (cluster-aware routing, closing the DevNet-URL-on-Mainnet-production bug Creator flagged) and `api/ledger/asset-catalogue.ts`/`api/ledger/known-asset-mints.ts` (restoring the Jupiter Tokens API V2 Reserve Asset picker, now that `JUPITER_API_KEY` is configured, with discovery's candidate-mint list kept bounded to on-chain-used mints rather than the whole catalogue). Also fixed a pre-existing bug in `lib/ledger/jupiterCatalogue.ts` that dropped `decimals` from every captured token, discovered live when the first real snapshot fetched 2,577 mints but the picker's safety filter excluded all of them. 620/620 offline tests passing, full validation clean, live-verified post-deploy (real RPC calls, real landing-stats data, 500 real catalogue tokens with decimals). See DEC-0121.

Verified on-chain via public Mainnet RPC (no private keys requested or inspected): the Squads Multisig Account is a genuine Squads V4 account (owner = the real Squads program); its raw data decodes to threshold=2 and exactly the 3 given signer addresses as members; the Treasury vault address is mathematically confirmed (independently derived, not assumed) to be the Squads vault-index-0 PDA of that exact multisig, and is genuinely off-curve. Verified via code review that the Squads Treasury vault (an off-curve PDA) is fully compatible as `default_protocol_fee_destination` -- the fee-collection instructions never require the destination to sign, use standard ATA derivation, and mint (never transfer-from) to it; the known duplicate-mutable-account edge case is already handled. Updated `docs/project/PROJECT_STATUS.md` (new "Authority & Treasury Structure (Mainnet target)" section), `docs/protocol/ACCOUNT_MODEL.md`, `docs/protocol/SSR_ARCHITECTURE.md` (including the pause-capability correction), and `docs/protocol/DEVNET_RUNBOOK.md` -- all append-only annotations, nothing deleted or rewritten. `public/road-to-mainnet.html` (frontend/database-backed checklist) was deliberately not touched, same precedent as DEC-0105. No code modified, no authority transferred, no protocol configuration changed, no funds sent, no deployment/upgrade performed, nothing committed or pushed. See DEC-0112.
